# THE BOARD — the work queue

**This is where agents pick up work and where they report it.** One item, one
row, one owner at a time. `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` stays the ledger of what the
owner asked for — an owner ask lands there first and gets mirrored here as one
or more workable items. Bugs found by tools, assets to generate, and infra work
land here directly.

## The rules

1. **Claim before working.** Set STATUS to `IN PROGRESS` with your agent name
   and the date. Two agents on one item is wasted work.

   **AND IF YOU ARE IN A WORKTREE, YOUR NEXT-FREE ID IS A GUESS.** On
   2026-08-03 six agents ran in parallel off worktrees branched at different
   commits, and *four of them* filed rows as SG-155/156, SG-159/160/161 and
   SG-159/160/161/162 — every one of those IDs was taken by a sibling before
   the merge, and renumbering them afterwards meant rewriting IDs across code
   comments, check names, design docs and this board. The next ID is not a
   fact you can read off a stale branch. Either take a block from the person
   dispatching you, or expect to be renumbered and keep your ID in as few
   places as you can manage.
2. **DONE needs evidence, and so does every coverage claim in the docs.** A
   named harness check string, a tool output, or a commit hash — never "looks
   right". This adopts the audit rule, and it holds REPO-WIDE, DESIGN DOCS
   INCLUDED: any sentence in any `.md` (STATUS, README, the design docs, this
   board) that says the harness covers/pins/guards/asserts a behaviour must name
   the check string it means, or be marked `UNVERIFIED —` until it can. A claim
   of coverage that names no check is evidence of nothing (SG-11).
3. **Found a bug? File it before fixing it.** One row, source named, even if
   you fix it in the same session. The ledger exists because things slipped
   when nobody wrote them down.

   **3a. "THE BROWSER DID IT THIS WAY" IS NOT A REASON FOR ANYTHING.** Not for a
   mechanic, not for a visual, not for a constant. The owner retired parity as a
   goal on 2026-08-01 and hardened it to mechanics on 2026-08-03, after SG-148
   took crit away from six damage sources on fidelity grounds alone and had to be
   reverted: *"We've moved so far beyond the browser version of this game. I do
   not want you going back and trying to refer to the browser game. We've
   transcended that. We should only be focused on making the Godot version of
   this as good as possible."*

   Recording where a number CAME from is good practice and stays. Using that
   provenance as the argument for keeping it is the thing that is banned. If a
   change's whole justification is browser fidelity, stop and ask the owner —
   and if a number is right, say why it is right FOR THIS GAME. The frozen
   browser build in `reference/` is an archive and a regression yardstick
   (`tools/parity.py` — "did we lose something that was good"), never an
   authority, and the answer may legitimately be "yes, and we keep ours anyway".
4. **BLOCKED names the blocker** — a missing key, a decision only the owner can
   make, an upstream item by ID. Anything blocked on the owner is also
   mirrored, with a recommendation, in `/NEEDS_ALEX.md` at the repo root.
5. **Nothing is deleted.** DONE and DROPPED rows move to the bottom table with
   their evidence or reason. `git log` on this file is the audit trail.
6. **Priorities:** P1 = blocks shipping or contradicts a written claim ·
   P2 = owner-visible improvement · P3 = worth doing, not worth doing first.
7. **Before starting anything**: read `STATUS.md`, run
   `SkyGear Tools.bat harness` (green before AND after your change), and check
   this board's IN PROGRESS rows so you don't collide.

**Where write-ups go.** `/NEEDS_ALEX.md` is a **page**, not a dossier — it holds
only what needs the owner's judgement, and it has had to be cut back from 600+
lines twice in one day. **Append your evidence to `docs/NIGHT-LOG-<date>.md`,
not to NEEDS_ALEX.** If something genuinely needs his decision, add a short
entry to its decision list and put the numbers in the log. A section he will not
read because the file is six hundred lines long is the same as not writing it.

| SG-183 | P3 | BUG | **The pause and settings screens save `settings.cfg` to disk on every redraw** | OPEN — found by SG-181 | `scripts/hud.gd:3932` (pause) and `scripts/hud.gd:5283` (settings) are immediate-mode: they call `game.audio.set_volume(key, ui.slider(...))` unconditionally from the draw, and `set_volume()` ends in `save_settings()`. So the value is written back whether or not the slider moved, and a build left sitting on either screen rewrites `user://settings.cfg` every frame — which is what made SG-181's collision constant instead of rare, and it is a config-file write per frame in a shipped build for the whole time either screen is up. The fix is one comparison: only call `set_volume` when the slider's returned value differs from the one that was passed in (and the same for `toggle_mute`'s neighbours), which keeps the immediate-mode shape and makes the write an edge rather than a level. Cheap; kept out of SG-181 because it is a behavioural change to shipped screens and wants its own row. |

| SG-171 | P3 | BUG | **`grip_at` is authored for all six weapons and NOTHING IN THE GAME READS IT** | OPEN — found by SG-170, filed per rule 3, deliberately not fixed | Every entry in the `weapons` block of `assets/models/weapons.json` carries `grip_at` — where along the shaft the hand belongs, 0 at the butt — taken from Alex's own modelling spec (`handoff-3d/weapons/PROMPTS.md:46`, *"a third up from the butt"*). `git grep grip_at` outside `reference/` returns that one prose line and nothing else: **no reader anywhere in the port**, which is STATUS's first failure mode sitting in the same file as the assets SG-170 is about. The fix, if it is wanted, is a `grip_at` term inside `hold()` plus a one-time re-solve of every existing `offset` in the same commit so the diff shows the numbers changing together. |

| SG-153 | P3 | BUG | **A clean harness run raises 56 engine `ERROR:`s, all of them `Drawing is only allowed inside this node's `_draw()``** | OPEN - found while fixing SG-149, filed per rule 3, deliberately NOT fixed | Found the moment SG-149 gave the run a way to count its own errors. Every one is the same message and they come from the harness driving the WIDGET LAYER outside a draw pass: `scripts/ui.gd:247/251/254` (`button`) and `scripts/ink.gd:187/189` (`write`) paint when they are called, and checks call them for their RETURN values — focus, hit-testing, fitted size — with no `NOTIFICATION_DRAW` in flight. Backtraces name `tests/parity_test.gd:169` (26) and `tests/parity_test.gd:5061-5063` (`_view`, 12) among others. **The assertions themselves are sound** — they read what the widget returns, not what it painted — so this is noise rather than a wrong answer, which is why it is P3 and why SG-149 pinned it at 56 rather than gating it to zero and shipping a red harness. **But it is 56 raised errors in a log we now want to be able to read**, and each one abandons the rest of the painting function it was raised in, so any future check that DOES assert on what a widget drew would be asserting on a half-painted widget. The fix is likely a harness-side draw context (an offscreen `SubViewport`, or a `_draw`-scoped fixture node) rather than touching `ui.gd`. **When it is fixed, lower `ENGINE_ERROR_BUDGET` in `tests/parity_test.gd` and the check tightens itself.** |
| SG-154 | P3 | BUG | **A crit explosion re-hits the boarder that produced it — the browser's `skip:e`, dropped on the one call site where it still matters, and A HARNESS CHECK PINS THE DIVERGENCE** | OPEN — **but its RATIONALE was struck by SG-159 (2026-08-03) and it may not be fixed on the grounds written below.** The owner: *"I do not want you going back and trying to refer to the browser game."* Every argument in this row is browser fidelity. The self-hit may still be worth changing — a crit explosion hitting the boarder who produced it for an extra flat 20 is a design question — but it has to be argued and measured on how the Godot game plays, or dropped. Do NOT flip `crit · and the hit that opened it is still a crit` from 21.0 to 41.0 because a browser line says so | SG-148's row noted a dropped `skip:e` and asked what it did. **What it does is one thing and nothing else.** `reference/storm-dusk-v11.html:1832`, inside `damageArea`, reads `if (e === o.skip\|\|e.dead\|\|e.state === 'climb') continue;` — pure identity exclusion of a single boarder from the circle's target loop. Nothing else in that function reads `o.skip`. The browser passes it on BOTH explosions: the crit explosion (`v11:1734`, `{ noCrit:true, silent:true, skip:e, knock:60 }`) and the kill explosion (`v11:1863`, `{ skip:e, noCrit:true, knock:70 }`). `scripts/game.gd`'s `_damage_circle` has **no exclusion parameter at all** — its only filter is `not enemy.dead`. **IT IS A SECOND DIVERGENCE, BUT AT ONE SITE, NOT TWO, AND THAT ASYMMETRY IS THE FINDING.** On the KILL explosion it is reproduced BY ACCIDENT: `scripts/enemy.gd` sets `dead = true` *before* it calls `game.on_enemy_killed(self)` at all three of its call sites (lines 578-579, 589-590, 633-634), so the `not enemy.dead` filter already excludes exactly the boarder the browser names. Correct, and correct for a reason no one wrote down — if that ordering is ever changed, the kill explosion silently starts hitting its own corpse's slot. **On the CRIT explosion it is NOT reproduced**: a crit that does not KILL leaves the struck boarder alive, and the circle opened at his own feet hits him again for a flat 20 and 60 knock. The browser gives him 40; this port gives him 60. **AND THE HARNESS ALREADY ASSERTS THE PORT'S NUMBER.** `crit · and the hit that opened it is still a crit` (SG-147) computes `61 - 20 (the crit-doubled direct hit) - 20 (its own explosion) = 21` and its own comment names the self-hit out loud. The check's STATED purpose — that the opening blow still crits — is sound and worth keeping; its ARITHMETIC encodes the missing `skip`. Under the authored rule that boarder ends on **41.0, not 21.0**. This is the seventh failure mode in its mildest form: not a check written from a misunderstanding, but a check whose expected value quietly ratifies a divergence nobody had identified yet. **NOT FIXED HERE ON PURPOSE.** SG-148 is a measured balance change with a before-and-after; adding a `skip` parameter to `_damage_circle` is a second damage change, it would move the SG-148 arms underneath the measurement, and a fourth change riding on a tuning commit is what SG-122 refused. **THE FIX** is a `skip: SkyGearEnemy = null` on `_damage_circle` passed only from the crit-explosion edge, plus flipping that one expected value from 21.0 to 41.0 in the same commit so the diff shows the assertion changing sides — the shape SG-119 used when it inverted `telegraph · a boarder that draws no wedge keeps its circle`. Worth pairing with a check that the KILL explosion still skips its own corpse, since today that is an accident of statement order rather than a rule. |
| SG-151 | P3 | BUG | **The triangle budget is derived from an asset's HEIGHT, which understates anything wide and open-topped under a camera that looks DOWN** | OPEN — found by SG-150, filed per rule 3, NOT fixed | `tools/meshy.py`'s `tri_budget` is `0.021 × px²` where `px` is the asset's on-screen HEIGHT, floored at 3,000. That is right for the figures and props it was calibrated on, which are seen side-on and whose height is a fair proxy for their silhouette area. It is wrong for `stern_counter`: 190 × 151 × 178 ground units, an open box whose entire interior deck faces a camera pitched **41° down**. The law asks 3,000 for it; at 3,000 its planking goes blotchy and its hatch ring turns to mush at the real camera, while the bow — same width, same law, same 3,000 — is fine, because the bow is a closed hull seen mostly in profile. **The floor's own docstring already argues the right principle** ("what breaks at that point is not fidelity but FEATURES") and then measures it against height anyway. A fix would derive the budget from PROJECTED AREA at the shipped camera rather than from height, or simply let a piece carry a `tris` override the way the gunner drone does. Not fixed here because it moves the budget for every asset in the manifest and wants its own before-and-after. Evidence: `.shots/sg148/compare/stern_counter_t3000-y035.png` against `stern_counter_t8000-y035.png`. |
| SG-152 | P3 | INFRA | **Nothing in the harness guards the triangle ceiling, and the `still` readback check can be satisfied by a COMMENT** | OPEN — found by SG-150, filed per rule 3, NOT fixed (`tests/parity_test.gd` is held by another agent) | **(a) No triangle guard exists.** SG-145 left three pieces out of `static_model.gd` on the triangle law, and nothing in the harness would have caught them being wired anyway; SG-150 has now brought them under it, and nothing would catch a regression either. It fails on the old state by construction: at HEAD~ those same three keys measured 10,312 / 30,666 / 30,410. **(b) The readback check is a substring test over the whole source**, so a tool that merely MENTIONS `RenderingServer.frame_post_draw` in a comment passes `still · and every frozen readback waits for the renderer, not for the tree` without ever calling it. Found accidentally while demonstrating that the check fails: stripping the `await` but leaving the header comment left it GREEN, and only stripping the comment as well turned it red. The `STILL: NOT APPLICABLE` marker has the same shape, but that one is a deliberate, documented declaration and this is not. Stripping comments first — which the sibling check at `parity_test.gd` ~line 338 already does, for its own reason — would close it. |
| SG-125 | P2 | MEASURE | **SG-48's stowage cut is still the right call, but not for the reason written down** — its evidence was gathered where the effect could not appear | OPEN — verify-and-annotate, NOT a request to resurrect the feature | SG-48 CUT the stowage variety spine on its pre-committed kill-test: close-share **live 5.38% (sd 2.20) vs flat 5.25% (sd 1.72)**, mean diff 0.13 ± 0.36 (t≈0.36) over 120 runs, read as "combat statistically identical". **SG-118 then established that `tools/balance.gd` never moved its bot**, and that a moving captain spends **~22%** of a run at close range against the stationary bot's **3–5%**. So the statistic that cut the feature was measured in a regime where the thing the feature acts on barely occurred: a 0.13-point difference inside a 5% band, produced by an instrument that spent ~95% of every run outside the range where the effect lives. **That is not evidence of no effect; it is an instrument that could not have seen one.** **Do NOT resurrect the feature.** It is independently superseded by the owner's own reframing — variety belongs to ENEMIES within a run, and ship modification happens BETWEEN runs — which is a perfectly good reason to have cut it, just a different one from the one recorded. This row exists so the repository stops claiming an evidential basis it does not have. **Action:** annotate SG-48's row to say the cut now rests on the design reframing, and either re-run its statistic on the repaired rig or state plainly that it was never re-run. Either is honest; the current text is not. This is the fifth failure mode reaching backwards — every verdict SG-48's family produced is in the same position, and this row is the audit of which ones. | **AUDIT DONE 2026-08-03 (opus, read-only).** Seven rows rest on this rig. **SG-48** — evidence void, cut stands on the owner's reframing (this row). **SG-57** — held-rate flipped sign on the repaired rig and lost significance; it was a bot fact; the owner's threshold question is retired. **SG-70 — THE ONE THAT WAS COSTING THE OWNER A PLAYTEST.** Its row is OPEN pending *"the owner's next playtest"* and explains its 25%-vs-58% ally-share gap by saying the bot "spends 55–60% of the fight at long range" — which is the stationary-bot artefact exactly. Close-range time is 3–5% stationary against ~22% moving. **Re-measure on the repaired rig BEFORE spending a playtest**; if it now reads ~58% the mystery closes and SG-62's fix can finally be judged. **SG-97** — already discounted its own numbers as noise for the right reason; conclusion stands. **SG-62** — deferred to SG-70. **SG-14, SG-26** — **EXAMINED 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-70), and the audit is now CLOSED at nine rows.** Neither is a mere capability-add: both RECORDED measurements from the stationary rig, so both belong on the affected list. But in both the balance figures sit in a single separable paragraph, and **everything else in each row is carried by checks that never step a captain and therefore still stands** — SG-14's six `heat ·` checks (data tables, `begin_run`/`open_draft`; cumulative stack, readers, the three rung effects, the summit sigil, the ladder UI, and monotonicity guaranteed by the `HEAT_HARDER` direction table rather than by measurement) and SG-26's eleven `shop ·` checks (32-cell matrix, reroll refused, capacity 5, the fifth shape dealt in the first card's place, the exclusion pair, the sigil economy). **So: not fine, but not broken either — each needs one paragraph struck, not a rebuild, and that is the shorter answer the audit was hoping for.** Both paragraphs are now annotated in place and the re-measure is SG-127. **AND THE AUDIT FOUND ONE THING THAT NEEDED NO RE-RUN AT ALL:** SG-14 records Heat 0 as **5/6 held** and SG-26 records Heat 0 as **3/6 held** — same tool, same Heat, same seed list, two answers. The rig's own header now declares an **8% noise floor**; n=6 could never have supported either number, and the disagreement was sitting in the ledger the whole time. **The finding that outlived the audit:** SG-97's row had already WRITTEN DOWN that the bot never moves, a day before SG-118 rediscovered it. The fact reached no other row and not the tool's own comment. That is now the sixth entry in STATUS's failure-mode list, and a seventh went in beside it from SG-119 — a harness check that asserts the bug.
| SG-121 | P3 | BUG | **Three fire-field creators write a `radius` and a `dps` that nothing reads** — the sixth instance of the project's first failure mode | OPEN — found by opus/SG-117's damage-source sweep, filed per rule 3, NOT fixed | `_update_fire_fields` (`game.gd:3923+`) hardcodes `78.0` and `7.5` for enemies and `78.0` / `3.0` for the player. All three callers of `_field` write per-field numbers it never consults: RESIDUE (`game.gd:2926`, `radius 62+22*residue`, `dps 13*residue`), the Boilerwright's **bleed-jet scald trail** (`game.gd:3367`, `trail_radius 46.0`, `trail_dps 9.0` from `game_data.gd:219`), and the broken lantern (`game.gd:3895`, which writes neither). So a scald trail is the same 78-unit pool at the same rate as a lantern fire, the RESIDUE card's whole scaling term does nothing, and the Boilerwright walking his own retreat trail eats the identical tick. STATUS names *"data with no reader"* as the failure mode that has happened FIVE times and says to extend the two existing guards rather than trust yourself; this is the sixth. Deliberately NOT fixed here: making those fields live changes fire damage a second time in the same session, and SG-117 already moves it. — **2026-08-03 (opus/SG-136): THIS ROW IS WRONG ABOUT `radius`, AND THE TRUTH IS A WORSE BUG THAN THE ONE FILED. STILL OPEN, AND STILL NOT FIXED, BECAUSE BOTH OF THE TWO OPTIONS IT OFFERS CHANGE THE GAME.** `radius` is not data with no reader. **`view3d.gd:4470` reads it** — `var fr: float = float(f.get("radius", 62.0)) * 2.2` sizes the fire decal, the scorch decal under it and the permanent `MarkKind.SCORCH` burn. So the fields are read by the RENDERER and ignored by the DAMAGE, which makes this the project's SECOND failure mode (two functions disagreeing about one number), not its first — and the disagreement is **the fire you can see is not the fire that burns you**, in all three creators and in both directions: the Boilerwright's scald trail is **drawn at 46 and burns at 78**, a hitbox 70% wider than its own telegraph; the broken lantern is **drawn at 62** (the renderer's fallback, since it writes no radius) **and burns at 78**; RESIDUE at one stack is **drawn at 84 and burns at 78**, so its card's whole scaling term moves the picture and nothing else. Standing where no fire is drawn costs the captain 12 dps in two of the three. **`dps` alone is genuinely unread** (`_update_fire_fields` hardcodes 7.5 to enemies and `damage_player(3.0)` to the captain). **WHY NEITHER OPTION WAS TAKEN:** wiring `radius` moves three hitboxes (two shrink, one grows); wiring `dps` moves fire's damage rate a third time in one session, on top of SG-117 and SG-122; and DELETING `radius` is not behaviour-preserving either, because the renderer would fall back to 62 and every drawn fire on the deck would change size. There is no version of "wire it or delete it" here that a agent may take silently — it is in `/NEEDS_ALEX.md` with the three numbers. No harness check was added: the only check that could be written today is `hazard · the fire you can see and the fire that burns you are the same circle`, and it FAILS at HEAD, so shipping it would ship a red harness. |
| SG-127 | P2 | MEASURE | **SG-14's difficulty claim and SG-26's two Article verdicts are unevidenced — both balance paragraphs were measured on the stationary bot** | OPEN — filed 2026-08-03 by opus/SG-70 closing SG-125's audit, annotated in place, NOT re-run | The last two rows of SG-125's audit. Neither row is broken — every structural claim in both is carried by checks that never step a captain (SG-14's six `heat ·`, SG-26's eleven `shop ·`) and those stand. **What is void is one paragraph in each.** SG-14: "Heat 0 held 5/6 avg wave 11.7 allies 34% · Heat 3 held 0/6 · Heat 5 dead on wave 4 every run, allies 4%", and the conclusion **"graded rather than a cliff"**. SG-26: "baseline 3/6 · opening_bid 6/6 wave 12.0 · second_hand 1/6 wave 10.8, passives 4.5% vs 3.2%", and the conclusions **"the named-kit benefit is real"** and **"the trade is real"**. **THE TWO THINGS WHOEVER TAKES THIS MUST NOT REPEAT.** (1) **n=6 is not a measurement on this rig.** These two rows disagree with each other about the same Heat 0 baseline — 5/6 against 3/6 — which is the cleanest available proof that the sample was too small, and the header now declares an 8% noise floor on damage-taken. Use `-- 6 <heat> <article> <reps>` and pool; SG-70 used n=30 per arm and SG-118's reference batches were n=120. (2) **Watch the CEILING.** Heat 0 now holds ~92%, so a hold-count comparison at Heat 0 is nearly saturated and will read "no difference" for reasons that have nothing to do with the thing under test — the same shape of error SG-125 was created to catch. Read wave-reached and damage-taken, or run the Article arms at a Heat where the hold rate is not pinned. **Do not resurrect or retune anything from the result** — SG-14's rungs and SG-26's Articles are both shipping and both mechanically evidenced; this is about what the ledger is entitled to CLAIM.. **✅ DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-128). All three claims re-measured on the repaired rig; ONE REFUTED, ONE NOT ESTABLISHED, ONE RELOCATED — and none was rescued.** **(1) "Graded rather than a cliff" (SG-14) — REFUTED.** Heat 0 n=240 held 86.7%, Heat 3 n=120 66.7%, Heat 4 n=120 77.0%, Heat 5 n=120 **0 of 120**, dead on wave 4.02 with sd 0.18. Heat 4 above Heat 3 is **not** claimed as a re-ordering (Wilson 65.1-85.8 vs 57.8-74.5, overlapping). Owner decision in NEEDS_ALEX §SG-127. **(2) "The named-kit benefit is real" — NOT ESTABLISHED**, claim as recorded refuted, and the arm is confounded by a bot-policy difference (SG-130). **(3) "The trade is real" — SURVIVES ON NEW EVIDENCE**, +20.4% damage taken at p=0.00003, while the hold-rate cost it was actually recorded on is null. **BOTH OF SG-127's WARNINGS WERE HEEDED**: n=120 not n=6, and Heat 3 rather than the saturated Heat 0. **Pre-registered before any arm ran**, all three outcomes fixed in advance including the wording of a null, and the analysis reports Bonferroni over all six tests rather than the best of them. | SG-124 | P2 | BUG | **`marks_shot.gd`'s "worst honest case" never puts a mark in the ring it measures** — it reports 0.0% overlap and its own header says that makes the figure a coincidence | OPEN — found by SG-116 while re-measuring on the corrected mask, filed per rule 3, NOT fixed | The deck-marks cost is `+0.02%` against a 3% gate, and it is `+0.02%` in BOTH passes — the wave-12 distribution AND the "worst honest case" that exists specifically to force an overlap by stamping the deepest mark this system can make directly under each rune. Both passes print **`of that ring, the marks darken 0.0%`**, and the tool's own next line says *"if this is ~0 the figure above is a coincidence"*. It is. **The marks are rendering and the stamp does work** — `marked-worst-tele.png` and `marked-telegraphs-tele.png` are different files and both differ from `clean-worst.png`, so this is not SG-101's "the mark is under the boarder's mesh" scar returning. The marks land somewhere; they do not land in the **12 px `RING_PX` band around the rune**, which is the only planking this measurement looks at. The likely cause is now visible for the first time: the worst case stamps at `t.at.lerp(captain, 0.3/0.5/0.7)`, but the melee rune is a wedge CENTRED on the boarder with radius `reach`, so those three points can sit well beyond the wedge and its 12 px ring. Until a mark is demonstrably inside the ring, **the deck-marks figure is evidence that marks and runes do not overlap in this pose, and not evidence that marks are cheap** — which matters because `MARK_CAP` 48→24 and `MARK_ALPHA_MAX` 0.30→0.12 were already retuned once on a number from this tool (SG-115). Fix: stamp inside the measured ring — `SkyGearRune.save_mask()` now writes the rune pixel set as a PNG, so where the ring actually is can be seen rather than reasoned about.. **⚠ MEASURED 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-128) — CONFIRMED, AND THE CAUSE IS WORSE THAN FILED: THE WORST CASE IS NON-DETERMINISTIC.** Five runs on unchanged code: new marks placed 0/13/0/0 of 60, ring darkened 0.2% / **14.0%** / 0.0% / 0.0% / 0.0%, cost +0.01% / **+1.33%** / +0.00% / +0.09% / +0.07%. **Four runs in five, not one of the sixty stamps reaches the planking**, so the 0.0% this row filed is the COMMON case rather than a one-off, and the tool's coincidence warning is firing correctly. **The cause is not the lerp geometry this row guessed at.** `_mark` has three outcomes and only one places a disc: inside `MARK_MIN_SEP` of a same-kind mark it DEEPENS that one at ITS position, and with the cap full — which it always is, because `_mark_the_deck` fills all 24 slots first — it retires a victim and queues the newcomer in `_mark_pending`. Whether that retirement completes depends on mark AGES, which depend on how the awaited real frames fell. Same code, same seed, sometimes 13 marks and sometimes zero: SG-108's family again, a scene less still than the tool believed. **FIXED IN THE INSTRUMENT, NOT IN THE GAME:** the tool now snapshots the slot state and prints how many stamps took a NEW slot, how many merely deepened, how many are pending, and how many of the twelve requested positions actually carry a mark — and says in words that a zero-fresh pass is the first pass photographed twice. **THE EVIDENTIAL QUESTION SG-128 WAS ASKED (does the `MARK_CAP` 48→24 / alpha 0.30→0.12 retune rest on anything?): THE CONTRAST LEG RESTS ON NOTHING, IN EITHER DIRECTION.** The 11.5%/9.1% pair is not reproducible from this repository (SG-115) and `view3d.gd`'s own comment already says so in writing; the current tool is inert four times in five. The one run where it worked reads **+1.33% against a 3% gate** — under it — and that is n=1 and is NOT claimed. **The COVERAGE leg IS reproducible**: 5.7% painted at cap 24, verified. **`MARK_CAP` AND `MARK_ALPHA_MAX` ARE DELIBERATELY UNCHANGED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS** — the owner has the marks on his play list to judge by eye, and the honest outcome here is that this number never meant anything, which makes his eye the deciding instrument rather than a competitor to one. |

| SG-132 | P2 | MEASURE | **Held-rate is running out of room at Heat 3 — the rung is turning into the saturated ceiling SG-127 warned about at Heat 0** | OPEN — filed 2026-08-03 by opus/SG-130 per rule 3, NOT fixed | Found while re-running SG-127's Article arm on the SG-130-repaired bot. **The repaired bot no longer spends draft slots on passives, so the Heat 3 BASELINE rose from SG-128's 75.0% held to 80.8% (97/120).** The two agree inside the +-9-point resolution, so this is NOT a contradiction — it is the same rung measured with a slightly better captain. **The consequence is what matters: 80.8% leaves 19.2 points of headroom, and n=120 needs +14.5 points under the Bonferroni(6) bar to establish anything.** So the entire establishable range at this rung is roughly **+14.5 to +19.2 points** — a positive effect must be nearly the whole of the remaining room before this rig can see it, which is exactly the shape of error SG-125 was created to catch and SG-127 warned about one rung lower. **ANYONE MEASURING A POSITIVE EFFECT AT HEAT 3 FROM HERE NEEDS EITHER A HARDER RUNG OR A METRIC WITH ROOM.** Wave-reached and damage-taken both still have range (this session read them at +2.7% and +11.0%; damage-taken cv 0.43, 15.6% resolvable at n=120). Heats 1 and 2 have still never been run — SG-128 records that gap and it is unchanged. **Not a bug and nothing in the game to fix**; it is a fact about what the instrument can still see, filed so the next three agents do not each rediscover it one at a time. | SG-136 | P2 | BUG | **`--fixed-fps` makes `lit_probe.gd` repeatable and NOBODY IN THIS REPO IS USING IT — but adopting it re-poses the deck, so the tool's published numbers are a rebaseline decision** | OPEN — filed by opus/SG-136 while working SG-133, per rule 3, NOT fixed | SG-133 asks for a fixed-delta settle and neither hand-rolled version of one works (see that row). Godot already ships the lever: **`--fixed-fps 60` forces a fixed delta on the WHOLE engine** — every `_process`, every `_physics_process`, every self-driving node — with no code change at all. Three runs of **unmodified HEAD** with the flag, against three without, off the same clean extracted worktree and the same import: whole-frame boarder pixels **78077 / 78108 / 78084 (0.04%)** against **99448 / 100066 / 99923 (0.62%)**; molten fraction **0.81% / 0.81% / 0.81% — bit-identical** against 0.24 / 0.23 / 0.25; figure/deck **1.016 / 1.023 / 1.009 (1.4%)** against 1.023 / 1.062 / 1.057 (3.8%). So the flag removes the whole of SG-131's residual and three quarters of the ratio's. **AND IT IS NOT A DROP-IN, WHICH IS THE HALF THAT NEEDS A DECISION.** The flag governs the tool's SETUP too, so the sim reaches the settle having advanced a different amount and the wave spawns more: **8 rigs and 10 AnimationPlayers with the flag, 3 and 4 without.** A different number of boarders in different facings is a different photograph, and it is what moves the molten fraction 0.24% -> 0.81% — so `MOLTEN_FLOOR = 0.18`, `PAINTING_MOLTEN_FRONT = 1.25` and SG-131's published 0.13-0.15 / 0.22-0.24 bands were all read off a composition that the clock chose. **AND THAT IS THE FINDING UNDER THE FINDING: `lit_probe.gd`'s scene is not pinned by construction. The number of boarders it photographs is a function of how fast the machine ran.** It happened to be 3 on three real-time runs here and on the three the SG-133 row recorded, so it has never been caught. Re-baselining those constants is changing tuning values to make a result look better, which this session's standing rule forbids doing silently — so it is in `/NEEDS_ALEX.md`. **RECOMMENDATION:** adopt `--fixed-fps 60`, then pin the roster with a check that reads the count rather than trusting it (`lit · and the deck holds the boarders this tool posed, not however many the clock spawned`), then re-measure the molten bands against the pinned scene and republish them as one edit with the before/after in the row. Files: `tests/lit_probe.gd`, `tools/still.gd`. |

So the crew half of his sentence is FALSE and the captain half is true, and they have the same cause. **THE DISPATCHER'S READING OF THE CHAIN WAS RIGHT IN MECHANISM AND WRONG IN MAGNITUDE.** The order is exactly as read — captain inside 280, then the lane cannon while the boarder is ahead of it, then the nearest crewman inside 220, then the Boiler — and the cannon DOES suppress crew targeting: with the three lane guns destroyed every frame (`gunsdown` arm) crew-aimed swings go **36.6% → 89.1%** and kills **21.6 → 71.4** (95% 59.3..83.6; the two intervals do not touch). A rig that asks for a clock and is TOLD it has one is not a rig that has one. `crew_probe` therefore sets no tick rate at all, steps at the engine's own delta, and proves `ground / (|v| x DT)` = **1.011** and **0.988** above its two arms. **NO TUNING, ON PURPOSE:** the crew are fighting and dying in numbers, which is the question he asked, and the thing that actually does not read is on SG-194. |

| SG-188 | DONE 2026-08-04 (opus/SG-188) — `scripts/{rig3d,view3d,enemy}.gd`, `tools/anim_timing.gd`, new `tools/swing_beat.gd`; **7 new checks, harness 1085 → 1092**; worst rate **4.00x → 0.98x**, plays per attack **2.50 → 0.13**; **the simulation is untouched and the captain and the Boilerwright are byte-identical**; witnesses `.shots/clips/sg188-before.gif` against `.shots/clips/sg188-after.gif`, beats in `.shots/sg188/{before,after}/` | **The furnace knight's swing was fitted to a window that COUNTED DOWN, so it accelerated into the 4.00x clamp — twice per attack — and was dealt again in between: 2.50 plays per single hit** **WHICH OF THE THREE CAUSES IT WAS: the first, and in a worse form than the brief supposed.** Not "a short variant played at speed" and not "authored fast at 1.0" — the clip was rate-scaled, but by a divisor that shrank every frame. `_sync_rig` handed the swing `enemy.state_time` as its window and `want()` recomputed `anim.speed_scale` from it on EVERY frame, so as `state_time` counted 0.90 → 0 the rate rose with it. And because `swinging` holds the rig in `swing` for the windup AND the recovery, the whole ramp ran twice per attack. **MEASURED LIVE, six consecutive attacks on a real deck (`tools/swing_beat.gd`): rate 1.83x rising to the 4.00x clamp in every one of them, and 2.50 full plays of the swing clip per single 34-damage hit** — the third play arriving because `want`'s `elif not anim.is_playing()` arm, which exists to restart a CYCLE the blend dropped, was dealing the finished one-shot again. Two and a half swings for one hit is read as one swing at two and a half times the speed, which is exactly what the owner reported. **THE SECOND CAUSE WAS ALSO REAL, JUST SMALLER.** His pack rotates six swing variants of 1.27, 1.43, 1.80, 1.83, 1.87 and **3.53** s; at the old window those played at 1.41x to 3.93x, a beat that changed by 2.8x shot to shot. **THE FIX IS THREE RENDERER-ONLY CHANGES AND NOTHING ELSE.** (1) `SkyGearEnemy.attack_beat()` — the windup times the Heat scale plus the recovery, asked of the SIMULATION rather than restated in the renderer, because `_windup_scale()` shortens the wind at Heat 2 and a renderer restating that is STATUS failure mode two with a difficulty ladder attached; `_sync_rig` fits the swing to that instead of to the countdown. (2) `want()` latches a one-shot's rate on the frame it STARTS and holds it for the one-shot's life. (3) a finished one-shot holds its last pose — the follow-through — instead of being replayed; the cycle case still restarts, and the same check asserts both. **THIS IS THE DEFECT THE FILE ALREADY WARNED ABOUT, IN THE ONE PLACE IT WAS NOT APPLIED.** `view3d.gd` says beside the half-health turn *"a window that shrinks every frame is a one-shot that accelerates as it plays"* and passes `TURN_TIME`; it says beside the crossing *"`want` re-reads the window only on the frame the state changes, so this is the value the clip is fitted to for the entire leap"* and passes `ARRIVAL_TIME`. **That second sentence was false** — `want` re-read the window for the RATE on every frame — and the attack was the only caller handing it something that moved. `enemy.gd`'s `ARRIVAL_TIME` and `TURN_TIME` comments both name the trap explicitly. The repository knew, three times, in the file the bug was in. **AND `hurt` IS THE SAME SHAPE**: its window is `maxf(0.12, stun)`, another countdown, so the flinch accelerated too; the latch fixes it without a fourth change. **THE FOURTH CHANGE, AND THERE IS NO NEW CONSTANT IN IT.** `_variant_of` had one gate — a variant must fit at `ATTACK_RATE_MAX`, "can it physically be got through". It now prefers, among those, the ones that fit at **1.0x, the speed the animator authored**, falling back when none do. That drops `swing2` (3.53 s, a longer MOVE rather than a variation of the same one) from the knight's rotation and takes the spread from 0.75x..1.86x to **0.75x..0.98x**. It can only bite where there is a choice: a hero's longest attack window is `clampf(cooldown*0.85, 0.24, 0.62)` and nothing in a hero pack is 0.62 s, so `natural` comes back empty and her rotation is untouched — asserted as the PREMISE rather than the conclusion, so ingesting a half-second swing next month cannot silently narrow her to it. **NOTHING THE SIMULATION SWINGS ON MOVED, AND IT WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** `attack_beat()` reads the table and writes nothing; every other line is `speed_scale` or a clip name. `telegraph · and his throughput against things that cannot dodge is SG-97's, untouched` is green at **17.9 dps** before and after, and windup 0.90 / recover 1.00 / damage 34 / hp 360 are the values SG-97 and SG-165 left. **This is a pure animation-rate change, which is what he asked for — he asked how it LOOKS.** **BEFORE AND AFTER, `tools/anim_timing.gd --model <x>`, every rigged figure:** ARMORED **3.93x → 0.96x**, SCRAPPER 4.00x → 2.93x, SWARM 2.75x → 1.57x, BOSS 1.22x → 0.75x; **captain and Boilerwright diff EMPTY** — every row byte-identical. Every boarder was carrying this, the knight worst because his beat is the longest. **THE CONTROL CAUGHT A REGRESSION I INTRODUCED**, which is the argument for running it: the first version of the `natural` gate was indented so that it captured the `else:` belonging to the filter above it, which turned the "nothing fits, take the shortest" fallback into "the captain always gets her shortest clip" and quietly ended her rotation. The captain's `anim_timing` diff showed CONE and CHAIN changing clip; the code was wrong, not the tool. **NEGATIVE CONTROLS, ALL FOUR RUN:** reverting the renderer window → 2 red (`'swing2' at 3.93x`, and **"the swing covers 47% of its 1.90s attack"** — the frozen half, on the record); un-latching the rate → 1 red at 4.00x; restoring the one-shot replay → 1 red; dropping the authored-speed gate → 2 red (`6 variants, 0.75x..1.86x`, `excluded: swing2`). **THE CHECKS (7), AND WHICH KIND EACH IS.** Carrying the argument, driven through the REAL `_sync_rig` on a real deck because a check that hands the rig its own window would go on passing if the renderer regressed — `figure · the renderer fits a boarder's swing to the WHOLE attack and never re-fits it mid-swing — SG-188` and `figure · and the clip it picked lasts the attack, so nothing is frozen waiting for the recovery to end`. Rig-level, and the only ones that catch the latch and the replay — `figure · and the rate a swing opens at is the rate it ends at — a window that counts down is a swing that speeds up`, `figure · a finished swing holds its follow-through instead of being dealt again, and a dropped walk cycle still restarts`, `figure · and every swing he can rotate up fits the beat at the speed it was authored at, so the tempo is the same twice running`. **And two that are REGRESSION GUARDS rather than evidence, said plainly on the SG-128 precedent**: `figure · a boarder's swing is fitted to the WHOLE attack, not to the windup it happens to start in` can only fail if `attack_beat()` itself breaks, and `figure · and a hero's swing rotation is untouched by the boarder's tempo gate — nothing in her pack fits a 0.62s cast at authored speed` pins a premise, not a behaviour. **THE TOOL WAS FIXED, NOT JUST THE GAME, because the tool is why this took a day to find.** `tools/anim_timing.gd` asked `want("swing", …, windup)` and printed 3.93x — the value on the FIRST frame of a window that then moved — so the shipping game's 4.00x and its 2.50 plays were invisible to the instrument built for exactly this question. It reads `attack_beat()` now and prints the whole ROTATION's spread beside the one sample, and its header carries why. New `tools/swing_beat.gd` is the thing that could actually see it: a real boarder attacking a real captain on the engine's own clock, counting `animation_finished` rather than inferring it. **It caught its own author first**: GDScript lambdas capture locals BY VALUE, so its `plays` counter incremented a copy and the first run reported "0 clip plays" against clips demonstrably finishing twice per attack — the fifth failure mode in one line, written up in the file. **THE STILLS ARE A CONTROL, NOT THE EVIDENCE, and that is the honest note.** `tools/telegraph_beat.gd`'s five ARMORED frames are **pixel-identical before and after** — `SkyGearStill.freeze` pins the AnimationPlayer, which is exactly what SG-108 built it to do, so a still cannot witness a speed. What they DO prove is worth having: the pose at every beat of the telegraph, and therefore its read, is unchanged. The tempo evidence is the gif (all 140 frames differ; at frame 58 the centre knight is deep into a downward chop with the axe at the deck BEFORE, and still winding with the axe legible overhead AFTER) and the numbers above. **SPAWNED SG-189**: the CREW rig has the same countdown window (`_sync_rig`'s fallback keeps them on exactly the behaviour they had, deliberately — an allies-side animation change does not belong in a row about the knight), and the latch already fixes the acceleration for them; what is left is whether their attack should be fitted to their own beat. |

The row was filed as "he cannot damage a turret AT ALL, structurally, because the stomp resolve calls only `damage_player`". **The first half of that is false and measuring it is what found the true shape.** The swing's victim chain (`damage_turret`/`hurt_crew`/`damage_boiler`) is still reachable — a lane-walking boss is never `targets_player`, so he falls into the `else` branch and takes the cannon as his target exactly as designed. **What is wrong is the CADENCE.** The stomp check sits at the head of `state == "move"` and PREEMPTS the swing whenever both are ready, so the swing only fires in the 1.4 s window the stomp clock leaves it. **MEASURED, `tools/lane_probe.gd`, a Colossus landed at the bow of lane 1 with the captain parked out of every radius he has, 60 s, deterministic:** Heat 2 — **8 swings, 208 of a 760 cannon** with the stomp on, against **25 swings and 650** with it off. At that rate the cannon needs **219 s**. The owner reported the outcome exactly. **THE FIX IS THE CIRCLE, NOT THE SWING'S BEAT BACK.** `_resolve_stomp()` asks `stomp_hits` — the same circle the renderer draws, no second shape, SG-119's rule — of the captain, every live cannon, every live crewman and the Boiler. **AND THE STRUCTURAL ZERO SG-166 BUILT THE BYPASS FOR IS UNTOUCHED**: the point was never "only the captain", it was that the chain is `if/elif` and can never test her; this goes through NONE of the chain, because an area has no priority order. **AFTER: 390 (Heat 0) and 442 (Heat 2)** off the same cannon in the same 60 s — **+114% and +112%**, which is 62–68% of the chase build's rate while keeping the telegraphed, escapable, readable beat that earned the lane walk. **THREE BALANCE CALLS, MADE AND ARGUED AT THE CONSTANT** (`scripts/enemy.gd`, above `COLOSSUS_STOMPS`): cannons take FULL damage and every one inside the rim rather than the nearest (the lanes are 560 apart and the rim is 240+34, so "all" and "nearest" are the same rule here and the simpler one is written); crew take FULL damage (68 HP against his 26 is three beats to put a sailor down, not a wipe, and halving it would be a special case earning nothing); the Boiler is IN. **SENTRIES ARE DELIBERATELY OUT** — nothing he has has ever damaged one, so adding them would be a second change wearing this one's clothes. **AND THE SG-166 ROW IS CORRECTED RATHER THAN LEFT STANDING — its Finding 4 was half right for a reason it could not have known, and the corrected answer is WORSE for the design, not better.** It measured Boiler HP lost in wave 12 falling 11.08 → 6.00 and attributed it to him spending 43% of his life planted. He also could not touch the Boiler at all. **Re-measured with the Boiler in the circle: 0.00, sd 0.00, in every one of 181 wave-12 runs across both arms, at Heat 0 and at Heat 2.** He does not reach it — 1,965 units of walk against 23–31 s of life, with a cannon in the way that now holds him. COLOSSUS-DESIGN §2's statistic fails for the FOURTH time, and for the first time the reason is not a missing damage path. *"He takes the ship away if you ignore him"* is still a design that has never once bitten, and it is now clear that the lever is his ARRIVAL and not his damage. **THE WAVE-12 RUN-LEVEL NUMBERS, `tools/boss_probe.gd` on the corrected SG-190 clock, Heat 0, four arms:** cannon HP lost in wave 12 **6.0 → 40.0** (arm A shipped → arm B fixed), damage by him **150.0 → 175.6**, ttk **23.1 → 27.0 s**. **AND THE HONEST n IS 6, NOT 120, WHICH IS SG-128's MISTAKE ARRIVING IN NEW CLOTHES** — SG-190 made the rig deterministic, so twenty reps of a seed are one run printed twenty times. Per SEED the cannon statistic goes from `{0,0,0,0,30}` to `{0,0,0,72,90}`: **one seed of five saw any cannon damage before and two of five see 2.4–3x as much after**, which is suggestive at n=5 and is NOT the evidence. It is evidence that `tools/bot.gd` never fights the hulk: the bot orbits boarders and is never inside the splash band, so the rig cannot see this change at all. **A bot that cannot reach the objective cannot measure the objective**, which is worth knowing before the next hulk question is asked of that tool. |
| SG-190 | DONE 2026-08-04 (opus/SG-185) — filed per rule 3 and fixed in the same session, because the SG-185 baseline could not be taken without it; `await physics_frame` in `tools/{balance,boss_probe,melee_probe,critx_probe}.gd`; **1 new `bot ·` check, harness 1094 → 1095** | **Every hand-stepping rig in `tools/` moved its boarders by the WALL CLOCK — `move_and_slide()` takes the idle frame's delta unless you are inside a physics frame, and not one of them ever was** **FOUND BY BUILDING A NEW RIG AND REFUSING TO BELIEVE IT.** `tools/lane_probe.gd` reported a Colossus covering **515 units in 18.4 s of walking at a table speed of 95**, then in a second arm of the SAME code and seed covered **12.67 units per hand-step against the first arm's 1.58** — 8.0x, differing only in that the second was resumed after `await process_frame`. **THE CAUSE IS ONE LINE OF ENGINE BEHAVIOUR NOBODY HERE KNEW.** `CharacterBody2D.move_and_slide()` does not integrate against the delta you hand `_physics_process`. It asks `Engine.is_in_physics_frame()` and takes `get_physics_process_delta_time()` if the answer is yes and **`get_process_delta_time()` — the idle frame's WALL-CLOCK duration — if it is no.** **MEASURED DIRECTLY, in a six-line probe:** in a `_run` reached by `call_deferred` from `_initialize`, `get_physics_process_delta_time()` returns **0.0000** and `is_in_physics_frame()` is **false**; after `await physics_frame` it returns **0.0500**; after `await process_frame` it is false again and the idle delta is **0.0069**. **So `Engine.physics_ticks_per_second = 20` — written in all four rigs, correct, and load-bearing — was read by NOTHING, because nothing was ever inside the frame that reads it.** Every boarder in every simulated verdict this ledger contains moved at **0.0069 s a step against the 0.05 the loops counted — about a seventh of its table speed — and DIFFERENTLY on a busier machine.** **THIS IS MOST OF THE "STILL NOT DETERMINISTIC" RESIDUAL `balance.gd`'s own header blames on the physics server, and the header was wrong.** The proof is the fix: with `await physics_frame` in place, **twenty reps of one seed return BIT-IDENTICAL rows** — `A0BAL3` came back `ttk 30.6s taken 208 boiler 0 dur 32.3s` twenty times out of twenty, across separate processes. Two arms of identical code run half an hour apart agreed on every statistic to the decimal. **THE RIG IS DETERMINISTIC NOW, AND THAT COSTS SOMETHING THAT MUST BE SAID LOUDLY: `reps` BUYS NOTHING.** `6 seeds x 20 reps` is not n=120, it is **n=6 printed 120 times**, and every interval computed off that 120 is about 4.5x too narrow — SG-128's exact sin in a new hat, and it would have been committed in this very session if the per-seed rows had not been read. `SEEDS` is **6 → 32** and `boss_probe` now prints a REFUSAL in words under any result whose reps came back identical, naming the honest n. **THE FIX IS TWO LINES PER TOOL**: enter a physics frame before stepping, and `await physics_frame` rather than `process_frame` inside the loop — the delta then equals the 0.05 the loops always meant. **ONE EXCLUSION, STRUCTURAL AND NAMED:** `tools/demo_reel.gd` captures frames and must await idle ones, because that is when the viewport has drawn; it does step boarders on a wall clock and that IS a defect, filed here rather than fixed under a bug about the Colossus. **THE CHECK** is `bot · a rig that steps a boarder by hand does it inside a physics frame — the SG-190 clock`: it reads the `tools/` ROSTER rather than a hand-typed list, exempts frame-capturers by `save_png` rather than by filename, and its second half is POSITIONAL — what is forbidden is an idle await BETWEEN steps, which is exactly the `if steps % 200 == 0: await process_frame` the four rigs carried. Reverting one tool's awaits turns it red naming that tool. **WHAT THIS REACHES BACKWARDS TO IS NOT SETTLED HERE AND SHOULD NOT BE READ AS SETTLED.** Every `balance.gd` and `melee_probe.gd` verdict in this ledger was taken with boarders walking at a fraction of their speed; whether a given conclusion survives is a question per row, and the rig is now cheap and repeatable enough to just re-run them. Start with the ones that turned on a boarder ARRIVING somewhere — COLOSSUS-DESIGN §2's Boiler clock above all. |

| SG-181 | DONE 2026-08-04 (opus/SG-181) — `static var store` in `scripts/audio.gd`, diverted in `tests/parity_test.gd`; **2 new checks, harness 1080 → 1082**; **8 consecutive runs 1082/1082** | **`audio · volume survives the session` was FLAKY BECAUSE THE HARNESS AND THE OWNER'S OWN RUNNING GAME WERE FIGHTING OVER ONE FILE — and the harness was writing his volume down to 0.42 on every run** **THE CAUSE IS NOT A RACE AGAINST THE FILESYSTEM AND IT IS NOT AN AUDIO BUG. IT IS A SECOND PROCESS.** `_audio()` wrote 0.42 into the player's REAL `user://settings.cfg`, built a fresh `SkyGearAudio` whose `_ready` reads that file, and asserted 0.42 — and `SkyGear-Godot.exe` (PID 17724, started 06:56, the itch build in `%TEMP%`) was open on this machine the whole time, **rewriting that same file several times a second**. The reported 0.85 was never a stale read of "the previous run's file": it was the OTHER process's live master volume. **MEASURED, NOT REASONED.** A probe that did nothing but `ConfigFile.save` then read back, 200 iterations per path, in one directory: `user://settings.cfg` **53–79 stale of 200** on three separate arms, `user://settings.dat` **0/200**, `user://mysettings.cfg` **0/200**, `user://sg181dir/settings.cfg` **0/200**, `user://sg181_probe.cfg` **0/200**. Deleting `settings.cfg` and letting the probe recreate it changed nothing (61/200), so it follows the PATH, not the file. `save()` returned OK every time; the write never became visible even after polling for 500 ms (**never-recovered 53 of 53**) — a lost write, not a delayed one — and two reads caught the file **0 bytes** and **1 byte** mid-write, which is the second process writing while we read. Finally, with no Godot of mine running at all, the file's mtime advanced 12 s in 12 s with its hash unchanged. **AND THE OTHER PROCESS IS DOING SOMETHING SILLY, WHICH IS WHY IT IS CONSTANT RATHER THAN OCCASIONAL:** `hud.gd:3932` (pause) and `hud.gd:5283` (settings) call `game.audio.set_volume(..., ui.slider(...))` from the DRAW, and `set_volume` calls `save_settings()` — so a build sitting on either screen writes `settings.cfg` on every redraw, forever. Not fixed here (it is not the harness's bug and it is behavioural), filed as **SG-183**. **THE FIX IS THE SG-83 PRECEDENT, EXACTLY.** `SkyGearAudio.store` is the pointer every read and write goes through (`SETTINGS_PATH` stays the const, unchanged for players); `_run()` snapshots the real file, points `store` at `user://settings.harness.cfg` and deletes it BEFORE the first check, so no `_new_game()` in the whole harness can read or write the owner's file; the scratch file is swept at the end and `store` put back. **Two new checks, and the second exists because "0.42 came back" would pass just as happily if `save_settings` had ignored the diversion**: `audio · and the write landed in the harness's own file, not the player's` (the scratch file carries `master=0.42` AND the player's file is byte-identical across that same write) and `editor · and it never touches the player's own settings either` (the last check of the run, the twin of SG-83's layout guard, byte for byte over every check in the project). **EVIDENCE THE FLAKINESS IS GONE, and it is evidence rather than a green run because the offending condition was still live**: PID 17724 was still running for all of it. Before: 1080, **1079**, 1080, 1080, 1080, 1080 (and 3-of-3 red earlier in the day for the agent who filed this). After: **1082 × 8 consecutive**, with `volume survives the session` reading 0.42 every time and the byte compare 127 → 127 every time. **THE THING THAT GENERALISES:** this check was not testing round-tripping, it was testing whether anyone had the game open, and it had been doing that for as long as `settings.cfg` has been shared. Every green run reported since is green on that check by luck. Two more harness fixtures still have this exact shape and one of them is destroying data — **SG-182**. |

Measured: hash of `runs.json` before and after one harness invocation — **changed: True**; the owner's log on this machine right now is **5 rows, heats `[2.0, 0.0, None, None, 0]`**, i.e. entirely harness fixtures including the deliberately malformed legacy row. His real history is gone and has been going for as long as those checks have existed. This is SG-49 exactly ("posed endings wrote fake rows into the player's own run log") in the one place SG-49 did not reach. **`user://keys.cfg`: overwritten, every run.** `SkyGearKeybinds.save()` is called at ≈L10295 with `dash` rebound to F, and it writes the real file; the file on disk is now pure project defaults (`dash=32`, KEY_SPACE), so a player who has rebound anything loses it to one `SkyGear Tools.bat harness` — and a run that dies between the save and the reset leaves him with dash on F. Content-hash was unchanged across the run measured only because an earlier harness run had already flattened it. **The fix is not a new idea, it is the third application of one that already works**: a `static var store` on `SkyGearRunLog` and on `SkyGearKeybinds`, diverted at the top of `_run()` beside the layout and settings stores, swept at the end, with a byte-for-byte untouched guard each — two more checks. `workshop.json` was checked in the same sweep and is CLEAN (`_new_game` already keeps the workshop in memory); `user://sg17_fx_probe.json`, `user://sg81_lights_roundtrip.json` and `user://clip_zero_frames` are harness-owned scratch paths and are fine. **—— THE FIX, AND ONE CORRECTION TO THE SENTENCE ABOVE (2026-08-04).** Both files go through a `store` now, defaulting to the real path so players are unaffected, diverted to `user://runs.harness.json` and `user://keys.harness.cfg` before the first check and restored after. **`workshop.json` was NOT clean and this row was wrong about it.** `_new_game` does keep the workshop in memory — that part is true — but `_fittings()` §4 wrote a fixture save straight to the real `user://workshop.json` twice (a round-trip state at ≈L1409 and a hand-built legacy row at ≈L1422) and put the owner's file back afterwards from a string it took first. It was not being DAMAGED (its hash is stable across a run, measured) but it is his scrip, his sigils, his fittings and his berths riding on nothing raising in between the write and the restore. Diverted the same way, and §4 now reads and writes `store` throughout — which is how the mismatch was found: with the fixture write still on `PATH` and the read on `store`, `fittings · a pre-berth winner's save keeps its wreck` went red the moment the store moved. **EVIDENCE.** Baseline before the change: 1082/1082 green and `runs.json` `b4f44928…` → `d0ee5ea9…`, i.e. changed by the run, exactly as filed. After: `keys.cfg c67d8401`, `runs.json d0ee5ea9`, `settings.cfg f74242fd`, `workshop.json a16eebd8` — **identical before and after a full harness run, and identical again after five more**. Totals, five consecutive: **1085, 1085, 1085, 1085, 1085**. Stronger than the four hashes: a recursive md5 manifest of the ENTIRE `user://` tree (engine-owned `logs/`, `shader_cache/`, `vulkan/`, `objectdb_snapshots/` excluded) taken before and after a run `diff`s empty, so nothing was created, deleted or moved either. **THE COMPLETE SWEEP, every `user://` path the harness touches.** REAL, diverted: `hud_layout.json` (SG-83), `settings.cfg` (SG-181), `runs.json`, `keys.cfg`, `workshop.json` (this row). SCRATCH, harness-owned, safe — named here so the next sweep does not re-flag them: `hud_layout.harness.json`, `settings.harness.cfg`, `runs.harness.json`, `keys.harness.cfg`, `workshop.harness.json`, `sg17_fx_probe.json`, `sg81_lights_roundtrip.json`, `clip_zero_frames/`. NOT the harness: `albedo_dump.png`, `dbg_hud.png`, `emission_dump.png` are stale artifacts with no writer anywhere in the tree any more (grep for `save_png` finds no `user://` target at all — every shot tool writes `res://.shots`), untouched by five runs, and safe to delete by hand; `logs/`, `shader_cache/`, `vulkan/`, `objectdb_snapshots/` are the engine's. **The five real files are the whole set, and now none of them moves.** |

The climb is read off the TEN up-facing tread planes (-0.761 to +0.353, a 12.4 riser, over a floor at -0.863) plus one riser for the landing — **eleven risers**, which is what the delivery note said it was built with. **THE PLACEMENT IS ONE DERIVATION AND IT RESOLVES THREE CONSTRAINTS THAT WERE IN TENSION.** (1) SG-176 measured that a stair running forward UNDERNEATH the platform cannot be found in the frame, so the flight must be AFT of the platform's aft edge. (2) Nothing in this kit may stand in the rectangle she walks, so it cannot be aft of the BOW LINE. (3) The stair's slope is the model's and its rise is the platform's height, so its run is not free — **354.7 units at the shipped rail scale**. Together those leave exactly one strip of ship for the flight, the apron between the bow line and the platform, and (3) says how wide it has to be. So **the platform's aft edge is one stair-run forward of the bow line** (`upper_deck_aft_z()` = -1518.7) and **its beam is the hull's own beam where it seats** (`_build_edge_prow`'s rule, for `_build_edge_prow`'s reason): 980 units at `hull_beam` 0.583, so the outer bays end **exactly on the strake lines** and no post stands in open air. There is no setting that puts the stair under the platform or in the rectangle. **THE PLATFORM'S HEIGHT IS DERIVED FROM THE SHIPPED RAIL AND THE HARNESS PROVES IT IS.** `upper_platform_top()` is `RAIL_TOP_NATIVE * edge_rail_scale() * 2` = **250.68**, and `edge · the upper deck's floor is two of the SHIPPED rail's own heights, and it moves when the rail does` asserts it from BOTH sides in one fixture (SG-146): it rebuilds at `edge_rail_tiles` = 8, where the rail's top is 156.7 rather than 125.3, and requires the BUILT bays' plank tops to have moved with it — **250.7 → 313.3**. A literal 250 passes the first half of that check and fails the second. **THE SEAM BETWEEN THE RAIL AND THE CORNER POST WAS THE THING ONLY VISIBLE ONCE BOTH WERE PLACED, AND IT IS CLEAN BY ARITHMETIC.** `upper_rail_count()` takes the largest number of modules whose run fits between the two posts — **3, not the 7 SG-176's mock put across the full 1,680 beam**, because this platform is 980 wide and the rail is NOT respecced (the module and its two-pitch step are the deck's own). The run spans x **-363.9..363.9** and the posts' inboard faces are at **-363.9 and 363.9**: the rail's cap ends exactly where the post begins, and its last stanchion clears the post by the 15.9 units of cap overhang, so there is neither a gap nor an interpenetration. Pinned by `edge · the rail run across the platform ends in the corner posts, and the whole run fits the platform's beam`, which also asserts the posts' outboard faces (+/-424.5) stay inside the 980 beam — the constraint that made the count 3 rather than 4. **MEASURED, IN ONE FREEZE, AGAINST A CONTROL THAT KEEPS THE PROW ON IN BOTH PLATES:** stem/1.00 **21.72%** of the frame, stem/1.55 **16.52%**, mid/1.55 **2.06%**, and **mid/1.00 0.00% with a maximum single-channel delta of 0**. That last number is the honest half and it is FORCED rather than a failure: SG-176's lattice found that at mid-deck at the shipped zoom nothing forward of the bow line reads at all (18 probes of 1,078, every one of them inboard of it), so **the upper deck cannot be seen from mid-deck at the shipped zoom without standing in the rectangle she walks** — and it may not. The mock's 1.24% came from a platform seated ON the bow line. The stem figures are likewise below the mock's 49.31/38.48 for the same reason: the mock was full-beam at the bow line and had a stair in it that could not be found. **THE VERDICT: FORECASTLE, NOT A LID, AND THE POSTS ARE WHY.** At the shipped zoom the platform's top is cropped by the frame and all the player gets is the soffit — which on its own is exactly the lid the open question feared — but the five posts descend through the frame to the planking with their brass foot plates lit, the knees break the beam line, and the stair climbs across the middle of it. A lid has no legs. At 1.55 the whole structure is in frame and there is no question at all. **SET DRESSING, PROVED FROM THE TREE.** Sixteen instanced pieces, not one collision node in the kit's 149 (`edge · nothing in the edge kit can stop her either`), nothing in the plan rectangle (`edge · nothing in the edge kit stands in the rectangle she walks`, now reporting 37 pieces in plan rather than 21), and a MARGIN rather than a rounding: `edge · the whole upper deck stands forward of the bow line, by a margin rather than by a rounding` reports the aft-most piece 4.0 units clear, because `Rect2.intersects` excludes borders and a piece seated at exactly `DECK_RECT.position.y` would pass or fail on the last bit of a float. `edge · the upper deck's stair climbs from the deck plane to the platform's own floor, and lands at both ends` reads the built node: foot at y 0.0, forward face at z -1519 against a platform aft face at z -1519, top tread 227.5 one 23.2 riser under a floor at 250.7. **Proved by negative control**: with `upper_deck` false the run is **1073/1077** and the four failures are exactly the four new checks. **AND A BUG IN THE CHECKS THAT WAS WORTH THE HOUR.** The first pass measured every upper piece at its own model origin — the stair's foot at y -86.3, its z at +95 — because the stern check above rebuilds the kit twice and `rebuild_edge_kit` REPLACES the node: the `kit` handle gathered at the top of the block was by then an orphan awaiting `queue_free`, and `global_transform` on a node outside the tree returns its LOCAL one. It also pushed 47 engine errors and tripped `harness · and no more engine errors than the ones already written down`, which is the check that caught it. The kit is re-fetched after every rebuild now, with the reason written beside it. **TWO DEPARTURES RECORDED HONESTLY.** (a) The bay is **0.57 as thick as it is wide** against §1's asked “about a fifth”, so at 5 bays the soffit sits 216.8 above the planking and the knees hang to 139.2 — she would clear the soffit and not the knees, which costs nothing because the whole platform is over the apron she cannot walk on, but a thinner bay would have bought a taller-looking deck. (b) **The rail crosses the head of the stair.** A real companionway has a gate there; omitting the module over the flight would delete a third of a 3-module run, and at the shipped zoom the rail is above the frame anyway. Left as it is, written down rather than worked around. |

A/B in one freeze: **49.31% of the frame at `stem`/1.0, 38.48% at 1.55, 9.22% at `mid`/1.55, 1.24% at `mid`/1.0** — the lattice's prediction, confirmed by a picture. **The rail reuse is therefore measured, not arithmetic: no new railing asset is specced.** The mock also caught a design error worth more than the spec paragraph it replaced — **a stair run forward UNDER the platform cannot be found in the frame**, so the flight climbs aft-to-forward on the open deck. **TWO BUGS IN THE TOOL, BOTH FOUND BY DISBELIEVING ITS FIRST ANSWER.** (1) It reported *nothing is visible anywhere*: the project renders at a 1920×1080 base into a 1600×900 window, so `unproject_position` speaks in 1920 while the readback image is 1600 — **every probe was sampled 167 pixels away from itself**. `edge_place.gd` has a note that these two differ and never converts; this one does. (2) The per-slice tables were IDENTICAL across four different cameras, because the lookup matched on lattice coordinates alone while `_rows` holds every pose — failure mode two, caught by four cameras printing one picture. **THE FLOOR IS PER-PROBE, NOT PER-FRAME**: SG-175's 0.17% of a mostly-sky frame could be 30% of a forty-pixel rectangle, so the first slice of every pose takes a third plate with the probes hidden and runs the identical per-rectangle measurement — **worst 0.00% at three of four poses**, 14.88% on one rectangle at `mid`/1.55 against a 4% trace threshold, reported rather than smoothed. **AND THE DOCUMENT IS PROSE, WHICH IS A CORRECTION MID-TASK.** Alex: *"measurements don't really help us with the workflow. Ratios and descriptive prompts are what's going to help us create the image and then the image gets turned into a 3D model."* So `UPPER-DECK-KIT.md` carries no ground-unit table at all — five pieces, each with an image prompt in `../weapons/PROMPTS.md`'s register, a ratio ("about a dozen risers", "two of your rails stacked", "as deep as it is wide"), and what it must read as at the size it is actually seen. **The numbers live here and in the tool.** Alex: *"Maybe we just need more objects like we did with the Ship kit so we can just more procedurally build out the prow and stern... I wanted there to be an upper level that you can see from the deck but you don't actually go on to it. I'm having it make a staircase model."* SG-174 is the constraint this has to clear: two hand-made sterns of different proportion both measured **0.000% of pixels changed** because this camera never sees the outside of the hull. So the first deliverable is not a spec, it is the ENVELOPE — `tools/edge_volume.gd`, a probe lattice beyond the play rectangle at both ends, judged the way SG-174 judged the stern (two plates in one `SkyGearStill` freeze, pixels changed per probe) rather than by eye. Then `handoff-3d/ship_edge_kit/UPPER-DECK-KIT.md`, and per his correction of 2026-08-03 — *"measurements don't really help us with the workflow. Ratios and descriptive prompts are what's going to help us"* — that document is PROSE, RATIOS AND PROMPTS. The numbers stay here and in the tool. |
