| SG-170 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-170) — `view3d.mount_weapon` + the `_sync_rig` call site, three `weapons.json` rows, `--fit` opened up in the lab, **eight `weapon ·` checks, harness 1061 → 1069**. Fits are PROVISIONAL: Alex finishes them in the lab | **Alex hand-made three weapons, they were ingested and committed, and NOTHING IN THE GAME EVER PUT THEM IN A HAND** The boarding pike, the furnace axe and the scrap wrench were in `assets/models/`, loaded through `ResourceLoader`, and were registered in `weapons.json` — and were not on the deck. **THE GAP WAS NOT A NUMBER, IT WAS A CALL THAT DID NOT EXIST.** `weapons.json` had per-character MOUNT rows for `boilerwright` and `captain` only, which is the visible half; the half that mattered is that **the only `hold()` in the whole renderer was the hero's**, in `_sync_captain`. `_sync_rig` builds the rig for every boarder AND for the crew (they come through it as kind `CREW`) and had never mounted anything, so table rows alone would have moved nothing. That is STATUS's first failure mode wearing its largest coat: not a field with no reader, **a whole asset class with no reader**, and every green signal around it — files load, table parses, `weapon_fit` returns a row — stayed green throughout. **THE FIX IS ONE FUNCTION, `SkyGearView3D.mount_weapon`,** called from the one-time setup block of `_sync_rig` (beside the `model_key`, rotor and parts stamping, for the same reason: `hold()` frees a `BoneAttachment3D`, loads a scene and walks the weapon's meshes twice, and twelve crew at 60 fps is a stall) — and the hero's own block was MOVED onto it rather than left beside it, so the 1.8 m-frame scaling that the whole thing turns on exists once. **The scaling is the part that is easy to get wrong and it is now pinned by measurement**: `weapon · a weapon is scaled by the figure holding it, not by the captain` mounts the same row on a 1 m body and a 2 m body and requires the reach to double — 0.938 m against 1.875 m, ratio 2.000. **THE DELIVERABLE IS THE LIVE CHECK.** `weapon · a live deck puts the pike, the axe and the wrench in the hands they were made for — the SG-170 regression` spawns ARMORED, SWARM and a crewman through the simulation, runs `view._process`, and reads `_rigs[key].held` — it calls nothing itself, because a check that calls `mount_weapon` would have passed every run of the week the deck stood empty-handed. **Proved by negative control, not asserted**: with the one `mount_weapon` line in `_sync_rig` commented out the run is 1067/1069 and the two failures are exactly that check and `weapon · and the weapon is mounted once, not rebuilt on every frame` — while its control, `weapon · and a kind the table never armed is still drawn, still a rig, still empty-handed`, stays green on SCRAPPER in the same frame, so the guard cannot be satisfied by a renderer that arms nothing. `weapon · a kind with no fit row keeps an empty hand — a refusal, not a crash` covers the other three rigged kinds. The roster checks read the TABLE, never a typed list (failure mode seven): arm a fourth figure and they fail until the renderer mounts it. **THE LAB COULD NOT OPEN ON ANY OF THESE THREE AND NOW CAN** — `tools/model_lab.gd` resolved `--fit` through `HERO_MODELS`, which has exactly two rows, so `--fit crew` silently fell through to the CAPTAIN's body and you would have been fitting a two-metre pike to her proportions. It resolves through `SkyGearView3D.model_path` now, and `--fit` also picks its OWN weapon when `--model` is not given — without that the lab mounted whatever was first in the list, which was the `armored` CHARACTER mesh, whose AnimationPlayer was then picked up as the timeline and posed the crewman face-down. `godot --path . --resolution 1600x900 --script tools/model_lab.gd -- --mount --fit crew` (and `armored`, `swarm`) now opens the right body holding the right object on its own clips; `.shots/sg170/lab/sheet_fix.png`. **FITS ARE PROVISIONAL AND SAID SO OUT LOUD**: crew `rot [-180,-135,0] off [0.283,0.043,-0.318] len 2.50`, armored `rot [135,-135,0] off [0.177,0.197,0.025] len 1.20`, swarm `rot [90,-45,0] off [0.062,0.041,0.179] len 0.85`. Each was searched numerically and then LOOKED AT through the game at idle and at three points across the swing (`tools/grip_sheet.gd`, `tools/grip_montage.py`, `.shots/sg170/shipped/sheet-*.png`), which is enough to say the weapon is in the right hand the right way up at the right rough size and no more than that. **The captain's row was not touched by this work** — it changed under the tree at 19:18 because Alex was saving his own cutlass re-fit out of the lab, and it now reads `rot [-16.848,20.577,-87.500] off [0.3044,0.1336,-0.0900] len 1.07` at full lab precision, which is exactly the fit he sent. `boilerwright` and the whole `weapons` block are byte-identical to HEAD; the file's reindent is his save, not this row's. |
| SG-172 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-170) — `tools/model_lab.gd:_deck_units` now calls `SkyGearView3D.boarder_height` | **The lab reported a gremlin as 165 ground units tall while the deck drew it at 83, under a comment predicting exactly that** `_deck_units` carried its own copy of `120 + radius*3` under a comment reading *"The same expression `_sync_all` uses for a boarder's height. If that one moves, this one is wrong and the lab will quietly lie about every enemy."* **It had moved.** `FIGURE_SCALE` arrived afterwards (SG-87, then SG-103 for the crew) and halves SCRAPPER, GUNNER and SWARM and cuts CREW by an eighth, so the lab has been printing SWARM at 165 against a deck that draws it at 83 and had no branch for the crew at all — failure mode two, caught by its own comment. It asks `boarder_height()` now, which is the function that exists to be the one copy, and `crew_height()` for the sailors. Evidence: `.shots/sg170/lab/sheet_view.png` reads CREW 144, ARMORED 216, SWARM 83. Found while opening `--fit` up to the three SG-170 figures, which is the first time anyone had pointed the lab at a shrunk one. |
| SG-169 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-169) — `tools/demo_reel.gd` + `tools/demo_encode.py`, nine shots, per-shot displacement measured | **A 27-second demo reel the owner can send to friends — and v1 failed because the captain never moved** Owner asked for something shareable, watched v1, and said: *"The demo is cool but the character never runs around? I'd like to show how enemies jump down onto the deck and show more areas of the game."* **V1 POSED HER AND POSING IS WHY SHE STOOD STILL** — she only acts when something is in reach, so she swung and never relocated. V2 hands the captain to `tools/bot.gd` through the real input map, and making that work was the SG-118 lesson in a second file: `game.set_process(false)` never reached the captain, so she was being walked by the ENGINE on a wall-clock delta while everything else stepped at DT. The figures and props are hand-stepped in one `_tick()` now and `Engine.physics_ticks_per_second` is pinned to 60, because otherwise `move_and_slide()` integrates velocity on one clock and commits ground on another. **EVERY GAMEPLAY SHOT IS MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED:** `track.csv` beside each shot records her ground position on every captured frame, and the tool prints the table — path length 741 to 6020 units per shot, all four exempt shots being cutscenes or menus where `controls_enabled` is false. **THE LANDING SHOT WORKS BECAUSE IT DOES NOT CALL `_place`** — that helper's third line is `state = "move"`, which is exactly what ENDS the arrival window, and is why v1 had no boarders in the air. They come through `spawn_enemy` and cross on the simulation's own arc, staged on wave 4 so the boarding hulk is grappled to the bow and visibly the thing they come off. Three other traps found by looking: the menu plates were seamed at x=1333 because `Screens.pose` was handed `root.size` against a 1920-wide `canvas_items` stretch base; the draft plate was mislabelled because `draft_heading` reads `opening_draft` first; and the arrival cutscene's last 45 frames are empty planking while the camera hands back — right in the game, dead in a trailer. CRF moved 18 -> 20 and the reason is in the tool's help: a camera that now moves in every shot gives inter-frame prediction far less to work with, so the same flags came back 31.2 MB. Re-measured rather than assumed — decoded back to PNG and diffed every frame against its capture, worst mean absolute error 1.81/255. **27.0 s, 811 frames, 1600x900, 24.0 MB.** The reel is gitignored (`*.mp4`); the two tools that build it are not. |
| SG-187 | DONE 2026-08-04 (opus/SG-187) — both halves of the ask shipped, and **the only measurable cost in the row turned out to be a cosmetic line nobody asked for, which is REFUSED and deleted**; harness **1085 → 1100**, fifteen `crew ·` checks; `.shots/clips/sg187-before.gif` against `sg187-after.gif` | **Crew stand idle at the end of a cleared lane while there is fighting elsewhere on the deck** — owner, after a full 12-wave run: *"Dont have crew standing around and auto attacking at the end of their lane - we can improve their AI to run towards available enemies if their lane is clear."* **TWO FAULTS, ONE LINE.** `_update_crew` sent a crewman with nothing to fight to a fixed point at the head of his lane and the arrival test there was `distance <= reach` — a test that asks how far away his GOAL is and never asks whether anything is STANDING in it. So he arrived, wound up, swung at bare planking, recovered, and did it again for the rest of the wave. Both halves of the owner's sentence are that one test. **THE RULES, decided explicitly and written at `SkyGearLanes.ASSIST_LEASH`.** (1) *His own lane outranks everything, re-asked every tick* — targeting is recomputed from scratch each frame with the own-lane scan first, so the recall is THE ORDER OF TWO LOOPS rather than a timer that can run out, and he turns for home the tick a boarder lands in his lane. (2) *The leash is one lane* — an ADJACENT lane only, and only a boarder within 560 (the `LANE_CENTERS` spacing, read off the deck) of his own STATION, not of his feet; a leash measured from his feet lets a man walk the deck one leash at a time, and measured from the post it is also the whole bound, since he walks straight at a point inside a circle he is already inside. Recall from the far edge is 560/118 = **4.7 s** against **6.6 s** for the fastest boarder in the game (a 230-unit gremlin) to reach the gun from the bow. (3) *One man per boarder* — an assisting hand CLAIMS his target in muster order, so the number that can leave a lane is bounded by the boarders next door inside somebody's leash; that is the anti-clump guarantee as arithmetic, not as a hope. (4) *The anchor* — the longest-serving unengaged hand in each lane never leaves it, so no arrangement of boarders can empty a lane. A vulnerable hulk still outranks a neighbour's fight (`siege` 22 against a swing of 1.5). **AND THE MEASUREMENT, WHICH IS THE POINT OF THE ROW.** Heat 0, `tools/balance.gd`, **n=240 per arm** (four batches of 60; the rig prints ±9 points on a held-count and ~11% on the taken mean at this n, so a smaller sample was not attempted — SG-128). Crew damage had to be separable from gunnery first: `tel.allies` is twelve sailors PLUS three deck cannons and the owner's 12% is about the sailors, so `src_slot = -4` and a `tel.crew` row that is a SUBSET of `allies` — counted in both, so every ally share ever printed still means exactly what it did. **BASELINE: crew 6.02% of all damage [5.70..6.33], taken 346.0, held 192/240 = 80.0% [74.5..84.6].** **SHIPPED: crew 6.30% [5.99..6.62] (+4.7%, Welch t=1.29), taken 358.5 (+3.6%, t=1.47), held 193/240 = 80.4% [74.9..84.9].** Nothing there clears what the sample can resolve — **the roaming is free**, and it is reported as "under the instrument at n=240" rather than as "no effect". **THE EXPENSIVE FINDING.** Every hand in a lane holds the SAME station, so four sailors of radius 15 stand inside one another — invisible while they mill through a swing cycle, obvious the day they stand still, which is what this row did. So the first version fanned them out ±120 off the muster jitter they already carry. That ONE line came back at **crew 4.55% (−24%, t=−7.2) and taken 406 (+17%, t=6.8)**, every batch outside the baseline's batch range. Fanned DOWN the lane instead of across it cost the same (**4.85% / 387**), and hold-station-with-no-roaming ALSO cost it (**4.71% / 387**) — which rules out "a roadblock with a hole in it" and leaves the real mechanism: **a stacked watch is four swings on one boarder with no walking**, and 120 units of separation is about a second of not swinging per man per boarder for the whole run. Crew damage is a RATE and a rate is what a walk costs. **So the fan is refused and `POST_SPREAD` is DELETED rather than set to zero — a knob whose only correct value is zero is a knob somebody turns** — and `crew · every hand in a lane holds the same station — fanning the idle watch out cost 17% more damage taken, so it is refused` carries the number. **FOR THE OWNER:** the watch still stands as one man on each lane; if you want it to look like a watch, that is buyable with a renderer-side nudge that does not move the simulation, or by paying for it somewhere — say the word and it is a small row. **THE STRAFE CLIPS STAY UNWIRED** and the assist does not create a case for them: a man crossing to the next lane still walks in a straight line at what he is fighting, so his travel vector and his threat vector are still one vector. The one genuinely new pose is the opposite of a strafe — a man STANDING, watching the bow, which is `idle`. Still your open question. **THE CHECKS** (15): the pre-committed regression is `crew · a boarder in his own lane is still his, whatever is happening next door`; then the feature, the leash, one-per-boarder, the anchor, the recall, the hulk's precedence both ways round, `crew · a man with nothing to fight stands at his post instead of swinging at the planking` (0 swing-ticks over 8 s on an empty deck), `· and he is standing still in the field the renderer reads, not only in the one the simulation does`, the station rule above, the two telemetry ones, and two arithmetic ones — including `crew · and two lanes over is out of reach by geometry, so the adjacency clause is a guard rather than the mechanism`, written that way because a check asserting "he ignores lane 2" would pass with the clause deleted (930 > 560) and would have been vacuous. **EVIDENCE:** `tools/clip.gd -- crewassist` (new scenario, 9 s, `--resolution 1600x900`, never `--headless`): twelve hands at their stations, five boarders in the middle lane, nothing in the outer two. BEFORE, the eight outer men stand in two clumps swinging at bare planking for the whole clip; AFTER, each flank keeps its anchor and the rest cross to the fight. Two staged numbers, both identical in both arms and both declared at the code: the boarders carry 2000 health (at 60 the captain's own auto-attack resolves all five in three seconds and the clip films an empty deck) and she is held invulnerable through the simulation's own i-frame field (she is the anvil the fight is pinned to — a fight staged at the bow otherwise walks down-deck at the Boiler and out of frame). **One tool bug found and fixed on the way:** `balance.gd`'s vow argument is the third positional and Windows PowerShell DROPS an empty native-command argument, so `-- 6 0 "" 20` arrived as `[6, 0, 20]` and the rig refused to start with "no such article: 20". `none` is the empty vow now. |
| SG-128 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-128) — status cell was left IN PROGRESS; its own notes recorded the completion | **Every `n=6` verdict in this ledger is a coin flip wearing a number — and the rig's "8% noise floor" is not a floor** Filed before fixing, per rule 3, off the contradiction SG-125's audit found and SG-127 inherited. SG-14 records Heat 0 as **5/6 held**; SG-26 records the same Heat 0, same six BAL seeds, same tool, same day as **3/6 held**. **Neither is wrong and they are not two answers.** Fisher's exact on that 2x2 gives **p = 0.55**; the Wilson intervals are 44..97% and 19..81% and overlap across 37 points. A held-count at n=6 is resolved to **plus or minus 40 percentage points** in the worst case, so the rig could not have distinguished them and nothing in the repository could say so. **n=6 was not an unusual sample — it was the DEFAULT**: `SEEDS` has six entries and `count` is clamped to its size, so every invocation predating SG-118's `reps` argument was n<=6. **AND A SECOND CONTRADICTION, IN THE PARAGRAPH THAT WARNS ABOUT INSTRUMENTS:** `balance.gd`'s header tells the reader to check a difference by running `tools/balance_ab.gd` and to *"disbelieve anything that does not clear"* an 8% floor. **`tools/balance_ab.gd` has never existed** — not in this tree, not in any commit; `git grep balance_ab` returns that one line and nothing else. **And the 8% is not a floor.** It was measured honestly (two n=120 batches of identical code, 8% apart on damage-taken) but read as an instrument property, and a floor does not shrink when you run more runs. **Action:** derive what this rig's sample actually buys, put it in the tool's OUTPUT and its HEADER rather than only in a board row, and give the harness the arithmetic so the next n=6 verdict fails a check instead of aging in the ledger for two days. **✅ DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-128).** THE ANSWER: **neither row is wrong and they are not two answers.** Fisher exact p=0.55; Wilson 44..97% against 19..81%. **A held-count at n=6 is worth +-40 points**, so the rig could not have distinguished them, and n=6 was the DEFAULT before SG-118 added `reps`. **THE INSTRUMENT:** new `tools/bal_stat.gd` (Wilson, overlap test, worst-case resolution, Welch t, and a POWER CALCULATOR); `balance.gd` now prints its Wilson interval, its worst-case resolution and a refusal in words under every result, and the header carries the table and the two corrections. **THE 8% "FLOOR" IS NOT A FLOOR** — bootstrapped from 240 real Heat 0 runs the batch-to-batch range of the taken-mean is +-6.4% AT n=120, which IS the 8%, and it falls to +-4.5% at n=240. It is sampling error mistaken for an instrument property. Confirmed independently: two n=120 Heat 3 baselines of IDENTICAL code came back 66.7% and 75.0% held (+8.3 points, p=0.16). **`tools/balance_ab.gd` HAS NEVER EXISTED** — the header's one instruction for checking a difference pointed at nothing; replaced with a rule that runs on what is in the repo. **THE REPLACEMENT IS A PRICE LIST, NOT A CONSTANT** (a constant gate was wrong in BOTH directions — roughly right at n=120 by accident, three times too permissive at SG-126's n=30 where the real bar is ~26%). **This rig sees about a third of a change and cannot see a twentieth at any price**: 30% costs n=23/arm, 10% costs 200, 1% costs ~20,000 (61 h). In STATUS beside the failure modes. **IT REACHES BACKWARDS — three results were dismissed on the mistaken reading**, all conservative, none shipped wrongly, the reasoning wrong differently each time: **SG-117** (-10%, t=2.43 = **p=0.015**) actually CLEARED its own bar; the floor argument double-counted the noise. Believe the sign, not the magnitude (n=120 is ~57% powered); the row's conclusion stands on its independent bot-walks-out-of-fire argument. **SG-119** (-1.0%, t=0.21) needed ~20,000 runs/arm — not unresolved, unresolvable. **SG-126** (n=30) cited a gate three times too permissive and was saved by its own instinct. None re-run; the correction is to the argument. In NEEDS_ALEX §SG-128 because the owner was told SG-117's restraint was good discipline. **CHECKS (8), harness 902 -> 910.** `bal · 5 of 6 and 3 of 6 are the same result` · `and the same two rates DO separate once the sample is honest` (the control) · `the floor below which nothing is recorded is derived from the gap it has to see, not picked` · `a held-count at the old six-seed default is worth plus or minus forty points` · `and the quotable sample is the one that meets the eight percent the rig's own header declared` · `and the rig refuses a verdict in words at a sample it cannot support` · `the eight percent gate was three times too permissive at the sample size it was last quoted at` · `and an effect the rig cannot afford to see is priced in runs rather than dismissed as noise`. **AND AN HONEST NOTE ON WHAT THOSE CHECKS ARE WORTH, because a reader counting harness numbers deserves to know which kind they are looking at: several of the eight pin ARITHMETIC IDENTITIES that only fail if the helper itself is broken. A check that can only catch its own helper breaking is a REGRESSION GUARD, not evidence the feature works.** Demonstrated rather than asserted, two ways: reverting `MIN_N`/`HONEST_N` to 6 (the pre-SG-128 de facto default) turns **3** red; making `held_resolution()` return 0 — which encodes the old belief that sample size does not affect resolution — turns **3** red, overlapping in 2. Five of the eight are guards; three carry the argument. **AND THE DERIVATION GUARD CAUGHT ITS OWN AUTHOR ON THE FIRST RUN:** `MIN_N` was set to a round 30 by eye and the check failed with *"MIN_N 30 against a derivation of 31"* before this file had ever shipped. It is 31 now. That is the case for tying a constant to its derivation instead of to a comment. Files: `tools/{bal_stat,balance,marks_shot}.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`, `docs/BOARD.md`, `STATUS.md`, `/NEEDS_ALEX.md`. Spawned SG-129 (drift, pre-specified, deliberately unresolved) and SG-130 (the Article arms are played by different bots). |
| SG-162 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-162) — `_fan_texture()` rebuilt around a rim line, **7 new harness checks**, `.shots/sg159/before` against `.shots/sg159/after`. Rune probe re-run and green. | **The danger wedge's boundary was a gradient, and the boundary is the one line in this game the player stands next to on purpose.** Owner on the SG-158 telegraphs: *"I like them, but I feel like the edges are a little soft... the edge of that should be very clear to the player. Having it lined with something a little harder, as opposed to that soft edge, could make it clearer."* **HE WAS DESCRIBING A SHAPE THAT WAS A GRADIENT IN ALL THREE DIRECTIONS AT ONCE.** The filled wedge was `(0.30 + 0.55*d)` brightening outward, an outer `smoothstep(1.0, 0.86, d)` fading the last 14% of the reach to nothing, and cut sides feathered over **16% of the HALF-ARC** — 9.6° down each side of the Colossus's 120° fan. Measured off its own pixels: it **peaked at 88% of the reach and had fallen through half its own brightness by 93.5%**, so on his 146 the brightest part of the danger zone was at 128 and the picture was over by 137, nine units short of the deck that kills you. In `.shots/sg159/before` it reads as spilled orange light — against the furnace knight it reads as *his own glow*. **THE TARGET WAS `_ring_texture()`'S OWN STATED PRINCIPLE, twenty lines up the same file and never applied here:** *the browser's rings are a fill you can see through and an EDGE YOU CANNOT MISS, and the ratio is what makes them readable.* The ring spends 0.05 on its interior and 1.0 on its rim; the wedge spent 0.85 on its interior and nothing on its boundary. **NOW: brightest pixel at 99% of the reach (144 of the Colossus's 146), still carrying half its brightness at 98.8%, full-to-half inside 0.0 texels, fill 0.21 against an edge of 1.00 — 4.7:1.** **THE FEATHERS ARE IN TEXELS RATHER THAN IN FRACTIONS OF THE SHAPE**, which fixed a second thing nobody had chosen: a feather quoted as 16% of the half-arc made the Colossus's boundary **three times softer than a gremlin's**. Measured after: 120° fan **0.9 texels**, 80° fan **0.2** (`telegraph · and the wide fan is no softer than the narrow one, which it used to be by 3x`). **THE SIZE DID NOT MOVE AND THAT IS PINNED TWICE:** `telegraph · the wedge is drawn at the reach the swing connects at` is untouched and green, and `telegraph · and the hard edge did not move the wedge — nothing is painted past the reach` reports **0 texels outside d=1.0**. This is SG-119's whole point — the drawn shape and the connecting shape are one shape, and a rim pushed outward to look bolder would have been that bug wearing this fix's clothes. **THE DECAL GOT LOUDER AND THE SHAPE GOT DIMMER.** The `flick * 0.5` multiplier is gone (the texture owns the ratio now, not the call site) and the inner clock wedge went 0.85 → 0.55, deliberately dimmer than the boundary it travels toward: two hard lines of equal weight would be two boundaries and only one of them is the one you must not cross. **THE FAN IS 256² now** for the sake of a one-texel rim, and that exposed a latent hitch the prewarm comment had half-prevented — `_decal` calls `_glow_map` on whatever texture it is handed and the prewarm built the fans without their emission maps, so the first windup of a run paid for a per-pixel GDScript pass. Prewarmed now. **A SIDE EFFECT THAT IS ACTUALLY THE POINT:** emission is alpha, so the bloom is now concentrated on the rim instead of smeared across the interior — which is exactly the "reads as spilled LIGHT rather than as a mark with an edge" failure SG-158 recorded about its first strike flash. **`tools/rune_probe.gd` RE-RUN, all six checks green:** the no-telegraph frame still selects **exactly 0 pixels**, the four-windup frame selects **5749** (floor 200), noise floor 0.00%. **VERIFY: 1034/1034.** Files: `scripts/view3d.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`. |
| SG-163 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-163) — one derivation, **4 new harness checks**, new `tools/pool_shot.gd`, `.shots/sg160/`. | **A fire pool burned you from outside its own picture, by up to 70%. Three numbers claimed to be one radius and the damage read none of them.** STATUS failure mode two, the one `SkyGearHUD.rail()` and `scripts/ink.gd` both exist because of. `_update_fire_fields` burned at a literal **78.0** written twice; `view3d.gd` sized the pool off `f.get("radius", 62.0) * 2.2`, and the three `_field()` sites set that to **46** (the Sear trail), **62** (a burning prop) and **62 + 22·residue**. The hidden 2D `_draw` had a **fourth** number — a `draw_arc` at 64 beside a `draw_circle` at 78, so even the debug view disagreed with itself. The Sear trail is the worst of them: its ring drew at about **41** after the renderer's `2.2 × 0.82`, against a burn of 78 — **an 88% gap**, and the band of deck between them looked clear and was not. Owner's decision, verbatim: *"For the fire hitbox, match the burn size. Fix the picture to match the damage."* **THE PICTURE MOVED AND NOTHING ELSE DID.** `FIRE_RADIUS` / `fire_pool_radius()` is the only place the number is written; the tick reads it, the 2D `_draw` reads it, and the renderer calls the accessor rather than touching the field dictionary. `_field()` STAMPS the radius, so the caller cannot put a size into a pool that the simulation will ignore — the two per-site `radius` arguments are deleted rather than silently overwritten. **`ring_span_for()` RATHER THAN A MULTIPLIER, and this is the part that would have shipped the same bug one order smaller:** `_ring_texture()`'s bright band peaks at **92%** of its own half-size, so sizing the decal to `burn * 2` would have put the visible line **8% inside** the burn. `RING_RIM_D` is a named constant now and the division is written once. **THE EVIDENCE IS MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED.** `tools/pool_shot.gd` refuses to draw a ring at `fire_pool_radius()` — that would photograph a number equalling itself, which is exactly how a fake fix photographs. It **walks the captain around the pool and runs one real `_update_fire_fields` tick at each of 48×20 spots**, then stands a post on the last spot on each ray that burned. The LETHAL arm returns `_blank("died on wave %d before reaching wave %d" % [game.wave, ...])` — and `game.queue_free()` plus an awaited frame happen on the two lines ABOVE it, so the format string raises `Invalid access to property or key 'wave' on a base object of type 'previously freed'`. That is ~13% of runs at Heat 0 (a run that dies on wave 9 has no wave 12 in it), so it fires most invocations. **THE DAMAGE IS NOT THE RAISE, IT IS THE BLAST RADIUS.** `_blank()` is never constructed, the row appended to `rows` is malformed, and `_summarise`, `_lethal_report` and `_verdict` all iterate every row and read fields as PROPERTIES — so the first bad row aborts the function it is in. Observed: **the BOSS lethal summary line missing entirely, `--- LETHAL:` printing NOTHING for either subject, and the verdict table stopping halfway.** The one arm SG-156 built the tool for produced no output at all, in a run that exited 0 and looked complete. This is STATUS's *"a check that RAISES instead of failing takes the rest of its function's checks with it"* — the same lesson SG-146 paid for in the harness — arriving in a measuring tool, where nothing is watching the exit code. Fixed both ways: the wave is read BEFORE the free, and every reader goes through `.get()` so a malformed row can only ever cost its own contribution. **AND A SECOND DEFECT IN THE SAME FUNCTION:** `_lethal_report` printed damage as `landed × 26` or `landed × 34` — the two rows' `damage` fields, TYPED THERE. Two places holding one number (STATUS failure mode two), and it broke the moment the Colossus gained a damage source that is not a swing: the derived total would have reported only the half it knew the arithmetic for. It reads `taken` now, which is `tel.taken_by_source[wave][kind]` — the simulation's own sum. The arm also prints a machine-readable `LETHAL kind=... secs=... taken=...` line, for the reason `boss_probe` prints `SAMPLES`. **ONE CAVEAT LEFT STANDING AND NAMED RATHER THAN FIXED:** since SG-166 the BOSS's `landed` column is contaminated at ~4% by a stomp resolving on the same frame as a swing (`landed` is credited from a damage delta). Read `taken` for the Colossus and `landed` for the knight. |
| SG-168 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-166) — filed per rule 3, fixed in the same session | **The harness's source reader stops at the first BLANK LINE of any function it is pointed at — every `.gd` in this repo is CRLF and it splits on `\n`** `_function_body()` splits on `"\n"`, so on a CRLF file every line it yields ends in a stray `\r`. A blank line inside a function therefore arrives as `"\r"`, which is not `""`, does not begin with a tab, and trips the *"we have left the function"* break. **So the reader has been returning the first paragraph of a function and calling it the function.** Found because a new SG-166 check wanted a branch 226 lines into `_sync_effects` and got `-1` for it. The standing user is `arrival · the arrival tier holds no clock of its own — the SG-133 regression guard`, which reported **"38 lines read"** of a 40-line function and passed on the part it happened to reach: a detector answering about a fraction of its subject while printing a number that made it look thorough. **The blast radius here is small and the row says so** — two lines, and the guard still passes at 40 — but the failure mode is not: a source-reading check that silently truncates is STATUS's *"a detector silenced to make a screen pass"* with nobody having silenced anything. The `\r` is stripped before the split; the printed count moved 38 → 40, which is the evidence. |

The mast trial is left in the file as `rig_model_masts`, **default OFF**, to answer whether the owner's mast should REPLACE the procedural casters, FEED them, or stand beside them. **THE CHECKS GUARD THE THINGS A SCREENSHOT CANNOT:** that the rail is the tiling module and not the solid bar it replaced, that it tiles exactly so the pitch survives every seam, that no module reaches inboard of the deck she walks, and that nothing in the kit can stop her. |

Knockback was ruled out arithmetically before it was measured: BOSS `mass` 24, so a 150-knock auto yields 6.25 u/s against a 1050 u/s decay — under two units of travel. The CROWD arm exists because the i-frame economy was the one hypothesis a duel could not see (`take_damage` grants 0.55 s on EVERY landed hit, so a 6-damage gremlin can in principle eat a 26-damage boss swing); with wave 12's own roster held topped up beside the subject it still reads **60 of 60 landed, 0 swallowed**. **THE ACTUAL FINDING IS LIFETIME, AND IT SPLITS THE OWNER'S TWO CLAIMS APART.** The LETHAL arm plays the real run to the subject's LAST wave with the bot drafting, then pins the captain and lets the subject die. **FURNACE KNIGHT — THE OWNER IS EXACTLY RIGHT, AND THE CAUSE IS HEALTH, NOT HIT DETECTION:** wave 11, 342 effective HP, it **LIVES 3.13 s** and in that entire life resolves **1.0 swing and lands 1.0** — 34 damage, once, 34% of a captain. Its own cycle is `windup 0.90 + recover 1.00 = 1.90 s`, so it dies inside roughly one cycle. SG-97 designed it as *"a wall you read, not a DPS check"* with a 0.90 s tell; **a tell nobody survives to complete is not a tell.** **COLOSSUS — THE REPORT DOES NOT REPRODUCE, AND THIS ROW SAYS SO:** wave 12, 5771 effective HP, he **lives 28.45 s** and lands **11.8 swings for 306 damage — three times a captain's pool.** Standing next to him is lethal and `boss_probe`'s 123.4 was not an artefact. **WHAT SEPARATES HIM FROM THE OWNER'S EXPERIENCE IS MOTION, NOT DISTANCE** — the brief's recorded hypothesis (the bot orbits at 210 while his second beat reaches 253) was half right about the cause and wrong about the mechanism. `attack_direction` is latched ONCE, on the frame the windup trips, and never tracks through the 0.90 s wind. Pinned: **60/60** land at 0.0° off-axis. Orbiting: **20/60**, mean **74.8°** off-axis against a 60° half-wedge. That latch is DELIBERATE — it is exactly what SG-97 bought with 234 units of walk after the wedge appears — so it is not filed as a defect. **NOTHING WAS RETUNED.** The knight's health and the Colossus's are balance decisions of the same class as `COLOSSUS_WALKS_THE_LANE`, they belong to the owner, and NEEDS_ALEX carries both with the numbers. **CHECK (1, and it is a REGRESSION GUARD, named as one in its own check string):** `telegraph · a captain standing in the wedge is hit by the swing that drew it — REGRESSION GUARD` (`BOSS -26 ARMORED -34`). It passed the moment it was written and could not have found the reported problem, because the problem was not there — **it is not evidence that anything was fixed.** It earns its row because every existing swing check in this file tests `_swing_hits` as pure GEOMETRY, and nothing tested that a windup resolving on a captain in that wedge reaches `player.hp`; between the two sit the victim chain, `can_be_hit()`, `damage_player`, `grants_invuln` and the i-frame window — five places a hit can be dropped with every geometric check still green. **DEMONSTRATED FAILING** by inverting the arc gate's final comparison in `enemy.gd`: `994/1000`, this guard reporting *"BOSS(swung at a captain in its own wedge and she took nothing) ARMORED(...)"*. **TELEGRAPH LEGIBILITY — the owner's second ask, *"the telegraphed moves should be more clear"*, and `tools/telegraph_beat.gd` (new) is the evidence.** `tools/telegraph_shot.gd` could not answer it: it poses ONE frame at `ST_FRAC 0.55` — the middle of the wind, where the tell looks its best — and **has no BOSS in it at all**, which is half of what the owner named. (2) **The wedge is drawn on the planking UNDER a figure that stands between it and the camera** — at 41° the Colossus's body occludes nearly all of his own tell, and the knight most of his. | SG-160 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-160) — **SETTINGS → OPEN ALL HEATS → ON**, the gate intact, and **a bypassed run banks nothing** | **The owner has only ever played Heat 1, and Heat 5 holds 0 of 120 simulated runs — he cannot judge the ladder he cannot reach** Owner, today: *"I've only ever played the game on heat 1, so I don't have a lot of information to share. For me, if you're able to just unlock it so I can jump to any heat, that would be better for my testing."* **THE SHAPE: two functions, not one.** `SkyGearWorkshop.heat_available` is the progression rule and is byte-unchanged — it is still "one rung above your best clear, and nothing before a first victory". `heat_ceiling(state, open_heats)` is new and is what the SCREEN may OFFER. Everything that draws or clamps asks the ceiling; everything that PAYS asks `heat_available`. Collapsing them into one number is the version of this feature that quietly deletes the ladder, which the ask explicitly did not want. **STORAGE: `user://settings.cfg`, `[playtest] open_heats`, beside `fullscreen` and the volumes in `scripts/audio.gd`** — the file that already exists for exactly this ("a second settings file for one boolean is a second settings file"). Deliberately NOT `user://workshop.json`: that file's schema is `fresh()`'s key list and `load_state` merges exactly those keys, so a field added there is a field written into every player's progression file. This is why turning the switch on cannot touch, migrate or invalidate a byte of an existing save — `heat · the bypass lives in settings.cfg and never in the save file` asserts both halves (no `open_heats` key in `fresh()`, and the set/get pair present in `audio.gd`). It persists across restarts and is not editor-only, so it works in the packaged Windows build. **THE PROGRESSION DECISION, MADE AND STATED: a run played above the rung you have earned banks NOTHING AT ALL** — no scrip, no sigil, no fitting, no `best_heat`, and it does not count as the first victory. Not "no heat sigil, everything else as usual": partial credit makes "the tree was bought at Heat 5" a sentence the harness cannot reason about, and the ladder has to keep being a thing other players climb. Decided in `bank()`, FIRST, against the state on the way in — before the first-win latch flips, so a bypassed clear cannot unlock the Workshop by unlocking itself. **AND THE OTHER HALF, which a blunter rule gets wrong: having the switch ON voids nothing.** `heat_bypassed` asks WHERE the run was played, never which switches were set, so a Heat 0 run with the bypass on banks in full — which is what makes the switch safe to leave on permanently. Said in three places: the settings caption, the rung's own `OPEN · … Banks nothing.` strip, and the results sheet (`OPEN HEAT · nothing banked…`, taking the payout line's slot, which the two can never both want). **THE UI:** the ladder is on the title from a save that has never won once the switch is on, the header reads `DIFFICULTY · OPEN`, and rungs above the earned one get their OWN state and colour (flame) rather than the teal that means "the rung you climbed to" or a padlock. The mode rides on the LEFT label on purpose, so the longest string this header can produce is still `HEAT 4 · BOARDERS ALOFT` — the one the audit already measures. **CHECKS: 13 new.** The pre-committed one is `heat · with the bypass off the ladder still climbs one rung at a time — the SG-160 regression`; the other load-bearing one is `heat · a run above the rung you have earned banks nothing at all`. Also `heat · OPEN ALL HEATS offers the whole ladder from a save that has never won`, `· and switching it off puts the ceiling back exactly where it was, from every state of the save` (walks all 12 states rather than asserting at one), `· and a bypassed first clear does not open the Workshop`, `· and a Heat 0 run banks in full however the bypass is set`, `· the bypass lives in settings.cfg and never in the save file`, `· a run can start at the summit from a save that has never won`, `· and it is the real rung — Skeleton Crew's cannons, not a Heat 0 run wearing the number` (cannon **380 at the summit against 760 at Heat 0** — the modifier read off the deck the run built, not off the table), `· and with the bypass off the same run is clamped back to Heat 0`. Three drive the REAL title through `_menu`: `menu · a save that has never won gets no Heat ladder — the gate, on the screen` (0 rungs drawn), `menu · and OPEN ALL HEATS puts every rung on that same title, clickable` (5 drawn), `menu · and the board is still exactly as deep as the column standing on it, with the ladder up and the Workshop absent` — that last one because ladder-present-with-Workshop-absent is a board height combination the bypass INVENTS and `_draw_title`'s arithmetic had never been asked for. **AND IT FOUND A FIXTURE ASSERTING THE RIGHT ANSWER FOR THE WRONG REASON.** `heat · clearing the summit records it but pays no sigil` banked a Heat 5 win onto a save with `best_heat` 0 — under the new rule that is a BYPASSED clear, so it was getting its "no sigil" from the bypass rather than from §5's "the summit is not a reward". One line (`summit.best_heat = h_top - 1`), and the check now tests what its name says. **VERIFY: 1024/1024, 0 script errors**, from this worktree with `--headless --import` run in it once (base at 5ea3ea3: 1011/1011). **TEXT AUDIT: CLEAN, 25 screens x 4 sizes, 8672 strings**, containment clean, worst contrast 2.84 against a 2.6 floor — unchanged. 25 rather than 24 because a pose was ADDED: `title + heat open`, a never-won save with the switch on, which is the state the switch exists to produce and the one carrying every new string on that screen. Counts updated in `STATUS.md` (x3), `README.md`, `docs/HUD-DESIGN.md`, `docs/STEAM-LAUNCH.md` and `tools/hub.gd` (which said 21 and had been wrong for a while). Witness: `.shots/screens/sg155/`. Files: `scripts/workshop.gd`, `scripts/audio.gd`, `scripts/game.gd`, `scripts/hud.gd`, `scripts/screen_poser.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`, `tools/hub.gd`, docs. |

He cannot land on a captain who RETREATS and lands freely on one who ORBITS — a different problem, and not one the chase gate solves. **FINDING: health alone did NOT make a longer boring fight** — wave 12 got 24% longer while damage in it rose 189%, so danger per second rose 157% (t=16.5). Carries a bot caveat (210 band inside a 253 reach) that only the owner's hands can settle. **FINDING: the chase gate FAILS its pre-committed statistic and is shipped `COLOSSUS_WALKS_THE_LANE := false`.** §2's amended (2) required Boiler HP lost in wave 12 to rise by >=40; measured **9.5 → 11.3, t=0.51**, Boiler untouched in 88% of runs — and with the gate on he deals the captain a STRUCTURAL zero, because the victim chain is if/elif and a boss not targeting her never tests her. §2's own rule for that is CUT, not tune. Fetched through `get` with a default now. (2) **The first draft of the gate checks was VACUOUS:** with the constant shipped `false`, deleting the call to `chases_captain()` from the targeting `if` left the harness at 946/946, because both branches behave identically when the gate is off. `walks_the_lane` is a per-instance var seeded from the constant so the harness drives BOTH positions; reverting the guarded line now turns it red (947/948). | SG-147 | DONE 2026-08-03 (fable/SG-147) — capped at depth 1, and **it turns out this was never a design question: the browser already had the rule and the port dropped it** | **A crit explosion can crit, and its explosion can crit — unbounded recursion that hard-crashes the game** `damage_enemy` ends with `if crit and float(mods.crit_explode) > 0.0: _damage_circle(...)`, and `_damage_circle` calls `damage_enemy` on every enemy in radius — each of which can itself crit and explode. **There is no depth guard and no "an explosion may not explode" rule.** It terminates only because the rolls usually stop; when they do not it recurses past a thousand frames and Godot aborts the run with `SCRIPT ERROR: Stack overflow`. Observed twice in ~1,400 headless wave-12 runs, both times with the boss's raised health keeping a dense cluster alive longer — so **it gets MORE likely as fights get longer, and SG-146 just made the longest fight in the game 2.4x longer.** Each crash wrote 700 MB–1.4 GB of stack trace. It is a real player-facing crash on any run that drafts crit-explode, not a headless artifact. **The fix is probably one argument** — a `can_explode := false` on the recursive call, or a depth counter — but it is a live balance path in a contended file and it wants its own before-and-after, not a fourth change riding on a tuning commit. Evidence: stack shows `cast_skill → _resolve_cast → _cast_chain → damage_enemy → _damage_circle → damage_enemy → ...` for 1,000+ frames. **THE DESIGN QUESTION WAS ALREADY ANSWERED AND THE ANSWER WAS IN THE REPO.** The brief offered three rules (depth 1, bounded depth, per-frame budget) and asked for a deliberate choice. The browser original settles it: `reference/storm-dusk-v11.html:1734` reads `damageArea(e.x, e.y, 70, 20, null, { noCrit:true, silent:true, skip:e, knock:60 })` - and `noCrit` is a FIRST-CLASS OPTION passed by **every** secondary damage source in that build (crit explosion 1734, kill explosion 1863, vent 1798, fire pools 3141, kegs 923, lane cannon 5247, crew 5342). **The port dropped the flag entirely.** So *an explosion may not explode* is not a nerf invented today, it is the authored rule, and this is a PORT-FIDELITY BUG rather than a balance decision. Per-frame budget was refused on sight (frame-rate dependence is SG-122 and SG-126); bounded depth 2-3 was refused because the hazard is the BRANCHING FACTOR, not the depth - N enemies in radius gives N^d damage events - and because it would be a rule neither build has. **THE FIX** is `can_crit` threaded through `damage_enemy` and `_damage_circle`, defaulting TRUE so every existing caller is byte-identical, and `false` on the one recursive edge. Short-circuited exactly as the browser writes it, so a no-crit hit consumes nothing from the seeded stream. **THE PRECISE CLAIM, because "it cannot recurse" would be too strong:** the crit-explosion edge is now depth 1 by construction - `crit` cannot be true when `can_crit` is false, for any seed at any frame rate - but ONE re-entry survives, because an explosion can kill and `_kill_enemy` opens a `kill_explode` circle that still crits. That cycle is still walkable, but only ever ACROSS A KILL, and a dead boarder cannot die twice. Every traversal now costs one living boarder permanently, so depth is bounded by the number of figures on the deck instead of by nothing at all. That is the difference between a terminating program and a lucky one. **CHECKS: 5 new, and the two witnesses were demonstrated failing on the code before them** - with the checks applied and the fix reverted, `crit · an explosion may not explode` reported the neighbour at **hp -19.0, dead** and `crit · and the hit that opened it is still a crit` reported **hp -39.0, dead**: both boarders killed by a chain off a single 10-damage hit. The reproduction is DETERMINISTIC (crit_chance forced to 1.0, fixed HP, one call, no time stepping) and the hit points are sized so the OLD code TERMINATES - a check that stack-overflows instead of failing takes the rest of its function with it, the trap SG-146 wrote down today. Three are named regression guards, not witnesses: `crit · the explosion still reaches a neighbour at all`, `crit · a circle opened first-hand still crits, so the cap did not reach the auras`, `crit · two boarders stand close enough to pass an explosion between them`. **THE BALANCE COST IS UNRESOLVED, AND THAT IS THE HONEST ANSWER.** `tools/bot.gd:153-169` gives every non-weapon card the same rank and ties to the first offered, so she has NO OPINION about crit-explode and `critx` is rare - an unforced probe measures a population that mostly does not carry the thing being capped, and would have returned a meaningless null. `crit_explode` was forced to 1.0 in BOTH arms (`tools/critx_probe.gd`, a copy so the shared rig is not modified). Pre-committed before running: n=60/arm, expected to resolve ~10%. Measured, Heat 0, base bcb891c: **capped n=52 ttk mean 18.84 s (sd 4.20, CV 22%) against uncapped n=18 ttk mean 21.02 s (sd 3.84, CV 18%)** - a difference of -2.18 s (-10.4%, t=2.03) against a pairing that resolves **10.8%**. Below the bar, so **UNRESOLVED, not null**. **AND THE UNCAPPED ARM CANNOT BE SAMPLED CLEANLY AT ANY n**, which matters more than the delta. CORRECTED after the run finished, because the first version of this row was written from a partial log and undercounted it: six uncapped batches actually executed (S1-S6), and **three of the six failed** - **S4 and S6 BOTH died with `SCRIPT ERROR: Stack overflow` (exit 127)** and S5 ran pathologically slowly for ~20 minutes before being killed by hand. Only S1-S3 completed, which is where the n=18 above comes from. The remaining four batches (S7-S10) are **VOID, not failures**: they produced no output whatsoever, having been started while a `git worktree remove` was deleting the tree underneath them, and they are NOT evidence about the game - counting them as crashes would have been the tidier story and the wrong one. Against that, **all ten capped batches and all sixty capped runs completed**. The censoring removes exactly the longest chain-reaction runs - the ones where the uncapped build is STRONGEST - so the surviving uncapped sample is biased against itself, the likeliest reason the capped arm looks FASTER rather than slower. **Nothing was tuned to compensate.** **VERIFY:** 997/997 from a freshly extracted worktree of HEAD 7408e48 with `--headless --import` run in it once (pristine base **986/986**); `git grep "msy_"` clean (doc rule-text only). Files: `scripts/game.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`, `tools/critx_probe.gd` (measurement only). |
| SG-148 | **REVERTED 2026-08-03 by SG-159 — the owner rejected it.** *"Why are we getting rid of CRIT? That's an important mechanic... I do not want you going back and trying to refer to the browser game. We've transcended that."* All six sources crit again, the `secondary_can_crit` gate is deleted, and the twelve `nocrit ·` checks are replaced by twelve `crit · <source> can crit` ones. **The measurement below stands as a measurement and its hold-rate half did not replicate at `2bc3a48` (see SG-159); everything else in this row is history now.** Original status: DONE 2026-08-03 (fable/SG-148+149) | **The port dropped the browser's `noCrit` flag EVERYWHERE, not just on the crit explosion - six secondary damage sources crit in this build that cannot crit in the original** SG-147 restored `noCrit` on ONE call site because that one was a crash. The flag is missing from all the others, and each is a live balance divergence from the build these numbers were tuned against. In `reference/storm-dusk-v11.html` every one of these passes `noCrit:true`: the KILL explosion (1863), the VENT (1798), a fire pool's tick (3141), the KEGS (923), the LANE CANNON (5247) and the CREW's swing (5342). In `scripts/game.gd` all six run through `_damage_circle` / `damage_enemy` with `can_crit` left at its default TRUE, so each rolls `mods.crit_chance` and can double. **A crit-built captain is therefore dealing more damage than the original design in six places at once**, which also means every balance number measured on this port carries the divergence. **The browser also passes `skip:e` on both explosions** and this port does not, so a crit explosion re-hits the boarder that produced it for an extra 20 - the struck boarder takes 40+20 where the browser gives 40. **NOT bundled into SG-147 on purpose:** that was a crash fix and these are tuning changes, and a fourth change riding on a crash commit is exactly what SG-122 refused to do. Wants its own before-and-after with `tools/critx_probe.gd`. Note the kill explosion is ALSO the one surviving re-entry into the crit-explode cycle described in SG-147 - fixing it there would make the damage path acyclic outright. **ALL SIX RESTORED**, each with the browser line beside it: the kill explosion (`game.gd:3381`, v11:1863), the vent (`3756`, v11:1798), a fire pool's tick (`4124`, v11:3141), the kegs (`4078`, v11:923), the lane cannon (`3885`, v11:5247) and the crew's swing (`4392`, v11:5342). The gate is `secondary_can_crit`, shipped **FALSE**, which IS the rule. It is a var rather than a `const` for one reason and it is SG-146's lesson: a gate whose checks can only read the shipped default is vacuous. Nothing in the shipped game writes it; the harness and `tools/nocrit_probe.gd` drive both positions. **DELIBERATELY NOT COVERED, and the browser is the reason:** the player's own AURA and PULSE passives, a deployed SENTRY (v11:1964 passes `silent:true` WITHOUT `noCrit`) and the dash (v11:2459, no `noCrit`). Every check drives its own call site — `on_enemy_killed`, `vent_pressure`, `_update_fire_fields`, `explode_keg`, `_update_projectiles`, `_update_crew` — because a check that called the shared helper would pass on a build where all six sites still asked for a roll, which is precisely the bug. Deterministic: `crit_chance` forced to 1.0 makes the crit certain, so the assertion is arithmetic and there is no seed or frame rate that changes it. **THE MEASUREMENT, AND IT IS THE POINT OF THE ROW. Pre-committed before running:** n=612 per arm, primary statistic the wave-12 boss time-to-kill, pilot CV 29% giving a resolution of ~4.7%; hold rate resolving 4.0 points. Both arms are the SAME BINARY on the same seed families, one process per arm, so no two-worktree agreement is being assumed and the SG-129 run-order confound does not apply. **FORCED PAIR (crit_chance = 0.75 in BOTH arms — the ceiling `cards.gd:750` allows a drafted captain, forced because `bot.gd:153-169` gives every non-weapon card the same rank and she has NO OPINION about crit). Wave-12 duration **22.42 s against 20.99 s, +6.8%, t=5.92** against 3.1% — RESOLVED. Damage taken in wave 12 **103.1 against 93.7, +10.0%, t=2.81** against 9.6% — RESOLVED, but only just. **HOLD RATE 76.3% (467/612) against 83.5% (511/612), a gap of 7.2 points** against a resolution of 4.0 — **RESOLVED**. Four statistics came back UNDER their resolution and are recorded as **UNRESOLVED, NOT NULL**: damage by the boss (+8.6% against 10.6%), by everything else (+28.5% against 31.8%), damage per second (+5.6% against 10.3%) and Boiler HP lost (+68.6% against 113.3%). **AND THE EFFECT IS UNDERSTATED RATHER THAN OVERSTATED, in two independent ways.** The shipped arm reached wave 12 less often (527 against 550), so its wave-12 sample is drawn from runs that survived a harder game — the stronger builds. And `ttk` is CENSORED when a run ends with him alive: that happened in **57 of 527 shipped runs against 35 of 550**, and a censored ttk is truncated DOWNWARD. Both selections push the measured gap toward zero. **UNFORCED PAIR, THE SAME 612 PER ARM, AND IT IS THE OTHER HALF OF THE ANSWER.** With nothing forced — whatever the bot drafts — **EVERY STATISTIC IS UNRESOLVED**: ttk +2.3% against a 4.6% resolution, hold rate 57.8% against 58.7%, a gap of 0.8 points against a resolution of 4.0. That is not a contradiction of the forced pair, it is the SG-147 caution measured rather than asserted: the bot has no opinion about crit cards, so most of that population never carries the thing being changed, and a naive probe would have returned this pair alone and called it null. **Read together, the honest statement is: the authored rule costs a CRIT-BUILT captain about seven points of hold rate, and the population that does not draft crit cannot be shown to notice at n=612.** **BOTH ARMS SAMPLED CLEANLY AT BOTH FORCINGS, which is worth saying because SG-147's could not be.** All twelve batches ran to their report: **zero `SCRIPT ERROR`, zero stack overflows, zero killed batches, 2,448 runs total.** There is no censoring story here beyond the two named above. **NOTHING WAS TUNED TO COMPENSATE.** The game got harder for crit builds and that is a finding for the owner, not a licence to rebalance — one short entry in `NEEDS_ALEX.md`, numbers in `docs/NIGHT-LOG-2026-08-03.md` per the rule added today. **THE `skip:e` HALF IS A SECOND DIVERGENCE AND IT IS FILED AS SG-154**, deliberately not fixed here: it is at ONE site, not two, the kill explosion reproduces it by accident through `enemy.gd` setting `dead = true` before the callback, and an existing harness check pins the port's number. **VERIFY: 1011/1011 and 0 script errors** from a freshly extracted worktree of **4bfc0c7** with `--headless --import` run in it once (base at f8850da: 997/997). **AND THE CLEAN WORKTREE EARNED ITS KEEP AGAIN:** the first attempt (9d99fba) reported a GREEN **999/999** there while the live tree read 1011/1011 — rebuilding the commit from HEAD plus my own hunks, which the shared-file rule requires because another agent's work had landed in `tests/parity_test.gd`, carried the four `_nocrit*` functions and dropped the one line in `_run` that calls them. | SG-149 | DONE 2026-08-03 (fable/SG-148+149) — the dict is fixed, but the deliverable is the second half: a run that raises can no longer report green, in the harness AND in the hub | **A skill dictionary somewhere is missing `cooldown_left`, and it raises fifteen times in a clean harness run — and NOTHING IN THE HARNESS COULD EVER HAVE SEEN IT** A freshly extracted worktree of HEAD 7408e48, harness green at **986/986**, prints `SCRIPT ERROR: Invalid access to property or key 'cooldown_left' on a base object of type 'Dictionary'` **15 times**. It is PRE-EXISTING - it reproduces on untouched HEAD, not on any working change - and it fails no check, which is why nobody has seen it: the harness reports a clean pass with fifteen raised errors in the log. Dot access on a Dictionary is legal in GDScript for a key that EXISTS, so this is a skill slot that genuinely has no `cooldown_left` key. **Source not isolated and deliberately not guessed at**: the candidates are `scripts/game.gd:3347-3350` (the `kill_autofire` path, which checks `skills.size() > 0` but not that slot 0 is a drafted skill) and `scripts/hud.gd:2506-2514` (the cooldown readout, which the harness drives with EMPTY slots on every screen pass). Whichever it is, a raised error inside a `_process` can abandon the rest of that function - the same shape as the SG-146 lesson about a check that raises instead of failing - so this is worth an hour even at P3. Reproduce: `godot --path . --headless --script tests/parity_test.gd 2>&1 \\|grep -c cooldown_left`. **SOURCE, ISOLATED: it was neither candidate.** It is THREE FIXTURES IN THE HARNESS ITSELF — `tests/parity_test.gd`'s SG-126 passive-tick checks (the aura-rate loop, `pulse_carry`, `pass_carry`) each wrote a skill dictionary by hand, `{shape, element, mods, passive_timer}`, which is every field `_update_passives` reads and none of the ones it does not. Each rig is then set to `State.PLAY` **and it is in the tree**, so its REAL `_process` runs beside the check: `_update_cooldowns` (`game.gd:3038`) walked those skills, and so did the HUD's `_draw` -> `_draw_game_hud` (`hud.gd:2506`). Five rigs x one `_process` = 5, five rigs x two draws = 10. **Exactly the fifteen.** Isolated by instrumenting both sites to print `skill.keys()` rather than by guessing, which is what the row asked for. **COSMETIC FOR A PLAYER, AND THE EVIDENCE IS THAT NO SHIPPED PATH CAN BUILD ONE.** `SkyGearData.make_skill` (`game_data.gd:465`) is the only producer of a skill dictionary in the game and it always carries `cooldown_left`; the one place shipped code puts a keyless `{}` into `skills` is `_slot_skill` (`game.gd:2181`), and those placeholders are removed by `_trim_empty_slots` inside the same synchronous call with no `await` between, so no frame can ever observe them. **No cooldown is silently failing to tick for anybody playing.** **BUT THE MECHANISM IS NOT COSMETIC, WHICH IS WHY IT WAS WORTH THE HOUR.** Verified directly rather than assumed: a GDScript runtime error is NOT an exception — it prints, **it abandons the rest of the function it was raised in**, and control returns to the caller as though the call had completed (probe: a `d.missing` on line N, the line after it never printed, the caller's next line did). So in each of those five rigs `_update_cooldowns` returned at its first statement and `_draw_game_hud` stopped painting mid-HUD, silently. With one skill in the fixture nothing measurable was lost. With two, the second one's cooldown would have stopped ticking and the check would still have passed. **THE FIX IS THE FIXTURE, NOT A `.get()` DEFAULT.** `_passive_fixture()` builds through `make_skill`, so the fixture cannot drift from the schema again. `game.gd` and `hud.gd` keep their dot access ON PURPOSE and are byte-unchanged: a skill without `cooldown_left` is a bug in either direction, and a silent `.get("cooldown_left", 0.0)` would have deleted the only signal that found this — that is failure mode three, committed as a fix. **THE REAL DELIVERABLE: A RUN THAT RAISES CANNOT REPORT GREEN, AND IT IS DETECTABLE FROM THE INSIDE.** Godot exposes `Logger` and `OS.add_logger`, so the harness now installs an `ErrorWatch` in `_initialize` — before the first deferred call, so the window is the whole run — and counts by `ERROR_TYPE_SCRIPT` / `ERROR_TYPE_ERROR`. No wrapper, no log scrape, and it works when the harness is invoked directly rather than through the hub. `tools/hub.gd` carries the outer half for every OTHER checker, none of which has a `_check` to hang it on: `_launch` already holds every line the child printed, so it counts `SCRIPT ERROR` and returns that as a failure when the child exited 0. **CHECKS: 2 new, and the witness was demonstrated failing on the code before it.** `harness . the run raised no script errors` is the WITNESS — with the guard applied and the fixture fix reverted it reported **FAIL, `15 raised — first: res://scripts/hud.gd:2506 in SkyGearHUD._draw_game_hud() — Invalid access to property or key 'cooldown_left' on a base object of type 'Dictionary'`, 998/999, exit 1**. `harness . and no more engine errors than the ones already written down` is a NAMED REGRESSION GUARD, not a witness: a ratchet pinned at 56 (see SG-153), because gating those to zero today would ship a red harness and scoping the new detector down to whatever happens to pass is the failure mode this row is an instance of. It is pinned rather than dropped so the 57th has to be looked at. **THE HUB HALF WAS WITNESSED ON THE REAL HISTORICAL CONFIGURATION** rather than an injected raise: pristine HEAD's `tests/parity_test.gd` with the new `tools/hub.gd` — the child printed `997/997 checks passed` and **exited 0**, and `hub -- harness` returned **15**. With the fix applied: `999/999`, hub returns 0. Both checks are last in `_run` on purpose and neither can raise; the index into the error list is guarded by the same `is_empty()` that decides the verdict. **VERIFY:** **1011/1011 and 0 script errors** from a freshly extracted worktree of **4bfc0c7** with `--headless --import` run in it once, against a pristine base of **997/997 WITH FIFTEEN SCRIPT ERRORS AND EXIT 0** — that base number is the whole row. Engine-error count 56, stable across six runs. Engine-error count 56, stable across four runs. Files: `tests/parity_test.gd`, `tools/hub.gd`. |

These are not that mesh. Measured, in the index space the decimator actually sees: bow_ram is **22,504 components across 30,666 faces** (welded: **1**); stern 24,044; mast 8,355; even the shipped rail 2,538. About **74%** of vertices are position duplicates — and `pos+nrm` splits harder than `pos+uv`, so it is HARD-EDGE SHADING, not seams, that explodes the index buffer. A quadric decimator handed that cannot collapse an edge, so it deletes faces instead: the 3,000-triangle bow came back **a cloud of loose triangles in the shape of a boat** (`.shots/sg148/after-detail-shredded/bow_ram_t3000-y035.png`). Same family as the gunner drone's welded propeller, arrived at from the opposite direction — there the decimator merged what should have stayed apart, here it could merge nothing at all. **So the surface is welded by position FIRST and the UVs are put back afterwards** by projecting each output vertex onto the original triangles near it, with a per-face chart choice: minimising the triangle's UV perimeter is exactly the test for "keep this triangle inside one chart". **THE DRONE HAZARD THEN REAPPEARED INSIDE THE UV TRANSFER**, which is the finding worth carrying forward: a mast is a **3 cm** cylinder, so the faces nearest a point on its surface include the faces on the FAR SIDE of the pole — the other end of the UV wrap — and sampling them turned clean timber into brass-and-steel marbling **at 8,000 as readily as at 3,000**, which is what proved it was not a budget problem at all (`.shots/sg148/ladder-marbled/`). Fixed by requiring a candidate face to FACE THE SAME WAY as the vertex it feeds: the same physical fact the drone override is about, caught by orientation rather than paid for in triangles. **THE PREDICTED FAILURE DID NOT HAPPEN — THE RIGGING SURVIVES.** The brief expected shrouds and rigging lines to be the first thing decimation destroyed, and `deck_trim.py thin` agreed in advance: the mast carries **18.2%** of its faces on members thinner than 1% of its diagonal, against bow 5.9%, stern 1.8% and the shipped rail 0.2%. It survived anyway — at **3,000** all six rigging lines, the crown ring with its openings, the yard and the four hook blocks are still there (`.shots/sg148/compare/mast_crowned_t3000-y035.png`). **WHAT ACTUALLY DECIDED THE NUMBER WAS THE STERN, AND IT IS A GAP IN THE LAW.** `meshy.py`'s `tri_budget` asks for **3,000 for all four** of these — they are 141–355 px at the real camera, all below the ~380 px where the area curve starts to bite, so every one lands on its FLOOR; the shipped rail is 3,060, which is the floor + 2%. Filed as SG-151. | SG-108 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-108) — the freeze is one helper, guarded four ways, and **two of the three answers that rode on the old floor came back different; one of them contradicts what shipped** | **Every screenshot tool in this repo photographs a moving scene and calls it still** — `AnimationPlayer` ignores `set_process(false)` **THE FIX IS ONE HELPER, NOT N COPIES.** `tools/still.gd` (`SkyGearStill`) stops seven clocks: the simulation, `Engine.time_scale`, every `AnimationPlayer.speed_scale`, every `GPUParticles3D.speed_scale`, the per-frame debanding dither, volumetric fog's temporal reprojection, and the brazier flicker phase. Particles are frozen IN PLACE rather than hidden and the fog stays on, so a frozen plate is still the picture somebody is judging. Eighteen readback tools now go through it or DECLARE why they do not, in themselves, on one line — `clip.gd` photographs motion on purpose and says so; the two HUD tools say so. **A hand-written exemption list in the harness would have been the 'detector silenced' failure mode with extra steps: it lives where nobody adding a tool will read it.** **CHECKS (5 in the harness, 3 at runtime).** Harness, source-level because headless has no GPU: `still · every tool that photographs this deck says whether it freezes it` · `still · and the one freeze still stops every clock that ignores set_process` · `still · and no photographing tool keeps a private copy of it` · `still · and every frozen readback waits for the renderer, not for the tree` · `still · and the runtime half of it is in the hub where somebody will run it`. Runtime, `tools/still_probe.gd`, windowed, in the hub as `still` so `all` picks it up: **`still · two plates of a frozen scene differ by exactly zero`**, plus the same across the re-flush an A/B tool does between plates, plus the same over the whole frame. **Exactly zero, not 'under the gate' — a floor allowed to be 3% is a floor that can hide a 3% feature, which is this item's entire history.** Either half alone is a check that cannot fail: the harness cannot photograph, and a runtime probe cannot see a tool that never calls it. **THE THREE RE-MEASUREMENTS, all at floor 0.00%.** (1) **SG-101's residual is FOUND and it was this.** The marks cost a rune **+0.02%**, not the **9.1%** §7.5 records (11.5% before the halving) — and they darken **0.0%** of the planking ring even when the deepest mark this system can make is stamped directly on the wedge. **So the halving — `MARK_CAP` 48→24, `MARK_ALPHA_MAX` 0.30→0.12 — was decided on noise, and `MARK_CAP` to 12 would halve an already invisible feature again. Nothing was retuned: the row says the deciding evidence is a playtest, and that is still true.** (2) **SG-107's rig: same verdict, two wrong claims.** The lattice COSTS a rune **+0.88%** at full light and **+0.29–0.57%** at the wave-8 floor — it does not HELP, as the row says at −0.47% / −2.67% — and it darkens **9.2–9.9%** of the planking ring, not 18–22%. Well under the 3% gate either way, so the rig ships unchanged. (3) **SG-107's shadow kill-test contradicts what shipped.** The no-ordnance figure reproduces (1.91% → 1.70–2.09%). The ORDNANCE figures, which are the row's evidence for 'THE AUTHORITY SHIPS WHOLE', do not: **OFF 4.88% → 1.85–2.16%, corrected-vs-today 11.76% → 2.18–2.28%, corrected-vs-OFF 9.62% → 2.00–2.30%**, four consecutive runs. **Every one is under the item's own 3% gate in both passes, so the delete gate AND the 'only the height falloff ships' branch both fire.** The layer was NOT deleted and no code changed: SG-107's real argument was always §13c's two cases (a bolt's mark, a billboard that casts nothing), which no pixel count over FIGURES can settle. **The row's stated evidence is gone and the question is back with the owner** — NEEDS_ALEX §SG-108. **PLATES RE-SHOT** at a quiet tick into `.shots/sg107/`: the old set caught the captain mid-swing behind a fire arc, these have no effect in frame at all and the contact cores are judgeable. `shadow-ord-*` is the same three with ordnance up. Nine `immunity ·` checks, two of them structural rather than statistical: `immunity · nothing outside enemy.gd names the arrival state by its string` and `immunity · every function in enemy.gd that lowers a boarder's hp asks can_be_hit()`, parsed out of that file's own source function by function. **TWO UNCOMPENSATED EFFECTS BEYOND THE ASK, both declared:** a Colossus can no longer be BURNED through his turn (the burn tick bypassed the turn guard that `rig3d.gd` says out loud it has), and a cannon or crewman no longer aims at something it could not hurt. **AND TEN CHECKS IN THE HARNESS WERE ALREADY LEANING ON THE BUG** — eight went red, and the two that did not are the interesting ones, because `class · landing a hit grants him NO Head` asserts pressure stays at ZERO and would have stayed green for entirely the wrong reason. | **The climb telegraph has never been visible, and only half the game respects it** A spawned boarder spends `state_time = 0.8` in `state = "climb"` with velocity zeroed. `enemy.gd::_draw()` draws it a gold `#e8c376` ring — **in the 2D scene, which is hidden, so no player has ever seen it.** `view3d.gd` has no `climb` branch at all: it asks only `moving = state == "move"`, so a climbing boarder renders as a fully-lit figure standing at idle on the planking. That is the whole of what Alex noticed when he asked for arrivals. **The second half is the real bug.** Exactly two places exempt a climbing boarder — cannon targeting (`game.gd:4094`) and crew targeting (`game.gd:4232`). **Every player skill, aura, fire pool and the auto-attack already damage a boarder mid-climb.** Nobody notices today because the climber is standing on the deck; the moment SG-134 puts it in the air, a player will be killing things that are visibly not yet aboard, and the fire-pool case is now live damage since SG-117/SG-122. | SG-142 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-142) — boarders cross from the transport and land on the ring; **986/986 from a freshly extracted clean worktree of HEAD (bcb891c) with `--headless --import` run in it first, pristine base 970/970**; 16 new `arrival ·` checks, every non-guard one demonstrated red by reverting the behaviour it names; frames at `.shots/sg142/`. **AND THE DESIGN'S §9 ASSET CLAIM IS WRONG A SECOND TIME, now measured on real rigs rather than on the manifest** | **The drop — boarders still appear on the planking rather than falling onto it** Stages 1 and 2 shipped (a hull comes forward; a gold ring closes on the landing spot) and **the thing Alex actually reported — *"I also still see enemies popping in"* — is this stage.** What the design specifies and what is still true of the repo: `"jump"` into `rig3d.gd`'s `PRIORITY` and `ONE_SHOT`; `react_land()`, which is written and called from nowhere; an EXPLICIT `jump -> run` fallback for SCRAPPER rather than through `_or_idle`, because a T-pose idle sailing through the air is the bug report; GUNNER excluded because `_fly()` already owns its `position.y`; BOSS excluded. The arc's endpoint is **re-read from `enemy.global_position` every frame and never baked** and its clock is `enemy.state_time` over `SkyGearEnemy.ARRIVAL_TIME`, both of which stage 2 has already made available. **TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE DESIGN'S ASSET CLAIM, measured off `tools/models.json`:** it says jump clips are ingested for four of five kinds, and the truth is that **only SWARM has a clip literally named `jump`** — ARMORED and BOILERWRIGHT carry `jump2` and `jump_attack` but no `jump`, SCRAPPER has none (`idle, walk, run, swing, hurt`), and GUNNER has no clips at all. So the fallback is not a SCRAPPER special case; it is the common path, and `VARIANTS` will need a `jump` row the way it has a `swing` row. **AND STAGE 0 FOLDS IN HERE RATHER THAN BEING ITS OWN ROW, deliberately, against the design's build order.** §8 asks for the airborne case to be resolved inside `shadow_pose()` first — but `shadow_pose` ALREADY has that arithmetic (`SHADOW_LIFT_SPREAD`, `SHADOW_LIFT_FADE`, the `flat/down` slide off `moon_track()`); what is missing is a CALLER that passes a lift, and the enemy sync passes `0.0`. Landing the rule before the caller exists would commit a branch nothing reaches, which is STATUS's first failure mode. The two real pieces of work there: a mark with lift must stop being a `SHADOW_CORE` (`_casts_own_shadow` shrinks it to 42%/62% at exactly the moment it is the only thing saying where a boarder will land), and the rig's own cast shadow has to be suppressed in flight or the moon draws a second, wrong mark ~390 units from the ring. **BUILT, AND HERE IS WHAT IT ACTUALLY DOES.** The arc is three pure functions of `enemy.state_time` — `arrival_arc_u`, `arrival_arc_lift`, `arrival_arc_ground` — launching from `ARRIVAL_HOLD_XZ` at `ARRIVAL_DECK_Y` (the arriving hull's own deck, which stage 1 measured BELOW ours, so a boarder climbs out from under our rail rather than dropping out of the sky) and **taking the landing point as a PARAMETER re-read from `enemy.global_position` every frame**. Pinned by `arrival · and the landing point is a parameter, so a boarder shoved in mid-air bends its arc instead of teleporting at the end`, which asks for two destinations at ONE instant of one crossing and requires both to end on their own mark with `==`, not `is_equal_approx`. **THE SHAPE WAS CHOSEN FOR THE RING, NOT FOR THE ARC.** `ARRIVAL_ARC_LEAD` 2.4 front-loads the travel and `ARRIVAL_ARC_APEX_U` 0.52 puts the apex early, so **82% of the crossing is done before the fall starts** and the last third is a near-vertical drop onto a mark that is already closed — pre-committed at an 80% floor by `arrival · and it is over its own landing ring before it begins to fall — the readability pin`, **with a hard CUT branch written into the check: if it cannot be held, lower the apex or drop the arc and keep stages 1+2, never relax the number.** `ARRIVAL_ARC_APEX` is 300 against `SHADOW_LIFT_SPREAD`'s 300 and `SHADOW_LIFT_FADE`'s 220, so at the top the mark is exactly twice as wide and 2.36x fainter. **THE PER-FIGURE HASH DEPARTS FROM THE DESIGN ON PURPOSE:** §10 says hash the rounded ground position, but that hashes a value that MOVES, so a boarder knocked across a grid boundary would pop its apex mid-flight — the exact thing re-reading the endpoint exists to make smooth. It hashes the instance id, which the same loop already uses for `phase`. **STAGE 0 FOLDED IN AS PLANNED and it was two pieces, not one.** `_shadow` now demotes a lifted `SHADOW_CORE` to `SHADOW_LEANS` — a property of MARKS, so every future caller inherits it — and `_arrival_flight` suppresses the rig's own cast shadow in flight, remembering the previous per-mesh setting rather than assuming everything on a rig casts. **THE RULE NEARLY SHIPPED ONE LINE TOO WIDE:** the first draft demoted ANY mark with height, which would have thrown every shell's mark down the moonlight — projectiles are lifted TODAY, so it would have been a live bug, not a dormant one. Narrowed to `SHADOW_CORE` only and guarded by `arrival · and a projectile's mark is still never thrown down the moonlight — the RULE ONE regression guard`. **THE ASSET CLAIM, MEASURED A SECOND TIME AND ON RIGS THIS TIME.** Reverting the clip work prints the truth in one line: `SCRAPPER -> idle, ARMORED -> idle, BOSS -> idle`, with SWARM the only kind reaching a real jump. So `VARIANTS` gained a `jump` row that is **a SPELLING table, not a rotation** — `["jump", "jump2"]`, which is what gets ARMORED its real clip without a per-kind branch — and `_fallback` gained an explicit `jump -> run -> walk` arm that never reaches `_or_idle`. `jump_attack` is deliberately excluded: a boarder that cannot be touched must not be drawn winding up. `react_land()` finally has a caller. **GUNNER IS INCLUDED AFTER ALL, and only in the half that is safe.** The design excludes it because `_fly` owns its `position.y` — so it crosses horizontally and keeps its own hover height, which honours the stated reason exactly and means **no kind pops in**. Read off `ROTOR_MOTION` and `SEGMENTED` rather than a typed roster, per STATUS's seventh mode. **THE HONEST LIMIT, and it is SG-134's limit one level further on: the transport holds 3,000 units off the bow, so most of the crossing happens OFF FRAME and what a player sees is the last third — a figure appearing high near the bow and dropping onto its ring.** That is §8's own prediction (*"boarders fall in from off-screen onto marks"*) confirmed photographically rather than argued: the first cut of the shot tool sampled the window evenly and put three of five boarders outside the picture. `.shots/sg142/drop-{bow,fight}-{arc,batch,land}.png` — `arc` is five boarders on ONE mark at five instants, so a still shows the curve rather than five leaps. **NOT DONE:** no `tools/clip.gd` scenario, so the motion is judged from stills; SG-138 (every player skill damages a boarder in its climb window while the cannon and crew skip it) remains filed and unbuilt. |
