The i-frames are still CHECKED whatever the source — a hazard tick cannot land on a captain mid-dash — so the flag governs whether a tick GRANTS the window, never whether it RESPECTS it; that asymmetry is the fix. **THE SWEEP FOR OTHER PERIODIC SOURCES WAS RUN RATHER THAN ASSUMED, and there is exactly one.** `damage_player` is the sole caller of `player.take_damage` and has four call sites: the enemy melee swing (`enemy.gd:267`), the GUNNER bolt (`game.gd:3720`), the keg blast (`game.gd:3907`) — all discrete — and the fire tick. Enemy burn stacks (`enemy.gd:394-422`) are a real DoT accumulator but drain the ENEMY; the steam taps (`game.gd:3535-3550`) do have a 0.5 s `while`-accumulator but call `_damage_circle`, which iterates enemies and props and never the captain (the taps' only player interaction is `anchored()`, a damage REDUCTION). No enemy aura exists; `on_boss_turn` does not damage the player. **MEASURED DIRECTLY** (deterministic: one captain, one pool, a boarder swinging 26 every 1.5 s for 60 s, stepped at the game's own 1/60): standing IN the fire **700 -> 1440** damage taken, standing CLEAR of it **1014 both**, share of swings landing in fire **51% -> 100%**, fire's own contribution **180 -> 426**. Read rows one and two together — **before the fix, standing in the fire was 31% SAFER than standing beside it**; after, it costs 42% more. Fire went up 2.4x rather than the predicted 3x, the difference being ticks correctly absorbed by i-frames a SWING granted. **MEASURED AT RUN LEVEL TOO, AND THE HONEST ANSWER IS THAT THE RIG CANNOT RESOLVE IT** — n=120 per arm from two clean worktrees of HEAD differing only by this diff: damage taken 241.2 -> 216.7 (-10%, t=2.43), held 92% -> 90% (z=-0.45). **But two n=120 batches of IDENTICAL code differ by +8% (t=1.75)**, so the floor is the same size as the effect and the -10% is NOT reported as a finding. The reason is worth keeping: the SG-118 bot walks out of fire, so it barely exercises an exploit that was always available to a player who CHOSE to stand in a pool — a whole-run bot average was the wrong instrument, and the floor is what proves it rather than a feeling. **NO TUNING VALUE WAS CHANGED**, per the standing instruction not to hide a fix behind a compensating tune; there is also no evidence one is needed, since hold-rate moves by less than its own floor. CHECKS (5): the pre-committed `hazard · a fire tick never buys immunity to a swing` · `hazard · but a dash still dodges one — the flag grants, it does not ignore` · `hazard · a swing still opens the 0.55 s window it always did` · `hazard · a fire tick lands` · `hazard · and a fire pool now deals its authored 12 dps, not a third of it` (11.4 dps over 10 s; it must step at 1/60 rather than 0.05 — see SG-122). **ANNOTATED 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-119): that check was named for the authored 12 dps and was reading 11.4, and this row called 11.4 authored.** SG-122's discarded tick remainder rounds the period up at 1/60 as well — 1/60 divides 0.25 no more exactly in binary floating point than 0.05 does — so the pool was ~5% under its authored rate at the frame rate the game actually runs. With SG-122 fixed the same check reads **12.0**. Nothing about SG-117's own conclusions changes: the i-frame finding and the 700 -> 1440 direct measurement are independent of the tick period, and both arms of that comparison carried the identical 5% shortfall.. | SG-118 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-118) — the bot moves, its policy is a published file, and the harness drives that same object | **`tools/balance.gd` never moves its bot, and its own comment says it does** **Confirmed exactly as filed**: the loop hand-stepped `game._process(0.05)` and cast at `nearest_enemy` and issued no movement input whatsoever, under a comment claiming it *"keeps moving"*. THE FIX: the policy is now `tools/bot.gd`, a file of its own, because a policy buried in a loop is a policy nobody can test — she holds the captain's own 210-unit band (`SkyGearData.CLOSE.range`), CIRCLE-STRAFES inside it so she is never merely standing at a nice distance, closes beyond it, backs off inside 0.55x, walks out of a fire pool she is standing in (but does NOT route around one she is clear of — the honest read of "competent, not optimal", and deliberately so, since a bot that dodged fire perfectly would have made SG-117 look free), steers off a wall within 140, and dashes ONLY defensively inside 90 units (dash grants i-frames; a bot dashing on cooldown would carry a background immunity that flatters exactly the thing SG-117 measures). Input goes through the real input map at ANALOG strength — `input_direction` is a local inside `_physics_process` and cannot be assigned, the lesson `_mobility` already recorded. **TWO MORE THINGS WERE TICKING BEHIND THE RIG'S BACK, both found while verifying this one and both fixed here**: (1) `game.set_process(false)` never reached the CAPTAIN — she is her own node — so the engine stepped her on every awaited real frame with a machine-dependent delta, and `Engine.physics_ticks_per_second` is now pinned to 20 so `move_and_slide()`'s internal delta equals the loop's 0.05 (otherwise she integrates velocity on 0.05 and commits ground on 1/60 and travels at a third of her speed); (2) **nothing had ever disabled or stepped the PROPS**, so `prop.gd:_process` burned a lit keg's 0.45 s fuse on the ENGINE clock — and a keg is 26 damage inside 192 units, comparable to a whole run's damage-taken total under the old stationary bot. The old comment blaming "the crew and the cannons" was wrong on inspection: `enemy.gd` and `player.gd` are the only scripts in `scripts/` defining `_physics_process`. **THE RIG IS STILL NOT DETERMINISTIC AND THE FILE NOW SAYS SO IN PLACE OF THE OLD CLAIM** — BAL1 returned 180/170/221 over three invocations of the repaired rig; the residual is below the scene tree (the captain is a `CharacterBody2D` and `move_and_slide()` queries a physics space the server syncs on its own tick), and driving `PhysicsServer2D` by hand is a project, not a fix. So the header states plainly that this rig reports a DISTRIBUTION and that its floor is NOT zero — measured at **8% on damage-taken between two n=120 batches of identical code** — and tells the reader to disbelieve anything that does not clear it. WHAT CHANGES: a moving captain is a different game. Heat 0, n=120 — damage taken **34.6 -> 241**, runs held **38% -> 92%**, close-range time **3-5% -> ~22%**, vents **~30 -> ~60**. Every balance number in these docs taken with this rig is a number about a stationary captain. CHECKS (3): `bot · the bot actually moves the captain — the SG-118 regression` (776 units of ground in 3.0 s, integrated from VELOCITY not position, per `_mobility`'s lesson) · `bot · and she walks out of a fire pool she is standing in` · `bot · the bot's band is the captain's own gauge range` (fails if a tuning pass moves `CLOSE.range` out from under a recorded measurement). | SG-122 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-119) — fixed, and **this row's own claim that it was "harmless at 60 fps" is false and is the finding** | A fire field's tick period is frame-rate dependent — the reset discards the remainder **Confirmed as filed, and then one step worse than filed.** THE FIX: `field.tick += FIRE_TICK` instead of `field.tick = 0.25`, so the overshoot is carried into the next interval, and a `while` instead of an `if` so a step LONGER than the period delivers every tick it spans rather than one. **THE CORRECTION, which contradicts this row as originally written.** It said *"Harmless at 60 fps"* and that the pool *"delivers its authored 12 dps"* at 1/60. Measured over ten seconds of standing in a pool: **1/60 gave 11.4 dps, not 12.0.** 1/60 does not divide 0.25 in binary floating point any more than 0.05 does, so the same round-up happens at the frame rate the game actually runs at — **a fire pool has been worth ~5% less than its authored rate in every build that has ever shipped.** It follows that SG-117's recorded evidence line `hazard · and a fire pool now deals its authored 12 dps` was reading 11.4 and calling it authored; that row is annotated rather than left standing. AFTER: **12.0 dps at 1/60, 12.0 at 0.05, 12.3 at 0.1** (against 11.4 / 10.2 / 10.2 before). The 0.1 residual is the coarse-step boundary effect of the pool's first tick landing at t=0, not a rate error. CHECKS (2 new): `hazard · and it deals it at any frame rate — the tick period is not the step` (three deltas, spread must be under 0.35 dps; it reads 1.20 on the old code) · `hazard · a fire tick carries its remainder instead of throwing it away` (one 0.3 step past a fresh 0.25 interval must leave 0.20, not a reset 0.25 — stated at the accumulator so a future rewrite that hits 12 dps by other arithmetic still has to keep the remainder). Both VERIFIED TO FAIL on the old two lines (893/895). **THE SAME PATTERN IS IN THREE MORE PLACES AND IS DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED HERE — filed as SG-126.** Files: `scripts/game.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`. |
| SG-126 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-70) — all three carry their remainder now, and **the before/after says the player is NOT meaningfully stronger: the honest gain at the frame rate the game runs is ~1.5–2% on the Field aura and under 0.4% on Pulse. No card was nerfed and none needed to be** | **The same discarded-remainder tick is in three more places, and two of them are the player's own damage** SG-122 fixed the fire field. The identical `= period` reset survives at three more accumulators. (1) **The steam taps**, `_update_taps` (`game.gd:3574`): `tap.tick = 0.5` after decrementing past zero — the taps are the captain's own gauge spend, so its rate is a player-facing number. (2) and (3) **The player's passives**, `_update_passives` (`game.gd:3074` and `3078`): `skill.passive_timer = 1.0 / tick_rate` for the **Field** aura and `= cooldown` for **Pulse** — so a drafted card's authored `tick_rate` is not the rate it ticks at, by the same `ceil(period / delta) * delta` rounding, and the AURA shape's `tick_rate` of 1.8 is exactly the kind of value that will not divide a frame. NOT FIXED HERE for the reason SG-122 itself was filed rather than folded into SG-117: these move PLAYER damage output, and a change to player damage wants its own before and after rather than being the fourth balance-visible edit in one commit. Whoever takes it should measure the three periods at 1/60 first — SG-122's lesson is that the shortfall at the real frame rate is NOT zero and must not be assumed to be. **FIXED 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-70), the same way SG-122 was: the remainder is CARRIED (`+= period` against the overshoot, which is already negative) and the test is a `while` so a step longer than the period delivers every tick it spans.** The taps get a named `TAP_TICK := 0.5` and the tick's damage is `dps * TAP_TICK` rather than a second hand-written `0.5` on the adjacent line — that pair was STATUS's second failure mode (two functions disagreeing about one number) waiting to happen on the next tuning pass. The passives compute one `period` from `skill_stats` and share one carry, with a stated `PASSIVE_CATCHUP_MAX := 8` safety bound that never binds at any real frame rate and exists only so a debugger pause cannot resume into a burst; a passive shape naming no period is skipped rather than spun on. **THE MEASUREMENT, AND IT DOES NOT FLATTER THE FIX.** Ticks per second, driven through the real `_update_taps`/`_update_passives`, before → after: **steam tap** 1/60 `2.000 → 2.000`, 0.05 `1.900 → 2.000`, 0.1 `1.700 → 2.000` · **Field aura** 1/60 `1.800 → 1.850`, 0.05 `1.700 → 1.800`, 0.1 `1.700 → 1.800` (counts include the tick at t=0; the steady-state aura rate is **1.7647 → 1.800**, a **2.0%** shortfall recovered). **So at an exact 1/60 the taps lost NOTHING — 0.5 is a whole number of frames — and Pulse lost nothing either, because 4.4 s is 264 frames.** That contradicts the expectation in this row's own filing, which assumed all three would show a 1/60 shortfall like the fire pool's. **BUT THE REAL NUMBER IS NOT THE EXACT-1/60 NUMBER, and this is the part worth keeping:** all three run from `_process` (`game.gd:1629/1634/1635`), not `_physics_process`, so the delta is the RENDER frame time — variable and machine-dependent, never exactly 1/60. Under a jittering delta `ceil(period/delta)*delta` overshoots by an arbitrary fraction of a frame each interval, averaging about half a frame: **~1.5–1.7% expected shortfall on the taps and the aura at 60 fps, ~0.2% on Pulse, and roughly double all of it at 30 fps.** The player-facing statement is therefore about FAIRNESS more than power — a drafted Field card was quietly worth less on a slower machine — and the honest headline is **the correction does not make the player meaningfully stronger.** **THE WHOLE-RUN BEFORE/AFTER CONFIRMS IT BY FAILING TO SEE IT**, which is the correct outcome and is reported rather than dressed up: `tools/balance.gd -- 6 0 "" 5`, Heat 0, live tempo, n=30 per arm on the repaired rig — before **allies 13% · passives 5.0% · held 23/30 · taken 192**; after, two independent batches, **allies 13% and 14% · passives 6.6% and 5.8% · held 29/30 and 26/30 · taken 211 and 210**. **Nothing there clears the rig's own floor.** The passive share's spread between two IDENTICAL-code after-batches (6.6 vs 5.8) is half the size of the before/after difference, and the before-arm's 23/30 hold is the outlier against SG-118's own n=120 Heat 0 reference of **92%** (the after-arms pool to 55/60 = 92%, i.e. exactly the reference) — **so the tempting "held 23/30 → 29/30" reading is a low draw on the before arm and is NOT claimed here.** Damage taken moved 192 → 210 (+9%), which is at the rig's documented 8% floor and is not reported as a finding. **CHECKS (5 new, ALL VERIFIED TO FAIL on the old code — 897/902 before, 902/902 after):** `hazard · a steam main ticks at its authored rate at any frame rate — SG-126` (three deltas; reads `2.000/1.900/1.700` on the old code) · `hazard · and a steam tick carries its remainder instead of throwing it away` (0.6 past a fresh 0.5 must leave 0.4, not a reset 0.5) · `hazard · a Field aura ticks at the rate its card authored — SG-126` (reads `1.800/1.700/1.700` before) · `hazard · and a passive tick carries its remainder instead of throwing it away` · `hazard · and a Pulse carries its remainder too — its period is the cooldown, not the tick rate` — that last one exists because Pulse takes its period from a DIFFERENT field, so a rewrite keeping the carry for the aura branch and dropping it for the pulse branch would pass everything else; it reads its period back through `skill_stats` rather than hardcoding, because `cooldown` is mod-scaled on the way through (the authored 4.4 arrives as 3.52) and a hardcoded expectation would have been asserting the table instead of the code. The rates are asserted as TICK COUNTS rather than damage totals so a crit roll or an element's status cannot flatter them. Files: `scripts/game.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`. |

At 80% power this n reaches 0.049/run = 19.8% end-to-end drift; the fit says -6.9% with an interval spanning zero. **The control’s slope points the OTHER WAY**, which is what no-trend-plus-noise looks like: if run order did anything the sign would at least be stable. **WHAT IT BUYS, NARROWLY: arms run sequentially inside one `balance.gd` invocation are NOT confounded by run order at any magnitude worth acting on — the within-invocation A/B shape is sound.** It re-opens nothing (almost every ledger comparison used separate processes and was immune either way); it protects future work. It does NOT say the slope is zero: below ~0.05/run it is still unresolved, and nobody should care at that size. **THE WITHDRAWN p=0.027 IS NOW EXPLAINED AND THE WITHDRAWAL WAS CORRECT** — a post-hoc thirds split that the pre-specified regression already disagreed with (p=0.063); run clean at 4x the n it reads 0.33 and will not even hold its sign. **FIVE AMBIGUITIES IN THIS ROW WERE RESOLVED IN WRITING BEFORE THE DATA EXISTED** (what “run index” means; what “an invocation split into four processes” means; sidedness; configuration; and all four cells of the decision rule, since the row named only two). **TOOL:** new `tools/bal_drift.gd` (hub: `drift`) — pass one log for the primary, four for the control; it offers no thirds, halves or pool-combining, and **the absence of the options is the feature**. Arithmetic (`ols_slope_t`, `t_p_two_sided`, Lanczos log-gamma, Lentz incomplete beta) in `tools/bal_stat.gd`. Both figures reproduced by an independent Python implementation to six decimals. **CHECKS (3), harness 926 -> 929, AND THE HONEST LABEL ON EACH:** `bal · a planted trend across run order comes back as the slope it was planted at` and `bal · and a trend resolves as n^1.5 where a mean resolves as n^0.5, which is why 480 runs can price a slope` are **REGRESSION GUARDS** — arithmetic identities, named as such in the check strings themselves, and NOT evidence about the rig. `bal · and the p that calls a trend comes from Student’s t, not the normal shortcut that would call it significant` **HAS TEETH** and is **demonstrated by mutation**: swapping in the normal approximation gives **928/929**, that one red and both guards green (at df=10, t=2.10 is p=0.062 Student against p=0.036 normal — opposite sides of 0.05). **NO CHECK PINS THE VERDICT ITSELF, DELIBERATELY:** it is a property of 960 measured runs, not of this repository. | SG-130 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-130) — found by opus/SG-128 while measuring SG-127, filed per rule 3 | **`balance.gd` plays the Article arms with a DIFFERENT BOT than the baseline — the draft rule switches on the option count, and one Article changes the option count** The bot's draft policy is: prefer a passive if the draft offers **<=4** cards, otherwise take cell 0. THE OPENING BID's whole mechanic is that every weapon draft becomes the **32-cell matrix**. So signing that Article silently switches the bot from "drafts a passive most runs" to "never drafts a passive": measured passive share is **5.20% in the baseline arm (114 of 120 runs) and 0.00% in the opening_bid arm (0 of 120)**. The two arms therefore differ in TWO things at once — the vow, and whether the captain ever takes a passive — and the +11.7-point hold difference between them cannot be attributed to the vow. **This matters because passives are separately recorded as barely worth a slot** (~5% of damage for a drafted card), so avoiding them may be an advantage in its own right, and the arm that avoids them is the arm that won. **The comment in `balance.gd` that introduced this rule was reasoning correctly and reached the wrong place**: it says preferring a passive inside the matrix "would open every run with a Field — a fact about this bot, not about the Article", which is true. But the fix it chose swapped one bot-fact for another instead of removing one, and nothing recorded that the arms were no longer comparable. **Fifth failure mode, in the tool that exists to avoid it.** **FIX (not attempted here — it needs a decision):** either make the pick rule Article-blind (same policy both sides, e.g. always cell 0, at the cost of the passive question), or run the bid arm with a passive-preferring pick inside the matrix and report both. Until then **no opening_bid-vs-baseline comparison from this rig is interpretable**, including the one SG-26 originally recorded and the one SG-128 just measured. **DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-130). THE POLICY IS WIDTH-BLIND NOW, IT IS PINNED STRUCTURALLY RATHER THAN STATISTICALLY, AND THE COMPARISON IT VOIDED READS "UNRESOLVED AT n=120" — which is neither of the things that arm has previously been recorded as.** **THE RULE WAS NOT IN `bot.gd`.** The ticket located it there; it was inline in `balance.gd`'s draft loop. It is in `bot.gd` now as `draft_pick(options)`, a pure function of the offered CARDS that never reads `options.size()` — because the bot's policy is a published fact and a policy buried in a loop is a policy nobody can test (the SG-118 precedent, one file over). **THE POLICY, in `bot.gd`'s header in plain words:** she takes the best card offered by a fixed order `DRAFT_ORDER` — the six ACTIVE shapes in the catalogue's own order, then the two passives (Field, Pulse) LAST — ties to the first offered; an upgrade draft, which carries no shape, takes the first card at any width. **WHY PASSIVES LAST IS A PROOF AND NOT A PREFERENCE, which is the part the next reader will assume is arbitrary:** two passive shapes out of eight means any draft of three or more DISTINCT shapes always contains an active, so with passives last she takes one only when the game leaves her nothing else, and **widening the offer cannot hand her one**. Rank passives above ANY active and widening the offer makes a passive strictly more likely to be present and therefore taken — **which re-creates SG-130 in a gentler form.** So it is the only ranking that is both representative and width-invariant; "always cell 0" is width-invariant but unrepresentative, and was rejected for that. **THE LEMMA, and the most reusable thing here:** actives occupy ranks 0-5 and passives 6-7, so **`draft_pick` returns a passive IF AND ONLY IF every card offered is a passive.** An identity, not a tendency. **Any residual gap in passive rate between two arms is therefore a difference in what the GAME OFFERED, never in what she PREFERRED** — the exact opposite of the bug, where the arms held different preferences. In the header beside the policy. **THE PASSIVE PROBE:** "is a passive worth a slot?" is an explicit instrument now (`bot.passive_probe`, env `SKYGEAR_PASSIVE_PROBE`), not something a draft width switches on by accident, and the header states that a probe run may only be compared with another probe run. `balance.gd`'s passive verdict no longer answers the question silently — it says whose fact the number is. **CHECKS (6), harness 915 -> 921, AND WHICH ONES ACTUALLY BITE — demonstrated by a specific edit, not asserted.** Re-implementing the old width-branching rule as `draft_pick` turns exactly THREE red: `bot · widening a draft does not change the card she takes` (**FAIL: "AURA at 4 cards, CONE at 32"** — the bug verbatim) · `bot · and she takes the best card offered, not the first cell` · `bot · she takes a passive only when the draft leaves her nothing else` (the lemma as an assertion). **THE OTHER THREE ARE GUARDS OR CONTROLS AND ARE NOT EVIDENCE THE BUG IS FIXED, stated plainly per the precedent SG-128 set:** `bot · the draft order is the catalogue's, with the passives at the back` is a drift guard (shown going red by promoting AURA to the front of `DRAFT_ORDER`) · `bot · and an upgrade draft takes the first card at any width` passed on the old rule too · `bot · and the passive probe inverts that on purpose, and only when asked` CANNOT fail on old code, because the flag did not exist there. **THE RE-RUN, PRE-REGISTERED before the bid arm finished** — price list, reading rule and the refusal to tune all fixed in writing with the baseline complete and the bid arm at 11 of 120. Heat 3 (not the saturated Heat 0), n=120/arm, per SG-127's two warnings. **THE PRICE LIST FIRST, from `bal_stat.gd`'s own arithmetic:** n=120 resolves a held-count to +-8.9 points; from the measured 80.8% baseline it can establish **+12.2 points raw and +14.5 under the Bonferroni(6) bar.** **SG-127's recorded +11.7 points needs n=177/arm raw and n=273/arm under Bonferroni — so that effect was UNDERPOWERED AT n=120 BY CONSTRUCTION, and SG-128's own arm was too.** **THE RESULT: baseline 97/120 = 80.8% held, opening_bid 103/120 = 85.8% — +5.0 points, z=1.04, raw p=0.30.** Wave reached 11.53 -> 11.84 (+2.7%, raw p=0.017). Damage taken 169.3 -> 187.9 (**+11.0%, the bid arm takes MORE**; raw p=0.061, and this n can establish 15.6% on that metric). **ALL THREE SIT UNDER THIS SAMPLE'S RESOLUTION, SO THE ANSWER IS "UNRESOLVED AT n=120" AND EXPLICITLY NOT "NO EFFECT"** — the mistake SG-125 exists to catch. Cost of resolving: the observed +5.0 points needs **n=870/arm raw, n=1343/arm under Bonferroni (~8.2 h)**; the recorded +11.7 needs n=273/arm (~1.7 h); damage-taken +11.0% needs n=242/arm (~1.5 h). **WHAT THAT DOES TO THE RECORD.** SG-127/SG-128 recorded "+11.7 points, raw p=0.022, dies under Bonferroni, confounded by SG-130". With the confound removed it reads **+5.0 points, raw p=0.30 — it no longer clears even raw significance.** **BUT THE TWO ESTIMATES ARE NOT DISTINGUISHABLE FROM EACH OTHER** (both n=120, +-9 points), so **"the effect halved" is NOT a claim this rig can make and is not being made here.** The honest statement: the named-kit benefit is **STILL NOT ESTABLISHED, now without the confound as an excuse**, and the sign stays positive on hold-rate and wave. **The damage-taken sign FLIPPED** (SG-128 read -8.4%, this reads +11.0%) — **both readings are under resolution, so the flip is not a finding either and must not be read as one.** **NOTHING WAS TUNED: no Article, no shape, no Heat rung was touched.** **AND THESE ARE NOT SG-128's ARMS, because the bot changed.** The old bot drafted a passive in 114/120 baseline runs; this one drafts one in 37/120 — and by the lemma those 37 are exactly the runs where COLD DECK's two-card deal offered Field and Pulse and nothing else. Baseline hold rose 75.0% -> 80.8% (+5.8, inside the +-9 resolution, so the two baselines AGREE). That rise is filed as **SG-132**. **AND THE RAW CROSS-ARM PASSIVE SHARE IS NOT A VALID TEST OF THIS FIX, AND IS NOT BEING REPORTED AS ONE.** It reads baseline 30.8% against bid 0.0%, a 30.8-point gap at p<0.001 — and that is CORRECT behaviour, because a width-blind policy still draws differently from different offers (the bid matrix always contains an active; a Heat 3 two-card deal sometimes does not). **The statistic that IS comparable is how often she took a passive WHEN AN ACTIVE WAS ON THE TABLE** — the lemma proves it is zero, and `balance.gd` now counts and prints it under every batch. Measured **0 of 83 such drafts in the baseline arm and 0 of 90 in the bid arm** (30 runs/arm; an invariant, not a powered statistic). Files: `tools/{bot,balance}.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`, `docs/{BOARD,NIGHT-LOG-2026-08-03}.md`, `/NEEDS_ALEX.md`. |
| SG-116 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-116) — the mask asks the renderer now, and **the rigging's cost nearly doubled when it stopped measuring fire**; it still passes its gate | The rune mask counts any saturated red-amber pixel, so a brazier's fire and an ARMORED boarder's glowing armour are being measured as telegraph `rune_read.gd`'s mask (inherited from `rig_probe.gd`, which is where it was written) selects on `s >= 0.55, v >= 0.42, hue within 0.11 of red`. On the wave-6 pose that finds **~26,000 pixels**, and looking at `.shots/marks/marked-worst.png` a large share of them are the brazier bowls and the lit plating on an ARMORED boarder rather than any rune. **This does not change any verdict yet** — every current answer is a RELATIVE one measured over the identical pixel set in both plates, so a contaminated mask dilutes a cost toward zero rather than inventing one, and all three re-measurements passed their gates by wide margins. But it means the ABSOLUTE contrast figures (1.41–1.49 here, the 1.91 §7.5 quotes) are a median over 'red things' rather than over runes, and **the next agent to decide anything on an absolute rune number must fix this first.** The fix is a mask that reads the telegraph decal's own pixels rather than a colour window. **FIXED 2026-08-03.** **THE MASK ASKS THE RENDERER.** A telegraph is a `Decal` and `view3d.gd`'s `_decal_class()` keys them `tg`/`tr`/`tn`; `SkyGearRune.mask_of()` hides exactly those nodes on an already-frozen scene, re-photographs, and takes the pixels that CHANGED. By construction rather than by resemblance. It reuses `SkyGearStill.MOVED` as "this pixel changed" rather than inventing a second threshold, and both consumers (`rig_probe.gd`, `marks_shot.gd`) go through the one call, so the two figures measured against `COST_GATE` stay comparable. **THE CHECK THAT FAILS ON THE OLD MASK.** `tools/rune_probe.gd`, windowed, in the hub as `rune` so `all` picks it up. Pose the deck with braziers lit, vents glowing and four boarders standing, and NOTHING in windup: **the mask must select zero pixels — it does, and the retired colour window selects 16,594 on that same plate.** The before-number is measured in the probe, not quoted, because a check that passes both before and after a fix is not evidence of a fix. Also `rune · and the frame that answer is measured on really has no telegraph in it` (`decals == 0`) — the first version of the pose failed it, honestly: the boarders re-entered windup in their OWN `_process` during the await, so the "quiet" plate had two live telegraphs and the mask correctly found them. The control was wrong, not the instrument. Six harness checks (source-level, headless has no GPU): `rune · the rune mask is a diff of two plates, not a window onto a colour wheel` · `rune · and what counts as a changed pixel is still the one the freeze uses` · `rune · the telegraph prefixes the mask hides are the ones view3d actually draws` (parses `_decal_class` and compares — a fifth prefix added there fails this until the mask learns it) · `rune · nothing derives a rune from a colour window but the probe that exists to fail it` · `rune · and the runtime half of it is in the hub where somebody will run it` · `rune · and every tool that decides on the gate takes its runes from that mask`. Four runtime checks in the probe plus its own noise floor at 0.00%. **HOW WRONG THE OLD MASK WAS: 85% of what it called rune was not rune** — measured, not estimated: run the colour window on the plate with EVERY telegraph decal hidden and it still finds 16,246 of the 19,108 it found with them up. Rune pixels on the wave-6 pose **26,095 → 4,544**. **THE RE-MEASUREMENTS, WHICH ARE THE REASON THIS WAS WORTH DOING.** SG-116 reasoned that a contaminated mask DILUTES a relative cost toward zero rather than inventing one; that is correct, and it means every published rune-contrast cost was an UNDERSTATEMENT. **The rigging (SG-107 item 1): +0.88% (SG-108's figure) / +0.96% (reproduced here on the old mask) → +1.21%, +1.46%, +1.60%, +1.61% over four runs on the corrected mask.** Roughly a doubling. **It still passes the 3% gate and the kill-test does NOT fire, so nothing was cut and the gate was not touched.** The lattice now darkens **12.4–13.1%** of the true planking ring, up from 9.4–9.7% of the contaminated one — so it is a wider pass than before, not a narrower one. At the wave-8 darkness floor the corrected figure is **+0.90%, −0.54%, −0.66%, −0.72%**: it straddles zero and the lattice mildly HELPS in the dark, which is the direction SG-107 originally claimed for both lights and which only survives at one of them. **The absolute figures moved too and they moved DOWN: edge contrast 1.458 → 1.243.** §7.5's quoted 1.91 and SG-108's 1.41–1.49 were medians over fire; the honest rune-against-planking number on this pose is about **1.24**, further below `ink.gd`'s TEXT floor of 4.5 than anyone has recorded. **The deck marks: +0.02% before and +0.02% after — genuinely unchanged**, because the marks sit in both plates of the pair and the mask cancels them. But it is a coincidence pass by the tool's own criterion (0.0% ring overlap in BOTH passes, including the worst case built to force one) and that is filed as **SG-124**. Both consumers now write `SkyGearRune.save_mask()` — the pixel set each published figure is a median over, as a PNG, because SG-116 was only findable by looking at a plate and asking what the mask had selected. Limitation filed as **SG-123**: the ranged aim DASHES share one `MeshInstance3D` with blade trails and are outside the mask; `.shots/rune/windup-notele.png` shows them still in frame with every telegraph decal gone. **VERIFIED FROM A FRESHLY EXTRACTED CLEAN WORKTREE of `ede4444`**, `godot --headless --import` run in it first: harness **881/881**, `rune_probe` all checks green (0 pixels on the quiet frame against the retired window's 18,105; 86% of the old mask not rune), and `rig_probe` reproduces at **+1.30%** full light — inside the 1.21–1.61% range measured in the working tree, verdict SURVIVES. That step is not ceremony here: the SG-117/118 commit had already swept an in-progress SG-116 board row into itself because the unit git stages is the file rather than the hunk, which is what `18feea6` records. Decision recorded in `NEEDS_ALEX.md` §SG-116. Files: `tools/rune_read.gd`, `tools/rune_probe.gd` (new), `tools/rig_probe.gd`, `tools/marks_shot.gd`, `tools/hub.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`. Frames: `.shots/rune/`, `.shots/sg116/`. |
| SG-123 | **SOLVED AND VERIFIED 932/932, DELIBERATELY NOT COMMITTED 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-129)** — the tree stopped being exclusive mid-task; see `docs/NIGHT-LOG-2026-08-03.md` | **The ranged windup's aim dashes cannot be isolated from blade trails — one `MeshInstance3D` carries both**, so no telegraph mask can contain them **IT IS NOT THE RENDERER RESTRUCTURE THIS ROW FEARED.** Every ribbon is written by ONE function (`_ribbon()`) into ONE scratch buffer, flushed once by `_ribbons_end()`. The dashes do not need a second writer — **only a second FLUSH.** `_aim_dash_ribbon()` records the vertex range it wrote; `_ribbons_end()` sends those ranges to a second `MeshInstance3D` **sharing the material instance**, so appearance cannot drift. With no windup on screen `_dash_ranges` is empty and the old code path runs byte for byte. **`_ribbon()`, `RIBBON_VERTS`, `_ribbon_peak`, `_ribbon_dropped` and every existing harness assertion are UNTOUCHED** — the scoping pass expected to have to edit them and did not. `SkyGearRune.hide()` takes the new node by the same `visible` idiom as the decals, **only on frames where dashes were actually drawn**, so the `decals == 0` guard and `rune_probe.gd`'s falsifiability pose (lit brazier, no windup, mask must be EMPTY) both survive. **VERIFIED at 932/932 in a clean worktree. THREE CHECKS, NONE A GUARD:** `aimline · and the aim dashes are on a node of their own, so a telegraph mask can hide them without hiding a blade trail` · `aimline · and a blade trail with no dashes beside it still lands on the ribbon node, not the dash one` (the negative control — a split that sent EVERY ribbon to the dash node would pass the first and be SG-116 pointing the other way) · `aimline · and a swing, a volley of dashes and a second swing in one frame sort onto the two nodes correctly`. **Demonstrated to fail on the old behaviour** by mutation: disabling the range recording gives **929/932**, first and third red reading *"dash surfaces 0, ribbon surfaces 1"*, control still green. **WHY NO COMMIT:** partway through, **HEAD moved twice past my commit and `view3d.gd` filled with uncommitted deck-asset work** (`_build_cargo`, `_build_skyships`, `_sync_skyships`, wall constants) that grew ~90 lines BETWEEN TWO OF MY OWN COMMANDS, while `parity_test.gd` filled with nine red-by-design TDD checks. `git add <file>` stages the whole file, so **the fix could not be committed without committing a stranger's WIP inside it.** The change was therefore REMOVED from the working tree rather than left there, because the next `git commit -a` would have swept an unreviewed renderer change into somebody else's commit under their name. Patch preserved in the session scratchpad; the mechanism above is enough to re-apply in ~40 lines, which is the honest handoff since those files are already stale. **A `git stash` in a shared tree also swept up the owner's work and gave it back on pop — verified intact, and recorded as a hazard.** The control run it bought was worth it: it proved the 8 failures being chased were HIS red-first checks, not this change (clean HEAD extraction = **929/929**). **THE ROW DOES NOT CLOSE YET:** the size of the telegraph-cost understatement is still UNMEASURED. Direction is known and unchanged (the mask omits telegraph pixels, so every published figure is low); measuring it needs the WINDOWED GPU probes, and opening a window on the owner's machine while he works in it was not done unasked.\|Every other telegraph pixel on this deck is a `Decal` and is therefore separable by node: `view3d.gd`'s `_decal_class()` keys the melee wedge, the inner windup-clock fill, the ranged aim band and the boss turn ring as `tg`/`tr`/`tn`, and SG-116's mask hides exactly those nodes and photographs the difference. The ranged windup ALSO draws dashes through `_aim_dash_ribbon()` (`view3d.gd:4050`), and those are emitted as quads into `_ribbon_node` — **one shared `MeshInstance3D` (`view3d.gd:1835`) that also carries blade trails and bolt ribbons.** Hiding it to catch the dashes would hide things that are not telegraph, which is the same contamination SG-116 exists to remove, only pointing the other way. So the dashes sit OUTSIDE the corrected mask and `rune_read.gd` says so in its header. **Impact is believed small and is NOT measured:** the dashes are thin, they are drawn at 1.45x the band's own colour, and only the GUNNER of the four wave-6 poses draws any. But "believed small" is exactly the phrase this project has been wrong about before, and a mask that silently omits part of the thing it measures understates a cost the same way the colour window did. The fix is to split the aim dashes into their own batch in `view3d.gd` so a tool can toggle them — a renderer change, which is why this is filed rather than done inside a tools-only ticket. |

| SG-106 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-107) — and the filed diagnosis was wrong; `_banner` is innocent and so is the width | The run report's first line overflows on the results screen — a regression from tonight, and the audit's only standing failure **THE DELIMITER WAS A KEYSTROKE.** `run_report()` joins its lines with a newline and `_draw_results` splits on one, and both spelled that newline by pressing Enter inside a string literal — `"` , end of line, `"`. A literal written that way is not a newline, it is *whatever that file's line endings are*: LF in an LF file, CRLF in a CRLF one. `game.gd` was rewritten with LF while `hud.gd` stayed CRLF, the split matched nothing, and the whole eleven-line report arrived as ONE string — **1277 px asked of a 692 px column**, at all four widths. The audit was right about the pixels and had no way to say why; `contains()` is why the harness never saw it, since every character was still present and in order. **Six literals in four files** were written that way, including `hud.gd:1695`, which is the same fault live and unreported in the profiler overlay (`profiler.gd:121` joins with a proper `"\n"` escape). All six are now spelled. The bitter part: `parity_test.gd` already carried `String.chr(10)` with the comment *"this file is CRLF, and a literal newline here would be two characters that never match"* — twenty lines below the report check, in the test covering the function that broke. Now a repo-wide check. Text audit: **containment clean, 24 screens × 4 sizes, 8384 strings**, nothing shrunk to get there. Checks: `report · and it arrives as separate lines, not as one long string` · `report · and no script spells a newline by pressing Enter inside a string`. Commit `ff55ef4`. |

| SG-110 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/SG-103) — one heading rule now answers for allies and boarders alike | Crew walked BACKWARDS into the fight — a crewman was the one figure on this deck carrying no direction at all **Owner, build-44 item 1:** *"Crew members walk backwards, they should face enemies when walking."* **He was reading a literal constant.** The crew branch of `_sync_all` passed `Vector2(0, -1)` — up-deck, forever — with the comment *"crew push UP the deck, into the boarders"*, which is true of the march and of nothing else a sailor does. `_update_crew` sends a crewman at the NEAREST BOARDER IN HIS LANE wherever that boarder is: astern of him and he walks back down the deck at it, still drawn facing the bow. That is the moonwalk. It was also wrong during his bayonet stab — a boarder off his shoulder was thrust at while he faced the horizon. **THE RULE, AND IT IS THE BOARDERS' OWN:** *travelling -> face where you are going; engaged -> face what you are fighting.* Not a second rule for allies — `figure_heading()` is the boarder branch's own two-line pick, hoisted out of the loop it was inlined in, and BOTH sides of the deck now call it. Two functions disagreeing about one number is STATUS failure mode two; two figures disagreeing about which way is forward is the same fault wearing a hat. **The crewman got the boarder's two FIELDS to go with it** — `attack_direction` and `velocity`, same names, written by `_update_crew` off the `to_goal` it had already computed for its own use, defaulted in `SkyGearLanes.make_crew`. No RNG, nothing reads them back, they die with the man. **WHY THE FOUR `strafe` CLIPS STAY UNWIRED, and this is the honest part:** a strafe sells advancing while facing somewhere ELSE, and this mover never does it — `_update_crew` walks a crewman in a straight line at his goal, so while he closes his travel vector and his threat vector are THE SAME VECTOR by construction. There is nothing for a strafe to play. The captain keeps her own path (`_sync_captain` hands `place()` a separate `travel`) because she faces her cursor rather than her feet and is the only figure here that genuinely walks sideways. **Checks (4):** `figure · one heading rule answers for an ally and a boarder alike — where you are going, or what you are fighting` (pure) · `figure · a sailor sent back down the deck after a boarder TURNS ROUND to walk at him, instead of reversing into the fight` (staged against the real `_update_crew`: boarder 360 units astern, heading came back `(0.00, 118.00)`) · `figure · and once a boarder is inside his reach he stops and faces it — the bayonet goes where the man is` (boarder placed abeam, state `windup`, facing `(-1.00, 0.00)`) · `figure · and with the lane clear he faces the way he is marching, which is up the deck where it always was` (`(0.00, -118.00)`). **Evidence:** `.shots/sg103/crew-facing-before.png` / `-after.png` — the same frame 80 of `tools/clip.gd -- crew` from two clean trees; before, three sailors with their backs squarely to the camera while scrappers stand among them; after, all three turned onto what they are stabbing. Detail crop `.shots/sg103/crew-facing-detail.png`; clips `.shots/sg103/crew-before.gif` / `crew-after.gif`. | SG-113 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/SG-103) — every figure fades now, mesh and sprite, inside the budget it already had | Figures blinked out of existence — and the ones with no death clip did it mid-stride **Owner, build-44 item 10:** *"Should enemies fade away after they die instead of just vanishing?"* **Three separate disappearances were happening and only one of them had a tail.** (1) The four kinds that carry a real `die` clip (knight, crew, goblin, and the scrapper's set) played it and then SANK — SG-85's answer, and still a body CLIPPING out at the deck line. (2) **The kinds with no `die` clip — the scrapper's borrowed library has none, the gunner drone has no clips at all — were `queue_free()`d the same frame the simulation finished with them.** That is the one the owner was actually looking at: a figure ceasing to exist mid-stride. (3) The billboard tier had no tail whatsoever: a figure sprite the renderer stopped claiming was hidden and shelved on that same frame. **THE FADE IS ONE CURVE AND EVERYTHING SPENDS IT.** `corpse_fade(life)` — solid for the whole death clip, then linearly to nothing across the last **`DEATH_FADE` 0.60 s**. A rigged body spends it on every mesh's `GeometryInstance3D.transparency` (per INSTANCE, not per material, so a fading corpse cannot make every other figure sharing that mesh see-through — which matters when one crew mesh serves the whole deck); a painted one spends it on `Sprite3D.modulate.a`. One function, because a body and the sprite it falls back to disappearing at different rates is the two-numbers fault one layer down. **THE FADE STARTS BEFORE THE SINK (0.60 against 0.40) ON PURPOSE:** the body has visibly begun to leave before it starts through the floor, so the deck line stops being the moment anything happens. **AND IT COSTS THE BUDGET NOTHING WHERE THERE WAS ONE:** `corpse_life()` is untouched at 2.00 s — the tail is carved out of the window a corpse already had, because rigs are FREED rather than pooled (DESIGN §13m) and a fade that lengthened the hold would be a rig-budget change in disguise. Where there was no budget — the clipless kinds — the hold is `DEATH_FADE` alone, no window and **no sink**, because a drop invented by the renderer for a character that never authored a death is a death animation nobody wrote. New `CORPSE_CAP 16` so a wipe that kills a dozen at once cannot turn the freeing rule into a reservation, and `FADE_CAP 24` on the sprite tail for the same reason. **THE SPRITE TAIL NEEDED THE SG-66 POOL-IDENTITY CHECK TO WORK:** figures are drawn with an ALPHA SCISSOR (`_dress_billboard`), which has no opinion between 0.99 and 0.51 and then discards the whole plate — a scissored sprite cannot fade at all. So a fading sprite drops the scissor and has its KIND STAMP CLEARED, and `_claim_billboard` re-dresses any node whose stamp does not match what is being asked for. No second reset path that could forget a property. Only FIGURE sprites fade: a decal, a spark and a prop plate all eat out of the same pool, and fading everything would put a half-second ghost on every sentry ring and every collected pickup in the game. **Checks (5):** `figure · a body has begun to fade BEFORE it starts through the planking, and the tail costs the corpse budget nothing` (`fade over the last 0.60s of a 2.00s hold, sink over the last 0.40s`) · `figure · a boarder with no death clip fades out where it stood instead of ceasing to exist mid-stride` (`held 0.60s, 1 mesh(es) to fade, no sink`) · `figure · and the bodies are capped — a wipe cannot turn a freed rig into a reserved one` (`16 bodies held against a cap of 16`) · `figure · a painted figure the renderer has finished with fades out instead of blinking, and drops its alpha scissor to do it` · `figure · ...and the sprite is back on the shelf when its tail runs out — a courtesy, not a leak` (`half way out at alpha 0.50, then shelved`). **THAT `transparency` ACTUALLY RENDERS ON THESE MATERIALS IS PROVED, NOT ASSUMED** — a property that is set and silently ignored is the assert-from-memory failure, and the ingested figures carry no custom shader (the `flash` instance parameter `rig3d.gd` writes has nothing listening to it, which is how the question came up). `.shots/sg103/fade-ladder.png` is three goblins at the shipped camera at 100%, 50% and 5%, one frame, deck visible through the middle one. **Evidence:** that ladder, plus `.shots/sg103/death-before.gif` / `death-after.gif` (`tools/clip.gd -- swarm`) and `.shots/sg103/crew-before.gif` / `crew-after.gif` for the ally side. Files: `scripts/view3d.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`. |
| SG-133 | FIXED ENOUGH TO MEASURE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-131) — the pose is pinned; the residual drift is filed here and NOT fixed | **`lit_probe.gd` is frozen but not REPEATABLE — two runs of one build posed the boarders differently, and its own gate passed once and failed once on identical bytes** Found while trying to A/B a texture change against it, and filed before it was touched per rule 3. `SkyGearStill.freeze` sets `speed_scale = 0`, which stops the clock and **leaves the hands wherever they happened to be**: the probe's 90 settle frames advance `game._process` on a fixed 1/60 but they advance the TREE on `await process_frame`, which is the wall clock. **Three runs on one unmodified build gave the knight's own pixels 14066 / 14222 / 14384 and figure/deck 0.993 / 1.013 / 1.045.** That straddles the tool's own 0.95 gate AND the 1.0 that `lit · the furnace knight reads brighter than the deck he stands on` asks for, so **that check passed twice and failed once on the same bytes**, and SG-86's published 1.041 is one sample at the top of the spread rather than the value. **THIS IS NOT A HOLE IN SG-108'S FREEZE** — the 0.00% floor between two plates within ONE run is real and reproduces every time. It is the other half of the problem: what makes two RUNS comparable, which is what an A/B of an asset needs. **DONE HERE:** every `AnimationPlayer` is seeked to `POSE_T = 0.40` after the freeze, guarded by `lit · and the pose is pinned too, because a stopped clock is not a repeatable one`. **STILL OPEN:** pinning the pose did not remove all of it — the boarders' pixel count still moves about 1% run to run (99314 / 99673 / 100281), because the rigs and the camera also smooth toward their targets on the real frame delta. That residual is small enough that SG-131's effect (0.13-0.15% against 0.23-0.24%) clears it with no overlap, and too large for a tool that wants to resolve a few percent. **The fix is to drive the settle on a fixed delta instead of on `process_frame`, and it belongs with whoever next owns `tools/still.gd`.** — **2026-08-03 (opus/SG-136): THE RESIDUAL IS LOCATED, THE PRESCRIBED FIX WAS TRIED AND IT DOES NOT WORK, AND THIS ROW'S OWN SIZE FOR IT IS UNDERSTATED. STILL OPEN; the evidence and the lever that DOES work are in SG-136.** Three fresh runs off a clean extracted worktree at HEAD gave figure/deck **1.023 / 1.062 / 1.057** — a **3.8%** spread on the number the 0.95 gate reads, not the "about 1%" this row records; the 1% is true only of the whole-frame boarder pixel count (99448 / 100066 / 99923, 0.62%). **And "drive the settle on a fixed delta" is not sufficient, because the settle loop was never the only wall clock in the room.** Six scripts drive themselves: `enemy.gd` and `player.gd` on `_physics_process` (fixed delta, but a wall-clock NUMBER of ticks per frame), and `rig3d.gd`, `prop.gd`, `view3d.gd`, `game.gd` on `_process(delta)`. `rig3d.gd:682` is the "rigs also smooth toward their targets on the real frame delta" this row names and never located. Both attempts at the prescribed fix were built and measured: quieting the self-processors and hand-stepping game+view left a **5%** spread AND changed the picture (the boarders stopped walking in — figure/deck 0.856 / 0.906 / 0.897, three checks failed); walking the whole tree and hand-stepping every `_process` in tree order left **2.9%** and changed it further still. **A settle helper that stops the scene rather than stepping it deterministically is not a fix, it is a new scenario** — worth writing down, because it is the shape the next agent will reach for first. |
| SG-138 | DONE 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-138) | **The cargo runs were a PAINTING of crates projected onto the LID of a box — and this repository had already written that down and fixed the wrong half of it** **The owner, off build 53: _"I still see some 2D sprites and just some straight, simple deck layouts."_** The mechanism is exact. A Godot `Decal` projects along its own -Y; the cargo-run decals in `_build_cargo` carried NO rotation, so `cargo_wall_module.png` — a cut-out the browser draws SIDE-ON — was stamped straight DOWN onto the top face of all eight runs. At the locked 41-degree camera the top face is most of what reaches the player, so eight of the largest objects on the deck were, precisely, a painted picture of crates lying face-up on a slab, next to a small crate that is real geometry. That is the contrast in his screenshot. **THE REPOSITORY KNEW.** SG-41's note in `tests/parity_test.gd` says it in so many words — _"`_build_cargo` projects it across every cargo run's TOP face, which is most of what a 41-degree camera sees"_ — then hue-shifted the navy band it had found there and left the projection standing. Sixth failure mode: the fact was in the repo and never reached the thing it was a fact about. **FIX:** the runs are built out of the SAME `crate_stack` mesh the deck's loose crates use — **120 stacks over eight runs through ONE `MultiMesh`** (one mesh, one surface, one material, 3017 tris), so the whole ship's cargo is one draw call. Per-stack variation is a hash of the three indices, NOT `visual_rng` and not `randf()`: the deck must be byte-identical between two photographs of it (SG-108) and must not consume from the stream a seed reproduces a run out of (SG-120). Alternate stacks take a HALF TURN, because one stack repeated down a run puts its rope lashings in the same place every 44 units and reads as corrugated iron; 180° mirrors them and leaves the footprint identical, which a quarter turn would not. The box survives as a 22-unit lashed plinth, and as the FULL wall if `crate_stack` ever fails to load. **Every stack is capped at `WALL_MODULE_H`** — `_occluded` tests a sight line against that number, so cargo standing taller would hide a boarder the x-ray pass had already ruled to be in clear air. **A TRAP FOUND AND WRITTEN DOWN: headless cannot read a MultiMesh back.** `set_instance_transform` writes to the rendering server and the headless dummy renderer keeps none of it — `get_instance_transform` returns IDENTITY for every instance whatever was written (verified windowed, where the same lines read back correctly). The first version of the height guard read the batch and reported a confident **94.7 for a build whose crates are 125**: a rig that cannot see its own subject, the fifth failure mode wearing a check's clothes. It asks the placement function instead. **CHECKS (4):** `cargo · the cargo runs are built out of the deck's own crate mesh` — **run against the old renderer and failed there: 0 batches, 0 stacks**; `cargo · and no cargo module is projected onto the lid of a run` — **failed on old: 24 module decals aimed at the planking**; two guards, `cargo · and no crate stack stands taller than the height the x-ray pass tests against` and `cargo · and every crate stack stands ON the plinth rather than sunk through it` (the second exists because the readback bug hid exactly that — at this camera a half-buried crate and a short one look identical). **Evidence:** `.shots/sg138/before/` against `.shots/sg138/after/`. **NOTE — newly orphaned asset:** the painted module is no longer drawn on the runs, so `cargo_wall_module.png` and SG-41's check `cargo · and the painted cargo module carries no steel-navy band` now guard art the renderer does not read. Filed rather than left silent (failure mode one). **STILL OPEN, and it is the other half of his sentence:** "straight, simple deck layouts" is about the LAYOUT — eight parallel runs in two dead-straight columns, `SkyGearGame.CARGO_RECTS`. This row changed what they are made of, not where they are. |

They should probably fade away after being destroyed."_ **He is describing four waves of it:** `game.gd` empties `hulk` only on a run reset, so a hulk broken on wave 4 stands at the bow until wave 8 replaces it. **FIX:** the wreck fades over 2.2 s through `GeometryInstance3D.transparency` — per-instance, the SG-103 corpse mechanism, chosen because these meshes share a material with every other instance of that model and writing the material would take the whole free list with them — and its shadow fades with it. **IT DOES NOT FADE TO ZERO, and that is the honest half:** `hulk_hull()` answers for all three states ON PURPOSE (_"a wreck is exactly as solid to walk into as a sealed one"_) and `correct_player_position` pushes the captain out of it, so a wreck faded to nothing is an **invisible wall across the bow** — a worse bug than the one being fixed. The renderer cannot release that hull. `WRECK_RESIDUAL` is one constant from 0.0 the moment `game.gd` drops the hull on death; that change is NOT made here because `game.gd` is another agent's file today, and it is mirrored to `NEEDS_ALEX.md` because "does a wreck stay solid" is a design answer, not a refactor. **CHECKS (2):** `hulk · a destroyed hulk fades instead of standing at the bow` — **run against the old renderer and failed there: transparency 0.00 -> 0.00, forever**; guard `hulk · and the next hulk to break is not born a ghost`, because the destroyed node is pooled per model key and handed to the next hulk that breaks, so a faded node off the shelf would put the next wreck on the deck already transparent at full health. **Evidence:** `.shots/sg140/before/hulk-destroyed.png` against `.shots/sg140/after/hulk-destroyed.png`. |

It is kind of distracting."_ **It is the enemy's boarding craft** — the thing that grapples to the bow on a push wave and disgorges boarders until you break it (`assets/art/props/boarding_hulk_*.png` is the same object in its three states; the "small crates attached to it" are its two hinged boarding ramps). **And the answer to "is it meant to be that size" is NO, measurably.** It was drawn at a flat `420`, and 420 is a SCREEN height — SG-79's ruler says a prop covers that many ground units of FRAME at the locked camera. That says nothing about WIDTH, and width is the only dimension that matters for a thing parked across a lane. Three numbers for one object — failure mode two — and `hulk_hull()`'s own comment had already made exactly this argument for the simulation side while the renderer never heard it. **It is not cosmetic: the centre lane between the two cargo runs is 440 units across, so the drawn hull was 88 units WIDER THAN THE LANE IT SITS IN** and overhung the cargo on both sides. That is his "it blocks that lane". **AND THE HARNESS HAD BEEN PRINTING THE NUMBER ALL ALONG:** `hulk · three faces, one wall — one ruler holds the silhouette still while the door changes` reports `widths 528 / 529 / 521 gu` on every green run. It pinned the three states to EACH OTHER and nothing ever compared them to the hull or to the lane. **FIX:** the ruler is inverted — ask for the screen height that makes the drawn WIDTH equal `hulk.radius * 2`, the one number `_crew_step`, `hulk_splash` and `correct_player_position` already share; the shadow reads the same number instead of its own 300. Now **380 wide, 223 tall, 308 deep**. **PLACEMENT IS CORRECT AND UNCHANGED** — bow centre is where it grapples. The owner's suggestion of seating it on the cargo was offered on the assumption it was deck furniture; it is not, it is a vehicle you destroy, and putting it on the crates would be wrong. **If he wants it to READ bigger the lever is `SkyGearLanes.HULK.radius`, which moves the picture and the collision together** — a sim number, deliberately untouched, mirrored to `NEEDS_ALEX.md`. **CHECKS (2), both run against the old renderer and both failed there:** `hulk · the boarding hulk is drawn at the width the simulation collides with` (old: 529 against 380) and `hulk · and it fits between the cargo runs it is parked between` (old: 529 in a 440 lane). **Evidence:** `.shots/sg140/`, and `tools/hulk_shot.gd` prints the width beside the hull on every run. |
| SG-141 | DONE 2026-08-03 (fable/SG-147) — a mesh figure's silhouette is now its OWN geometry drawn through the cargo, photographed against the 120 crate meshes | **The x-ray silhouette pass has been DEAD for every figure in the game since the boarders became meshes** `_xray()` reads `_billboards.get(key)` and returns immediately if it is null. Every enemy archetype ships as a rigged mesh today, so that early return fires for **every figure on the deck, every frame**. The feature the browser turned on at v3 _"because the alternative is that a boarder walks behind a container and stops existing for two seconds — which in a game where the thing killing you is usually the one you lost track of is not an aesthetic problem"_ is OFF, and has been since the figures were ingested. **The cargo runs hide boarders today with no silhouette.** This is the general form of the complaint SG-139 fixed one instance of, and it is why SG-139 does NOT add the hulk to `_occluded`: that change was written, and then measured, and it does nothing — `_xray` would discard it for every mesh figure anyway. **Two measurements worth keeping, both now pinned by `hulk · a boarder spawns inside the hulk's own footprint, not behind it`:** a boarder spawns at y = -1115 and the hulk's footprint is y = -1154..-846, so the centre lane's boarders spawn INSIDE it (a point inside a box is un-occluded by that box — the same answer a captain standing in a cargo rect gets); and there is no deck BEYOND the hulk to hide, because the planking ends 6 units past it. What the owner saw at the bow is SCREEN COVERAGE, not occlusion, and that is what SG-140 and SG-139 answer. **RECOMMENDED SHAPE:** not a duplicated rig — `material_overlay` on the rig's existing mesh instances with `no_depth_test` and a flat unshaded tint draws the silhouette through the cargo over the same geometry, set when `_occluded` says so and cleared when it does not. Needs its own before/after and its own checks. **FIXED, and not by restoring the old behaviour - the old path assumed sprites and there are none.** `_xray` read `_billboards.get(key)` and returned when it found nothing, which it did for EVERY figure, every frame. A mesh figure has no texture to clone, so the silhouette is now **its own geometry drawn again with the depth test off**: `rig3d.gd::silhouette()` sets `material_overlay` on the rig's `MeshInstance3D`s to an UNSHADED, `no_depth_test`, alpha-blended `StandardMaterial3D` at `render_priority 8`, back faces culled so the far side of the body cannot draw over the near side. That is BETTER than the sprite version rather than equal to it: the ghost is posed by the same skeleton on the same frame, so it reads as THIS boarder mid-swing rather than a generic plate. `view3d.gd::_xray` keeps owning the WHEN (`_occluded` stays the single answer) and the rig owns the HOW. **THE TRAP WAS ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN ONE FILE OVER**, which is why it was checked: the corpse fade (SG-103) records that one crew mesh serves the whole deck, so anything set on a shared MATERIAL or MESH leaks to every figure using it. `material_overlay` is a property of the GeometryInstance3D - exactly like the `transparency` the corpse fade uses - and the material is allocated PER RIG, so a tint set for one boarder cannot reach another. **TWO LEAKS CLOSED:** the ghost is cleared on the else rather than only set on the if (a boarder stepping into the open would otherwise glow through a crate he is no longer behind), and `rig.silhouette(false)` is called where a rig leaves `_rigs` for `_corpses`, because a corpse is never handed to `_xray` again and nothing else would ever turn it off. **CHECKS: 6 new. The one that would have CAUGHT this names the premise rather than the fix** - `xray · a boarder is a mesh with no sprite, so the painted-plate silhouette could never have drawn one` - because a check that only asserts "the silhouette is on" can be satisfied by a sprite figure that no longer exists. Witnesses, both failing on the old code which sets no overlay at all: `xray · a boarder standing behind the cargo is drawn as a silhouette` (1 of 1 mesh parts) and `xray · and the silhouette draws THROUGH the crates rather than being hidden by them` (no_depth_test and unshaded read off the live material). Named guards: `xray · and it clears the moment he steps into the open`, `xray · and a corpse does not keep the ghost the boarder had`, `xray · the deck has somewhere to hide and somewhere to stand in the open`. **THE TEST POSITION IS SEARCHED FOR, NOT TYPED IN** - the check scans the deck for a spot `_occluded` itself calls hidden, so it cannot rot when a cargo rect moves (the hand-typed-roster mistake in STATUS's last failure mode). **EVIDENCE, against the 120 CRATE MESHES rather than the old decals:** `.shots/sg141/behind-cargo-{before,after}.png` and the `-crop` pair - one frozen scene, two exposures, captain at (-280, 620) and a boarder at (-280, 120) with `_occluded` true at exposure time. Before: the crate run, and no boarder anywhere in the frame. After: the boarder reads clearly through the crates. `tools/xray_shot.gd` (new) takes it, WINDOWED per SG-29 and frozen through `SkyGearStill` per SG-108. **Two lessons paid for in that tool:** a boarder left in `move` walked out from behind his own crate during the settle ticks (physics process is off for the pose now), and `SkyGearStill.freeze` runs its own zero-delta `_process` ticks which RE-ENTER `_xray` and overwrite any silhouette state set before the freeze - so the first run printed a confident line and saved two identical pictures. The ghost is set AFTER the freeze and before the readback now. **VERIFY:** 997/997 from a clean worktree of HEAD 7408e48 (base 986/986). Files: `scripts/rig3d.gd`, `scripts/view3d.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`, `tools/xray_shot.gd`. |

Map pixels are never rewritten. **EVIDENCE, read out of the shipped artifact and not asserted:** `assets/models/boss/boss_parts.scn` reports `metallic 1.0000` at the previous tip and `0.3524` after (commit `3a82cdb`); effective metallic mean 0.4947→0.1744, p95 0.7804→0.2750, peak 0.9647→0.3400. Rebuilt from the OLD `0803021335` export per the owner's decision — no Meshy credits spent — and **geometry is byte-identical: all thirteen part records unchanged, 7,996 triangles, same joints**, so the only thing that moved is the material. **THE THIRD PATH WAS REAL:** `tools/skyships.py`, which brought in all five transports, does not contain the word "metallic". **THE AUDIT — `python tools/lamplit.py audit`, and it is the check string for this row: 14 of 27 shipped models are above the ceiling on mean effective metallic and ALL 27 have `metallicFactor` UNSET.** gunner 0.7103 · scrapper 0.6355 · steam_vent 0.5576 · sword_gearblade 0.5501 · harpoon_ballista 0.5355 · salvage_pile 0.5264 · wrench_pipe 0.5125 · boiler 0.4507 · sword_cutlass 0.4357 · skyship_cutter 0.4058 · lantern_post 0.3572. **THE AUDIT REPORTS AND DOES NOT REWRITE, deliberately.** The ceiling was derived from one frame of a large dark machine that must read as a machine; whether a brass lantern post or a gearblade WANTS to be shiny is a judgement about that object and this deck's light, not an arithmetic consequence.
