Mapped to the handoff's archetypes BY LOOKING — rendered side/plan/three-quarter and matched against the owner's own concept paintings (`.shots/skyships/id/`), the method that caught the hulk's swapped state labels: **"Copper Cloud Voyager" is the SKIFF** (open timber boat, bag on struts, coiled bow hooks), **"Brass Leviathan" is the BARGE** (flat freight deck, rope-and-stanchion rail, hinged ramp, four rotors in brass rings), **"Gilded Leviathan" is the CUTTER** and is the narrowest hull delivered (0.460 across against the barge's 0.940 — a leviathan is the one thing it is not), **"The Iron Zephyr" is the HULK-TENDER**, the slowest and most patched thing in the fleet. **"The Brass Leviathan" is a SECOND barge** — deeper, three stacks, bladed rotors — ingested, budgeted and checked but NOT placed: four archetypes is what the handoff asked for and a fifth hull in the same wedge is traffic, not a fleet. One row in `SKYSHIPS` brings it back. **235 MB -> 4.9 MB**, all of it texture: every ship shipped a 4096 PNG normal map worth 31 MB on its own. **NOTHING IS DECIMATED AND THAT IS A FINDING, not a shortcut** — the delivered topology is 20,401 indexed vertices for 5,125 positions with **91.7% of its edges used by one triangle**, so a quadric decimator can only delete and at 3,500 all four hulls came back as clouds of loose brass shards (`.shots/skyships/decimated-3500-shredded-*.png`). **Moving masses cut where something should actually move**, by measurement and never by `GLTF_n`: the barge's four corner rotors (horizontal disc, thin through its axis, outboard on both plan axes) and the skiff's envelope (the highest long mass). The cutter has a segmentation twin and is deliberately NOT split — its bag is strapped tight to the hull and a strapped mass does not bob. **THE PLACEMENT IS MEASURED AND THE HONEST ANSWER IS MIXED** (`tools/skyship_probe.gd`, 140 stations x 6 real play poses x 2 zooms): the visible band is a wedge OFF THE BOW and BELOW — 63 of 140 stations are in frame from no pose at all, and every station abeam or astern is visible from at most 3 of 12. The four ships read from the bow and the port rail at zoom 1.0 and from anywhere at 1.55 (`.shots/skyships/probe/`). **From mid-deck at zoom 1.0 they are invisible — and so is everything else: the deck is 100% of that frame edge to edge** (`probe/mid-z1.00.png`), which is a property of the locked 41.25° solve and not of these stations. `SKYSHIP_BOW_HOLD` is measured and recorded for the arrival tier and nothing reads it yet — no spawning, no jumping, the sim untouched, 804/804 from a freshly extracted worktree. |
| SG-100 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/deck) — the ship has a shape now; the painted prow is retired with it | **A HULL SHAPE AROUND A RECTANGLE, and the sim never learned about it.** The owner's ask: *"can we address the deck shape and consider how to keep the playable area a rectangle, but make the visual deck look more shiplike."* **The ask contains its own answer and it is worth naming: the shape the player COLLIDES with and the shape the player SEES have never been required to agree.** `DECK_RECT` is one number in `game.gd:14`; the ship is a pile of `MeshInstance3D`s in `_build_world`. Nothing ever forced them to match — the deck was a slab because nobody had drawn anything the rectangle did not already imply. **WHAT IS DRAWN:** a **sheer strake** (20 boxes a side, inner face exactly on `\\|x\\|= 840`, top riding `sheer_lift(z)` — one static function, so DECK-IDENTITY item 4's stanchion rail can seat on it rather than retype the curve), a **bow** (an apron of the deck's OWN material carrying the planking 620 units forward of the bow line and narrowing on `hull_beam(z)`, with the strake following it in), and a **stern counter** (380 units to a 52% transom). **WHICH WAY IT ERRS, and it is by construction rather than by tuning:** toward *drawn where she cannot stand*, never *standable where nothing is drawn*. `hull_beam` returns **exactly 1.0 for every z inside the rectangle** — the taper cannot start early because the argument is clamped against the rectangle's own ends before it is used — and none of the geometry carries collision of any kind, so drawn geometry cannot stop her because there is nothing there to stop her with. **THE SIM DID NOT LEARN**, proved the SG-56 way and one step further: `hull · a bow, a stern and a bloody deck, and the sim sails today's numbers exactly` compares `_ship_snapshot()` and `rng.state` against a bare game at the same seed, and `hull · the clamp never learned the ship has a bow` runs **775 `correct_player_position` probes** on a lattice spanning 2800 x 3800 units — well outside the drawn hull — and every one lands back inside the same rectangle. **THE HARNESS CAUGHT TWO REAL GEOMETRY FAULTS THAT EYES WOULD NOT HAVE:** the naive strake, centred on each segment's MEAN half-beam and then yawed to the taper, swung its inboard corner **back across the bow line to (799, 1159)** — fixed by seating it on the tapering EDGE LINE and pushing it out along that line's own normal, which is outboard whatever the taper does; and the brass capping, being 12% wider than the timber under it, **overhung the deck by 3.6 units** because it was centred rather than grown outboard — measured at (839, 1159), and a rail cap proud of the deck is exactly the class of thing that clips a boarder's feet at the rail and is never noticed until somebody dies to it. Note the lattice is per-piece **in the piece's own frame**: an AABB test would have been wrong, because a yawed strake's bounding box overhangs a rectangle its timber does not. **TWO NUMBERS CHANGED AFTER LOOKING AT A PICTURE.** DECK-DESIGN P3 proposed a forward sheer of 150; at 150 the two bulwarks converging on the bow filled the top of the frame with a dark wall from the captain's own eye-line — **the same fault the painted `bow_prow.png` was retired for on the same afternoon, rebuilt in timber.** Cut to **96**, which also clears P3's other written trap for free (boarders spawn at `y = -1115`, 45 units inside the bow line, and P3's own safe cap was 110). And the apron drew its BACK face first — a black wedge between the bulwarks, `.shots/marks/clean-bow-z1.00.png` is the shot that said so — fixed by winding and a two-sided copy of the deck material. **THE PAINTED PROW IS RETIRED** (owner, same session: *"Get rid of this 2D sprite - its not needed anymore"*): `view3d.gd:688`'s 900-unit Sprite3D is gone and the apron is what replaces it. `bow_prow.png` **stays on disk** — the row that drew it is what went, and restoring it is a comment plus twelve lines. It never had a chance: DECK-DESIGN §1 measured 65 units of vertical headroom at the bow and the painting was 900. **DOES IT REACH THE FRAME? Honestly:** not amidships at zoom 1.0 — the deck's half-width at the captain's own depth is 490 against the deck's 840 and no hull fixes that. **But amidships is one lane of three.** `LANE_CENTERS` is `[-560, 0, 560]`, the camera's x is the focus's x (`view3d.gd:3315`), so **a captain in either side lane is 280 units from her rail** and the edge is in the near field for as long as she is there. `.shots/sky/port-z1.00.png` at HEAD is that frame with a flat gold band down it; `.shots/marks/clean-port-z1.00.png` is the same frame with a segmented bulwark and a brass cap rail. **CHECKS (6, all `hull ·`)**: `the ship has a drawn shape, and it is one node the harness can find` (146 pieces) · `the drawn deck is a superset — she is never asked to stand where nothing is drawn` · `every drawn piece of her is outboard — nothing overhangs the deck she walks` (365,328 lattice probes) · `nothing outboard can stop her — the drawn hull carries no collision at all` (147 nodes, all MeshInstance3D; 8 cargo rects, 0 fitting walls) · `the clamp never learned the ship has a bow` (775 probes) · `the sheer is one curve — peaked forward, lower aft, and flat where the fight is` (bow 96.0, midships 47.5, stern 90.0). **IT DOES NOT TOUCH THE GUNWALE BLOCK.** DECK-IDENTITY item 4 (stanchions) has NOT landed — there is no `stanchion` in `scripts/` — though an untracked `assets/models/railing_segment/` says somebody is on it. This is built BEFORE the rail and OUTBOARD of it, which is what DECK-DESIGN:373 asked for; the two compose in either order and neither reads the other's constants. The gunwale's two end caps are untouched and now read as a **breast rail** dividing the working deck from the head and the counter, which is a real ship feature and happens to draw the play boundary in brass. **VERIFY: harness 821/821 against an 804/804 baseline**, both from clean worktrees of HEAD (0ca87b2) — this row and SG-101 share the +17. Text audit windowed: CONTAINMENT clean, `0 faint · 0 small`, exit 0. Files: `scripts/view3d.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`, `docs/DECK-IDENTITY-DESIGN.md` §6. |
| SG-101 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/deck) — the system is built, capped and evicting; **its legibility number is NOT established and this row says so** | **MARKS THAT ACCUMULATE — the deck as a record of the fight.** The owner: *"Maybe consider some subtle elements to break up the deck visuals? Blood stains, scorch marks? These could appear over time to add some irregularity to the deck."* `VFX-PLAN.md` §7 had deferred exactly this, and the sentence it deferred it with became the specification: *"a persistent accumulating set needs a cap and an eviction policy, which is a system rather than an effect."* **That was right and it is not overturned — the unbounded version is still refused.** What ships is the system, cap first, because **every performance problem this project has had was an unbounded collection**: the decals before `DECAL_BUDGET`, the seventy clustered shadow `Decal`s before `SHADOW_CAP`, the ribbon pools before `_trim`. **THE NUMBERS:** `MARK_CAP 24` live plus `MARK_PENDING_CAP 8` waiting, so the batch is sized 32 and there is no path that allocates a 33rd · `MARK_HOLD 90 s` then `MARK_FADE 60 s`, so a mark lives 150 s ≈ two or three waves · `MARK_EVICT 0.60 s` · `MARK_IN 0.35 s` · `MARK_MIN_SEP 70 u` · `MARK_ALPHA_MAX 0.12` · one MultiMesh, one material, one draw, no node per mark. **EVICTION IS THE WHOLE POINT AND IT NEVER POPS:** at the cap the **faintest** live mark (ties to the oldest) is set RETIRING and its alpha driven to zero over 0.6 s; the newcomer waits in the queue and fades in when the slot clears. A mark vanishing between two frames is a pop, and a pop in the corner of the eye mid-fight is worse than no mark. **`_mark_strength` exists because of a real bug the harness found:** ranking "faintest" on DRAWN alpha meant a mark stamped this frame ranked as the faintest thing on the deck and was evicted by the next one — a deck under heavy fire would have evicted every mark it had just made and kept nothing (the check reported *"48 of 48 retiring"*). Strength now ignores the fade-in ramp: only AGE may make a mark expendable. **REPEATS DEEPEN RATHER THAN MULTIPLY** — a same-kind mark inside 70 units adds to the existing pool instead of taking a slot, which is why a lane where six boarders died is one dark pool rather than six discs, why a bleed-jet trail of fire fields collapses into a scorched run, and why the eviction path is the exception. **MARKS LAND ON EVENTS, NEVER ON A CLOCK** — blood/oil at `_corpses[key] = {…}` (the line that already fires exactly once, at the kill location, for every figure with a `die` clip; oil for the gunner drone, keyed off `model_key`), scorch on any burst of radius ≥ 150 (which separates the keg's 175 and the hulk's 260 from a kill's own `radius*2.5` ≈ 60–100, and cannot go stale the way `radius == 175.0` would), scorch once per fire-field id, scald once per Boilerwright tap. **NO VENT SCALD, deliberately:** a vent has no event — `_fill_head` polls a distance and emits nothing — so a vent scald could only be driven by a clock, which is DECK-IDENTITY item 6's own named failure, *a decoration wearing an event's clothes*. **SUBTLETY IS STRUCTURAL, not careful:** never emissive, never a ring (rings mean gameplay on this deck — SG-59's teal stand-here, a turn ring, a telegraph), refused under a cargo run or over the side, only while `game.is_playing()`, and cleared when the wave number goes BACKWARDS, which is `begin_run` on every path in. **THE MEASUREMENT, AND THE HONEST STATE OF IT.** The threshold §7.5 pre-committed — `ink.gd`'s `CONTRAST_FLOOR = 4.5` — turned out to be **the wrong yardstick, and measuring is what showed it**: 4.5 is a floor for TEXT and the SHIPPED build's rune-against-planking contrast is 1.91. A gate nothing has ever passed cannot decide anything. `tools/marks_shot.gd` (new) was built to measure the relative gate instead, and **three confounds were found inside it**, each of which had been silently changing the answer: two separate processes reach the shutter with the braziers at different points in their cycle; `view._process` keeps advancing `_flicker` between the two plates and the brass gunwale measurably brightened; and `GPUParticles3D` runs on the GPU's own clock and does not care that the tree stopped. Before those fixes the same build reported the cost as **0.72%, 2.86%, 2.97%, 6.60% and 13.55%** on consecutive attempts — a measurement of the weather. **After all three fixes it still answers non-proportionally:** halving the cap and nearly halving the alpha moved it from 11.5% to 9.1% when the painted fraction fell by more than half, and a rune median cannot move 8% when 5.7% of the deck is covered at alpha 0.12. **Whatever that residual is, it is not the marks, and it has not been found — so this ships at the conservative end and says so rather than quoting the flattering number.** Cap halved 48 → 24 (measured coverage 12.5% → **5.7%**) and alpha 0.30 → 0.12, which is the pre-committed *"the density drops"* branch taken rather than argued around. **The rig did settle one thing and it was worth the exercise: the tints were an order of magnitude too bright.** These are unshaded colours written into the HDR buffer, and an honest bleached-timber scald at `Color(0.40, 0.375, 0.330)` composited **brighter than a telegraph rune**. The calibration point is the contact-shadow batch — `Color(0.02, 0.015, 0.03)` — not a paint chip. **WHAT IS ESTABLISHED:** bench PASS at 60 boarders, `p99 7.09 / 7.89 / 12.59 ms` marked against `7.85 ms` at `MARK_CAP = 0`, **medians identical either way (6.06–6.25 ms)** and draw count unmoved (837–914 in both, the variation being decals and effects); text audit exit 0, `0 faint · 0 small`; legibility probe exit 0. **CHECKS (10, all `marks ·`)**: `the cap is a number, not a hope — five hundred events leave two dozen marks` · `at the cap the faintest is retired and fades out — nothing ever pops` · `no mark ever glows — the light on the planking is telegraphs only` · `a mark is never a ring — a ring on this deck means gameplay` · `and no mark is louder than the ceiling the design named` · `one batch, one material, one draw — and no node per mark` · `nothing is stamped under a cargo run or over the side` · `the same death in the same place deepens one pool instead of stacking twelve discs` · `marks land on events and never on a clock — thirty seconds of nothing leaves nothing` · `a new run starts on clean boards`. The ceiling check reads `_mark_alpha` rather than `MultiMesh.get_instance_color`, which reads back `(0,0,0,1)` here whatever was written — the colours render correctly (proved by forcing them red), the getter does not round-trip, and a check that trusted it would be asserting on a constant the engine invented. **ALSO FIXED IN THE TOOL:** it opened real runs and left rows in `user://runs.json`, which the harness reads — `game.log_runs = false` now, because a tool that changes what the harness measures has broken the harness. **THE OPEN LEVER IS `MARK_CAP` TO 12, and the deciding evidence is one playtest, not another rig.** Evidence: `.shots/marks/{marked,clean}-{mid,port,bow}-z*.png` and `{marked,clean}-telegraphs.png`. Files: `scripts/view3d.gd`, `tools/marks_shot.gd` (new), `tests/parity_test.gd`, `docs/DECK-IDENTITY-DESIGN.md` §7. **CORRECTED 2026-08-03 BY SG-108: THE RESIDUAL IS FOUND AND IT WAS NOT REAL.** This row says the cost 'still answers non-proportionally' at 11.5% → 9.1% and that 'whatever that residual is, it is not the marks, and it has not been found'. It was the fourth confound in the family: every rigged figure's `AnimationPlayer` advances on the engine's clock and ignores both `set_process(false)` calls, and this tool only escaped the worst of it by shooting its two plates one frame apart. **Frozen properly, the marks cost a rune +0.02% and darken 0.0% of the planking ring — even with the deepest mark this system can make stamped directly onto the strip of planking each rune is read against.** Two consequences. (1) **The halving was decided on noise.** `MARK_CAP` 48 → 24 and `MARK_ALPHA_MAX` 0.30 → 0.12 were the pre-committed 'the density drops' branch taken because the marks appeared to cost 11.5%; they cost nothing measurable, then or now. (2) **`MARK_CAP` to 12 now points the wrong way** — it would halve a feature that is already invisible in a real frame (`.shots/marks/marked-worst.png`). **NOTHING WAS RETUNED**: this row is right that the deciding evidence is one playtest and not another rig, and the rig can now only say the marks are not costing legibility, not whether they are worth seeing. The owner's call is in NEEDS_ALEX §SG-108. See also SG-115: the 11.5% and 9.1% were never reproducible from this repository in the first place. |
| SG-97 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/SG-97) | **"Furnace knights need slower more telegraphed hits, hard hitting but designed to be dodged"** (build-44 item 6). Four numbers changed as ONE design, plus the fault underneath them. **The numbers, and what each is FOR:** `windup 0.55 -> 0.90` — THE READ; 0.55s is under a human's recognise-and-commit budget with a full deck on screen, 0.90s is the Colossus's own tell so the two heavies wind at one tempo, and at 260 u/s it buys 234 units of walk against the 36 units of backward give between the trip (82) and the reach (118). `recover 0.60 -> 1.00` — THE PUNISH; a whiffed swing leaves a stationary 180-HP target for a full second, or stepping around a knight is just retreating. `damage 20 -> 34` — THE MISTAKE; three connected swings kill a full-HP captain (102 vs 100), so the bar moves on the first hit rather than the fifth. `hp 180`, `speed 75`, `reach 118`, `swing 120 deg` UNCHANGED — the reach and the swing ARE the drawn wedge, and moving them moves the tell. Cycle 1.15s -> 1.90s with the damage rising to match: **17.4 dps -> 17.9 dps against anything that cannot dodge**, so the boiler/crew/cannon pressure the wave schedule was tuned against is intact by construction, and only the player's half of the exchange changed. **THE REAL FAULT, found while measuring:** SG-3 unified the telegraph's RADIUS and left the ANGLE alone — the wedge on the deck is a 120/95/80-degree fan but the connect test was `distance_to(...) <= swing_reach`, a full circle. A player who did the thing the picture asks for, stepped around the flank, was hit by a swing that visibly went the other way. That is STATUS failure mode two in the dimension nobody checked, and it is most of why "designed to be dodged" did not describe the game. `enemy.gd::_swing_hits` now gates on the arc as well as the radius, against the target's BODY (`swing/2 + asin(body/d)`) so being clipped by the edge of the picture still hurts; enemies with no `swing` (the Colossus's phase ring) keep the circle, because there is no drawing to be honest to. Nothing static notices — `attack_direction` locks pointing at the target when the windup trips, so the boiler, a cannon and a crewman are dead centre of the fan and connect exactly as before. **Evidence:** harness **840/840**; baseline **804/804** re-measured from a freshly extracted clean worktree at 0ca87b2 — and note the trap that bit the last agent: a virgin worktree must be `--headless --import`ed once before `--script`, or the global class cache is missing and `tests/parity_test.gd` fails to PARSE rather than to pass. New checks: `telegraph · the furnace knight winds up at least as long as the Colossus`, `telegraph · and leaves a punish window longer than the read`, `telegraph · and hits hard enough that eating three is fatal`, `telegraph · a swing lands inside the wedge it drew and nowhere else`, `telegraph · a boarder that draws no wedge keeps its circle`. **THAT LAST CHECK NO LONGER EXISTS AND WAS ASSERTING A BUG (annotated 2026-08-03, opus/SG-119).** The clause above — *"enemies with no `swing` (the Colossus's phase ring) keep the circle, because there is no drawing to be honest to"* — was never true: `view3d.gd` drew the Colossus a 120° fan out of a fallback constant the whole time, so SG-97 unified the arc for three boarders and pinned a 360° hitbox in place behind the fourth. SG-119 gives BOSS the two fields, deletes both carve-outs, and INVERTS this check in place — it now reads `telegraph · and stepping behind the Colossus works, which it never did before`, with `telegraph · while standing in front of him still costs you` beside it. Everything else in this row stands. SG-3's pins hold unchanged: `telegraph · the wedge is drawn at the reach the swing connects at`, `telegraph · every melee windup has a reach and a swing arc to draw`, `telegraph · the windup gives at least the browser's warning time`. **Balance, before/after, 6 seeds, two isolated worktrees off 0ca87b2 so only this diff differs** (`tools/balance.gd -- 6`, HEAT 0, tempo live): BEFORE — 3 held, average wave 10.8, player 82% / allies 18%, damage taken mean 35 (across-run sd 8, within-run wave-sd 4.8). AFTER — 3 held, average wave 11.0, player 79% / allies 21%, damage taken mean 39 (sd 10, wave-sd 5.0). **Read that as noise, and here is why rather than letting it stand as a verdict:** balance.gd's bot never moves the captain. A stationary target is permanently dead centre of the fan and permanently inside the reach, so the longer windup and the arc gate are both INVISIBLE to it; the only thing it can feel is the bigger bite at the slower rate, which is the +4 taken. A bot that cannot dodge will report a longer telegraph as EASIER and a narrowed hitbox as identical, and neither is a statement about a human. **NEEDS_ALEX** — this is the row that most needs his hands. Files: `scripts/game_data.gd`, `scripts/enemy.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`. |
| SG-98 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/SG-98) | **"Can we make sentry abilities be toggle-able to always drop on player at their location? Maybe by holding the hotkey and that triggers a visual indicator it's on autocast"** (build-44 item 8) — built exactly as asked. **ARMED:** hold the skill's OWN hotkey for 0.45s (`SkyGearGame.AUTOCAST_HOLD`). The action is `skill_%d`, never a letter, so it works on whatever `keybinds.gd` currently says owns that slot. **DISARMED:** the same hold again — one gesture, both directions, which is the only off-switch a hold-to-toggle can honestly have. **SHOWN:** two marks on the slot, because they answer different questions at different distances — a lit ring in the element's own colour around the glyph, which you catch without looking, and the word **AUTO** in that colour on the brass key row, which says WHICH thing is on. A ring alone was the first version and read as "something about this slot is different". **BEHAVIOUR:** a sentry auto-place already existed — a ready sentry left alone for `auto_after` 2.5s drops itself at the captain's feet — so ARMED simply means the grace is zero: the slot stops waiting to be sure you were not about to press it, because you have said you never will. `manual = false`, and the ally cap and the per-slot `max_live` are untouched. **THREE THINGS IT REFUSED TO GET WRONG:** only a sentry slot watches for a hold, so every other skill keeps press-to-cast with no added latency and nothing to re-learn; a sentry slot fires on RELEASE, because a press cannot be known to be a tap until it ends and casting on press would deploy a sentry every time you armed one; and the flag lives on the skill DICT beside `sentry_idle`, not in an array keyed by slot index, which is the whole of why it survives — a wave change never touches `skills`, a pause only stops `_process`, a fresh draft replaces the whole dict (so a newly drafted sentry starts disarmed, the right default) and `_trim_empty_slots` can move a slot out from under its index. **TEACHING:** one coach line — `"HOLD {key} to arm the Sentry — it will drop at your feet on its own. HOLD it again to stand it down."` — carrying the way OUT as well as the way in, because a toggle with an undocumented off-switch is a setting. `{key}` was hardwired to `"deckwork"` in `coach.gd`; it now resolves per hint (`_key_action`), so the line names the sentry slot's own live binding. Goes quiet forever the moment one is armed, on the `find_vent` rule. **Evidence:** harness **840/840**; new checks `sentry · a fresh sentry slot is not armed`, `sentry · arming a slot is visible to anything that asks`, `sentry · an armed slot drops at the captain's feet with no grace`, `sentry · and stays armed across a wave change`, `sentry · a paused armed slot places nothing`, `sentry · and is still armed when the pause lifts`, `sentry · standing it down puts the grace back`, `sentry · the autocast hint names the slot's own binding, not a letter`, `sentry · a slot that is not a sentry cannot be armed`. Text audit windowed — 24 screens x 4 sizes, 8291 strings drawn: CONTAINMENT clean, LEGIBILITY `0 faint · 0 small`. Files: `scripts/game.gd`, `scripts/hud.gd`, `scripts/coach.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`. |
| SG-99 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/SG-99) | **"Can we get a way to change the auto-attack to another element? So it's not always fire?"** (build-44 item 9). **WHERE THE CHOICE LIVES: the title screen, on a cycling plate directly under WHO IS ABOARD, reading `THE CORE · Ember Cleave`.** The justification is cheapest-first, and every rejection is a rule the option would have broken. **A card** was cheapest to build — `cards.gd` already carries RETUNE CORE, which re-elements a slot — but a card is DEALT, and "so it's not always fire" is a request about EVERY run; an answer that arrives in some runs is not one, and it can never arrive in the first two waves, which is where the complaint was formed. **The opening weapon draft** is where the run's first choice already sits, but the draft deals SHAPES: the Cleave is deliberately absent from `DRAFT_SHAPES`, and the Opening Bid's 32 cells are 8 draftable shapes x 4 elements DERIVED BY COUNTING — putting the auto in there changes that arithmetic and puts the Cleave back on a card, breaking both rules at once. **A Workshop node** would gate a flavour choice behind scrip, making "not always fire" a thing you grind for. The title plate breaks none of them, and it is where the decision already half-lived: WHO IS ABOARD has always chosen the auto-attack's element silently (captain -> Ember Cleave, Boilerwright -> Steam Scald). This makes the second half of that decision separable and says so. **DETERMINISM:** it is a NAMED choice, not a rolled one — it consumes no RNG at all, which is the `_bid_matrix` precedent and the only reason a new pre-run choice can exist without shifting every seeded card deal, element roll, crit and spawn jitter in the game. `auto_element == ""` means "whatever the class brings", so a player who never touches the plate gets a byte-identical run to the one they got before this existed. The run report and the persisted run log now name the LIVE weapon (`auto_name()`) instead of the hardcoded `"Ember Cleave (auto)"` they printed even for the Boilerwright's Scald. **TWO PRE-EXISTING LIES FIXED IN PASSING:** the portrait's auto-attack ring was drawn at a hardcoded `0.45 * 0.8` period, a hardcoded 190 reach and a hardcoded ember `#ff9a4a` — all three now read the class's own `auto` dict, so the ring was already wrong about the Boilerwright before the element became movable. **Evidence:** harness **840/840**; new checks `auto · an untouched core is the class's own`, `auto · and naming another one costs the seeded stream nothing`, `auto · the pick reaches the swing, not just the nameplate`, `auto · the core cycles every element and comes home`, `auto · and the Cleave is still never dealt as a card` (which asserts `CLOSEHIT not in DRAFT_SHAPES` AND `DRAFT_SHAPES.size() * ELEMENTS.size() == 32` in one place, so the two can never drift). The Opening Bid's own pins hold: `shop · the bid opens the whole matrix, not a deal` (32) and `shop · and the matrix shrinks by the shape you now hold` (28). Text audit windowed: CONTAINMENT clean, `0 faint · 0 small` — the new plate and its sentence sit inside the title board, whose height is computed from its contents and was extended by one plate plus one blurb. Files: `scripts/game.gd`, `scripts/game_data.gd`, `scripts/hud.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`. |
| SG-91 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/SG-91) — the title is rebuilt and the vocabulary is written down; the owner's look-check is owed and is in NEEDS_ALEX, and SG-93 is blocked on it | **The menu is HARDWARE BOLTED TO THE SHIP now, not fourteen hairline rectangles** (P2 UI) **THE OWNER'S ASK, with a screenshot:** *"I want another agent investigating how we can get a more interesting and dynamic looking menu system. Right now the header UI element feels on-theme but the rest are just simple text boxes."* **THE SURVEY FOUND EXACTLY WHY, AND IT IS `docs/MENU-DESIGN.md`.** The SKYGEAR banner is one `draw_texture_rect_region` of painted brass carrying four things — material, a bevel, corner ironwork and weight. Everything under it was `SkyGearUI.button`, which is **two shape calls**: a flat tint and a hairline, with its whole state carried by swapping two colours and a border from 1.4px to 2.0px. Fourteen of those in a column is the visual grammar of a preferences dialog. Three more things the screenshot showed that nobody had filed: the column was **ragged** (COMPARE THE TWO at 480, everything under it at 300), **BEGIN RUN was not the door** (it differed from QUIT by six pixels of height and a hue), and the menu **floated** — it had no ground, so it sat on the planking while the header was bolted to something. **THE VOCABULARY — seven nouns, no new art**, every idiom already drawn somewhere in this build because a menu language invented from scratch would be a fifth visual language in a game that has four: the **board** (a dark iron bulkhead with a brass edge, rivets down both stiles and a bracket at each corner — the biggest change and the cheapest, it is what stops the menu floating), the **plate** (shadow, body, bevel, rim, two rivets in each end, and an engraved channel), the **lamp** (the lit state is a LIGHT, not a tint: a warm wash down the plate, brightening bevel and rivets, a deepened shadow so it reads raised, flickering on the fire fields' own `0.86 ± 0.14` clock), the **door** (BEGIN RUN — taller, teal, the banner's own corner brackets, and its lamp never goes dark), **cold iron + padlock** (locked is a material, with rivets drawn as EMPTY HOLES — the `_rivets` distinction), the **hatch** (QUIT leaves the board entirely and is made of different stuff, because a destructive control identical to SETTINGS is a misclick waiting to happen), and the **rungs** (the Heat ladder is a brass rail with rungs bolted across it — `_pipe` and `_collar` reused rather than redrawn — and the SELECTED rung is **seated**, its bevel inverted, so the pick reads by shape and not only by the tab it used to wear). **THE RULE THAT MAKES THE FLICKER SAFE, and it is mechanical rather than a promise:** the engraved channel is drawn OPAQUE over every lit layer beneath it, so the lamp cannot reach a glyph — which matters because the legibility pass renders each screen twice, once with `hide_text`, and samples the second frame under each string's box; a light that moved under a label would make those two frames disagree and the contrast figure would be noise. **CHECKS (9, all named `menu ·`)**: `the title stands on a board rather than on the planking` (512x560), `every plate on the board is one column wide` (8 plates, 1 off), `QUIT is the one control that leaves the board`, `no two plates share pixels, and none sits on the board's frame` (through `SkyGearUI.collisions` — the same function the audit and the F4 live verdict read, so it cannot drift from either), `the board is exactly as deep as the column standing on it` (foot gap 18.0 = stile 18.0), `every Heat rung is a named element, not an anonymous widget`, `a nudged Heat rung takes its own art with it` ((730,392) → (747,383)), `the lamp flickers within bounds and never goes dark` (0.723..0.998), `the engraved channel is opaque, so no light reaches the words`. **797/797 harness** (788 at baseline with concurrent work, 774 from a clean worktree of HEAD). **Text audit CLEAN at all four widths** — CONTAINMENT and LEGIBILITY both, 8259 strings, and the pass **turned a detector ON**: nothing on the title was inside any frame before, so the containment half had no opinion about the menu at all; it is measured against the board now. **AND IT FOUND A BUG, fixed here (`MENU-DESIGN.md` §7):** `_draw_heat_ladder` declared each rung as a `bare` widget and then drew with the rect from BEFORE `SkyGearUI._adjusted` — so a saved F4 offset moved the rung's click target and left the painted rung behind. Nothing caught it because the shipped `assets/hud_layout.json` has no `title` entries at all. A `bare` widget is adjusted by its caller through `_widget_adjust` now, with `{"adjusted": true}` so it is not applied twice; the five rungs also stopped keying off the `widget` fallback and have names (`heat`..`heat_5`). **ONE CHANGE WAS DRAFTED AND DROPPED:** showing THE WORKSHOP and THE BERTHS as cold-iron locked plates instead of hiding them before the first victory — a direct violation of `scripts/workshop.gd`'s first hard constraint, *"NONE OF THIS EXISTS UNTIL YOU HAVE WON. Not unlocked, not shown, not earned."* That rule is what "structurally forecloses the Rogue Legacy failure" and is worth more than a fuller title screen; recorded at `MENU-DESIGN.md` §3.5. A two-column board was also drafted and dropped (§4): `SkyGearUI` focus is a flat list in declaration order, so Up/Down across two columns steps in an order the eye cannot predict. Evidence: `.shots/sg91/compare-title-heat-1920x1080.png` and `compare-title-1600x900.png` (before beside after), plus all 24 title frames as `-before`/`-after` pairs at the four audit widths. |
| SG-92 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/tail) — filed and fixed in one session per rule 3; the bug was REAL but the ID it had been filed under was not | **THE BERTHS' foot strip OVERFLOWS: `SPARE GUN  ·  a fourth deck can…` needs 640, given 578** (P2 BUG) Reported by `tools/text_audit.gd` on EVERY windowed run at HEAD, and carried in SG-67's own evidence as "the known BERTHS overflow already recorded at SG-68" — which it was not. **SG-68 is the DONE crate-verb tabling row and has nothing to do with this screen.** A standing known-failure with the wrong ID attached is the worst version of the third failure mode: the tripwire fires, everybody recognises the sound, and the row it points at says the work is finished. It has its own row now. **AND IT WAS NEVER ONE STRING.** The audit reported ONE finding because a posed screen rests on ONE slate and the strip only speaks about the slate under the cursor. Measured by hand at the 12pt ink floor, **four of the six fittings busted the box**: bow_barricade 605, winch 614, spare_gun 640, scupper_grating 662 — against 578 given. **ROOT CAUSE: the strip split its width by two hand-picked fractions**, `strip.size.x * 0.58` to the lead and `* 0.38` to the status. A fraction cannot know that `earned · tap to berth` (100 px) wants a third of what `locked · earned by: …` (341 px) wants, so on every slate the short status hoarded room the long lead was overflowing for. **FIXED, and the fix is arithmetic:** `SkyGearHUD._share_strip` gives each column the width its own string needs AT ITS OWN FLOOR — the width below which `_fits` cannot save it — and shares the slack in proportion to what each still wants at full size. ONE function returns both widths (failure mode two), and when the two floors cannot both be paid it SPLITS what there is and leaves the overflow to be REPORTED, rather than widening a column past its own strip to quiet the detector (failure mode three). The strip's geometry came out of `_draw_berths` into `berths_page()` / `berths_strip_budget()`, so the harness measures the rectangle the screen actually draws on rather than a second copy of the sum. **THREE STRINGS TRIMMED** where arithmetic alone could not pay: bow_barricade's text and earn rule, spare_gun's text, scupper_grating's text — the SG-68 precedent, which trimmed the winch's blurb for this same strip. **CHECKS (4 new, 774→778):** `berths · every slate's foot strip fits both its sentences at the ink floor` walks the WHOLE cross-product — six fittings x every state the screen can put them in x all four audit window sizes — through the same `_share_strip` on the same `berths_strip_budget`, so a new fitting whose prose runs long fails the harness before it reaches the audit, and fails naming itself · `berths · and the tightest slate on the board still keeps room to spare` holds 40 px of headroom (worst is scupper_grating at **52 px**, against **-56** before the trims) · `berths · the strip hands its slack to the lead when both sentences fit` · `berths · and never widens a column past the strip to hide an overflow`. **PROVEN TO BITE:** restoring bow_barricade's old earn string ALONE fails the first two at 776/778, reading `lead needs 554 given 551 · status needs 409 given 407` at all four widths. **TEXT AUDIT, windowed, from a clean worktree of HEAD + this diff: 24 screens x 4 sizes, 8275 strings, CONTAINMENT CLEAN, 0 faint · 0 small.** Evidence `.shots/screens/sg87/the-berths-{1280x720,1600x900,1920x1080,2560x1080}.png` (shot before this row was renumbered off the drone's ID). Files: `scripts/{hud,fittings}.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`. |
| SG-84 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/tail) — code done; the owner's feel-check at the bow is still owed and is in NEEDS_ALEX | **The captain can walk INSIDE the boarding hulk and vanish** (P3 BUG) Filed by SG-76. `correct_player_position` clamped to `DECK_RECT` and to nothing else — harmless while the hulk was a billboard, not harmless against a 429-unit hull. **FIXED, minimally and mechanically, in the place the deck clamp already happens.** `SkyGearGame.hulk_hull()` is ONE accessor — the `barricade_rect()` / `cargo_rects()` pattern, for the one shape on this deck that is a circle — so the captain's push-out reads the SAME `hulk.radius` the crew march on (`_crew_step`) and every splash measures against (`hulk_splash`). No second copy of that number exists anywhere. It answers for ALL THREE states, because `hulk_state()` draws a mesh in all three and a wreck is exactly as solid to walk into as a sealed plate. **THE BOARDERS ARE UNTOUCHED BY CONSTRUCTION:** this is the CAPTAIN's clamp; `correct_enemy_position` has never known about the hulk and still does not — a wall around it would strand every boarder it lands. **THE BOW IS NOT AN EXIT**, which is the only non-obvious line in the fix: the hull sits at (0, -1000) with radius 190 while the deck runs to y -1143, so the deck reaches 143 units NORTH of the hull's centre and the geometrically shortest way out of the circle is sometimes off the ship. When the nearest outside will not survive the deck clamp she leaves ASTERN at her own x — the way she came in, and the only side of that circle with planking under it. **CHECKS (4 new):** `hulk · with nothing grappled on, the bow is open deck as it always was` · `hulk · the captain is stopped at the hull — walked into it from any side she ends outside it, and on the deck` (all three states x seven approaches, including the dead centre where there is no direction to leave by, and the owner's own y = -1134; it asserts BOTH that she ends outside the hull AND that she is still on the deck, because either one alone can be had by cheating — a push that ignores the rail, or a clamp that puts her back in the hold) · `hulk · and only there — every other step on the deck is handed back untouched` · `hulk · and the boarders walk out of it exactly as they always did — the hull is hers alone`. Every SG-76 grapple/state and push-wave check is still green. **PROVEN TO BITE:** with the push-out disabled the harness goes 781/782 and reproduces the report verbatim — `(0.0, -1134.0) -> (0.0, -1134.0)`. **EVIDENCE:** `.shots/sg84/inside-the-hull-before.png` (same seed, same pose, shot from a clean worktree of HEAD — the captain is not in the frame at all) against `.shots/sg84/stopped-at-the-hull.png` (she stands at the hull, whole). **THE FEEL-CHECK THE ROW ASKED FOR IS STILL OWED**, deliberately: the fix is the minimal circle, so it changes movement at the bow on every push wave. If being stopped there feels wrong the honest answers are a smaller radius or a bow-only exception — not a second collision story. Files: `scripts/game.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`. |
| SG-72 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/tail) — the railing SHIPPED on its second roll; the hatch was generated THREE TIMES and REJECTED, and the paint stays | **The purge's unfunded tail: railing segments (x4) and cargo hatches (x2)** (P3 ASSET) **140 credits of a 200 cap** (balance 1997 -> 1857; the brief's "was 267" was stale — the owner had topped up). **THE RAILING WON, on the SG-64 shape: v1 lost, the fault went into the prompt, the re-roll cleared the bar.** v1 came back STANDING ON A SOLID TIMBER BOARD, because the subject said the stanchions stood "on round bolted base flanges" while the shared `DECK` frame says "No base, plinth or ground plane" — one prompt carrying both halves of an argument, the FOURTH time this file has been bitten by exactly that (the hulk, the furnace knight, crate_small's brackets, and now this). On the deck it read as a BENCH lying at the rail. Its handrail also arrived pale bare timber against the painting's dark oiled one, and its three rods merged into a single bar — the one thing that could not be allowed, because the GAPS are the whole case for paying for this prop instead of keeping the card. v2 removes the flanges rather than negotiating with them, refuses the plinth by every name it might arrive under, says dark in both prompts, and states the empty space as a property of the object. It measures **1.898 x 0.818 x 0.288** — about 120 ground units across at `PROP_HEIGHT` 52 — stands on its own two feet, and shows planking through three separate rods, lit by the deck's own lamps where the painting carries its lighting baked in and blue-tinted. One departure recorded: at 2.3:1 it is a lower, wider rail than the painting's ~1.35:1 — nearer a low fence than a waist rail. Evidence `.shots/sg72/railing-after-v2-crop.png` against `railing-before-crop.png` (the painting) and `railing-after-crop.png` (v1's bench), same pose, same deck edge, at the real 41-degree camera. **THE HATCH LOST, and it is the first entry in `static_model.gd` to lose on its own merits rather than on budget.** v1 (1.899 x 1.507 x 1.535) came back a CUBE — the generator hears "cargo hatch" as a container with a lid, and the proportion clause lost the argument to the noun, the way crate_small's v1 heard a leather strap and a buckle as a treasure chest. v2, the honest description of a flat panel ("only a hand deep ... not a box, not a crate, nothing hollow"), **FAILED AT PREVIEW three consecutive times, uncharged, at 99%** — Meshy's preview stage will not build a near-flat plate. v3, with flatness written as buildable geometry ("four times as wide as it is thick"), came back **1.897 x 1.557 x 0.865** — a box again, standing proud of the planking with upright sides, reading as a fifth kind of small crate on a ship that already carries four (`.shots/sg72/hatch-after-v2-crop.png` against `hatch-before-crop.png`). And underneath all three, the reason it cannot win is geometric rather than a prompt that can be fixed: **a hatch cover lies FLAT IN the deck, so at the locked 41-degree camera essentially all of it that reaches the player is its own top face — which is a texture in both worlds.** That is the rope coil's standing verdict one size up, and 3D has nothing to add to it except depth the object is not supposed to have. The generated hatch is deleted, the row is out of `PROP_MODEL`, and the prompts and all three verdicts stay in `tools/meshy.py` and `tools/static_model.gd` so the decision can be revisited rather than re-discovered. **DISCIPLINE:** house palette; judged at the real camera against the painting it replaces; `camera_span` scaling (the SG-79 ruler now pins **11** rows — `prop · every wired prop stands the height of the picture it replaced, at the camera that actually looks at it   11 rows: all within a tenth` — and `prop · every wired deck prop scene keeps the statics' bargain` reads 15 scenes clean); textures shrunk the hulk way (25.17 MB -> 0.27 MB); sources archived to the gitignored `.model_originals/`; `git grep "msy_"` clean. **ONE HAZARD FOUND AND RECORDED:** `tools/static_model.gd` rebuilds the .tscn for EVERY key in its table, INCLUDING the rigged figures — running it clobbered `scrapper.tscn` and friends down to dumb static holders and took the harness to 725/729, four `figure ·` checks reporting missing parts and missing clips. Reverted; the .tscn files are HEAD's, byte for byte, and none is in this commit. That is a trap for the next person and it is in `NEEDS_ALEX.md`. Files: `tools/{meshy.py,meshy-state.json,static_model.gd}`, `scripts/view3d.gd`, `assets/models/railing_segment/`. |
| SG-17 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/tail) — scoped honestly: three of the nine dials, and the six that stay in source are named | **FX dials in the lab need a home: give the renderer a reader for effect constants** (P3 FEATURE) SG-81's shape, second use — and **THE READER CAME FIRST**. `assets/models/fx.json` is read by `scripts/view3d.gd` at launch (`load_fx`, beside `load_model_lights` in `_build_world`, before the environment that spends the first dial and long before `_build_impacts` spends the other two), and only then did the lab's SAVE start writing it. **THE SCOPE, stated rather than glossed.** Three of the lab's nine FX dials are RENDERER constants and all three are in the file: `glow` (Environment.glow_intensity), `spark` (a scale on all three `PARTICLE_BODY` figures) and `particle_life` (the impact emitters' lifetime). The other six are not constants at all and a file would be lying about them — `radius`/`arc`/`life`/`damage` are arguments the SIMULATION chooses per shape at the moment it fires, so their home is the `_fx({...})` call site in `game.gd` and they still go to the clipboard with the call they belong to; `period` and `slowmo` are the LAB's own controls (how often the preview re-fires, and `Engine.time_scale` while you watch it) and do not exist in a run at all. **AND TWO OF THE THREE WERE DIALLING NOTHING**, which is the half of this row nobody had gone looking for. GLOW wrote `glow_strength` — a property this renderer has never set, so the dial moved a number the shipped game does not carry, which is "no home" by another route. SPARK wrote `mesh.size` on `draw_pass_1 as QuadMesh`, and **SG-63 gave the impact particles real bodies** (a prism for the two made of light, a lit sphere for the puff), so that cast has returned null and the dial has moved nothing since the day the cards were retired. Building a reader for dials that reach nothing would have been the failure wearing the fix's clothes. Both are re-pointed, and SPARK is a SCALE now rather than one absolute size, which also keeps a shard long and a puff round where one number flattened them together. **ONE EXPRESSION PER DIAL:** `fx_glow` / `fx_particle_life` / `fx_particle_body` are the only places the tuning is ever spent — the builders call them, the LAB calls them, the harness walks them — so there is no second copy in `model_lab.gd` to drift (failure mode two). The lab also clamps through the renderer's own `FX_LIMITS`, so what you see is what saves and what saves is what loads. **CHECKS (6 new, 782->788):** `view · with no fx file at all the renderer runs its own shipped numbers` (an absent file is today's rendering, exactly — `FX_DEFAULT` is the literal each constant shipped with) · `view · the fx dial file round-trips through its own reader` · `view · a spoiled fx dial falls back alone, a wild one is clamped, an invented one is dropped` (per-KEY fallback, the `hud_layout`/lights rule, plus the read-time ceiling so a hand-typed 40 is the ceiling on the deck) · `view · every dial in the fx table is read by the renderer` (SG-81's twin-guard, one dial at a time) · `view · and the renderer on screen is built out of those dials, not out of literals beside them` — the other half, and driven off a tuning nothing like the defaults, because the shipped file IS the defaults and a comparison at defaults would pass against a hard-coded 1.0 just as happily · `view · the lab's save is the renderer's load — and it clamps on the way out, not just on the way in`. **BOTH SIDES PROVEN TO BITE:** restoring `node.lifetime = 1.0` fails the built-from-dials check at 787/788 reading `spark lifetime 1.00, shard lifetime 1.00, steam lifetime 1.00`; dropping the scale out of `fx_particle_body` fails the twin-guard reading `3 dials walked, dead: ["spark"]`. Files: `scripts/view3d.gd`, `tools/model_lab.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`, `assets/models/fx.json`. |
| SG-66 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/small-bugs) | **Sentry models occasionally vanish, replaced by floating colored orbs** (P2 BUG) Owner, 2026-08-02 mid-morning: "Occasionally, sentry models seem to vanish and are replaced by just floating colored orbs instead of sprites." LEAD: the SG-40 bolt cores are pooled emissive colored spheres — a pool collision between the sentry visual and the core pool (the 13e array-compaction family) would produce exactly this. Check the sentry's node lifecycle against the core/free-list claims; reproduce with a Sentry-heavy build (his Ember Sentry run surfaced it). Guard with a pool-identity check when found. **ROOT CAUSE, and it is not the core pool the lead named.** The bolt cores (`_cores`) are innocent: they live in their own dictionary with their own free list and never touch a sentry. The collision is one level down, in the `Sprite3D` pool that `_place`, `_spark`, `_draw_figure` and `_xray` ALL claim out of and ALL shelve into (`_billboards` / `_free_billboards`). Each of those four carried its own copy of the claim block, under the same comment: *"Every property below is set unconditionally, which is what makes a reused node safe to hand out: nothing carries over from whoever had it last."* That sentence was false. `_spark` installs a `material_override` — an ADDITIVE, unshaded `StandardMaterial3D` whose `albedo_texture` is the round spark blob and whose `albedo_color` is the element tint — and no other claim site ever cleared it. A `material_override` BEATS `node.texture`, so a node that had been a spark and came back through the free list rendered its next owner as a glowing coloured blob: **"sentry models seem to vanish and are replaced by just floating colored orbs"**, precisely. Nothing about the sentry was wrong. `_place("sy%d")` claimed a shelved spark — and the sentry's OWN head (`_spark("syh%d")`, drawn two lines later) is one of the things that keeps stocking that shelf, along with `fire%d`, `keg%d`, `lob%d` and the turret glows. The free list is LIFO and a Sentry slot appends a fresh `sy` key every nine seconds, which is why it read as occasional and why an Ember Sentry run is where he saw it. Three more properties carried over the same way: `region_enabled`/`region_rect` (a prop inheriting one cell of somebody's run cycle from `_draw_figure`), `flip_h` (a prop drawn backwards), and — from `_xray` — `no_depth_test` and `render_priority = 8`, which no site cleared at all, so a shelved silhouette handed to a prop drew that prop through the cargo. **FIXED:** the four claim blocks are gone. One `_dress_billboard(node, kind)` writes every property the four kinds disagree about, unconditionally, and stamps `billboard_kind` on the node; one `_claim_billboard(key, kind)` claims through it and RE-DRESSES whenever the stamp does not match what is being asked for. That stamp is the pool-identity check this row asked for: a node cannot be issued as one kind while wearing another. **CHECKS:** `view · a shelved spark comes back as a plate, not as a floating orb` (shelves a spark by hand exactly the way `_recycle` does, dirtied first with all five carry-overs, then claims it through `_place`) and `view · every deployed sentry is a ballista wearing its own head, never an orb` (stocks the free list spark-side-up, deploys a Sentry slot, and insists every `sy%d` is `art` with no override while its `syh%d` stays `spark`). **BOTH PROVEN TO BITE:** with `node.material_override = null` removed the harness goes 772/774 and the second failure reads *"2 posts of 2 sentries, 2 wearing a spark"* — the owner's bug, reproduced inside the harness. Files: `scripts/view3d.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`. |
| SG-67 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/small-bugs) | **The victory line reads "DECK HELD — " with an empty suffix** (P3 BUG) The browser writes "DECK HELD — twelve waves repelled." Owner's winning report shows the dash and nothing after it. One string, plus a check that both endings carry their suffix. **The third ending had no sentence.** Both defeats have always named themselves (`"The captain fell on wave %d."`, `"The Boiler was destroyed on wave %d."`); victory set `end_reason` nowhere, so `run_report` printed `DECK HELD — ` and the results screen's second line was blank. **FIXED:** one string, `"Twelve waves repelled. The deck is yours."` — the browser's own line, verbatim — written in `_set_state` rather than at either of the two `_set_state(State.VICTORY)` call sites, because a reason kept in two places is a reason that drifts; and only when nothing has been said already, so a caller that sets its own keeps it. `screen_poser` stopped typing its own copy (`"twelve waves repelled"`) and clears the field instead, so the posed screen shows the shipped line and the audit measures the real string. **CHECK:** `report · both endings carry their reason — neither verdict trails an empty dash` drives a real run to VICTORY and another to GAMEOVER and asserts BOTH verdict lines run past their own prefix. **PROVEN TO BITE:** with the assignment removed it fails reading `won: DECK HELD —  / lost: BOARDED — The Boiler was destroyed on wave 1.` **TEXT AUDIT** (windowed, 24 screens x 4 sizes, 8275 strings): the new line is clean at every width; the run's single finding is the known BERTHS overflow already recorded at SG-68, and is byte-identical to the same audit run against a clean worktree at HEAD. Files: `scripts/game.gd`, `scripts/screen_poser.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`. |
| SG-71 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/small-bugs) | **`_fill_head` measures the vent stand radius as an inline `150.0` while `SkyGearData.VENT_STAND` now owns that number** (P3 BUG) Found during SG-59, filed per rule 3.
