| SG-78 | DONE — fable/visual-corrections, 2026-08-02 | **Aim indicator far too intense — owner wants ONLY the small reticle locked to aim distance** (P1 BUG) Screenshot 2026-08-02 09:14: the range ring rendered as a flooded opaque disc across half the deck. His spec verbatim: "it should be subtle, way more subtle. I am more interested in the smaller aiming reticle that's locked to the aiming distance of the player, I don't think we need more than that in-game." **WHY IT DREW AS A DISC:** the ring went through `_art("ring", …)`, which prefers the painted plate `rune_player.png` over the generated rim-only `_ring_texture()` — and that plate is 68% alpha-255, a FILLED disc, so DESIGN §13e's premultiplied emission map came out filled too and lit the whole projection box at `range * 2` = 840 ground units. **SHIPPED:** `fxaim_ring` and `fxaim_out` are gone from `_sync_aim`; the reticle (`fxaim_land`) is the whole feature — hollow `_ring_texture()` directly, never the painted seam; its own blast girth clamped by `aim_mark_girth` to 30–150 ground units so no radius card can reopen the disc; 0.45 alpha and lit in range, 0.22 and unlit past it, because the dim IS the out-of-range read. The `pose_aim`/`clear_aim_pose` tool seam is untouched. **CHECKS, old → new (rewritten to the reduced contract, none silenced):** “arming a targeted skill draws the ring at skill_stats' own range” → “no ring, no disc — the reticle is the whole feature”; “a range card moves the ring, because there is only one number” → “a range card moves the clamp, because there is only one number”; “past the reach the marker clamps at the range and the glow dies” → the same, plus “and nothing else appears”; “nothing is drawn while nothing is armed”, “the read stops the frame nothing is armed”, “in range the marker sits at the cursor and glows” and “Cleave and passives arm nothing” all kept, widened to the absence of all three keys. NEW: “the reticle is small: its own blast, never the reach” and “the reticle is floored and ceilinged, so no card reopens the disc”. **EVIDENCE:** `.shots/sg78-82/aim-in-range.png` (a small lit hoop under the cursor; the deck behind it is deck) and `.shots/sg78-82/aim.png` (past the reach: clamped, dimmed, and no second shape anywhere in frame). |
| SG-79 | DONE — fable/visual-corrections, 2026-08-02 | **Some imported 3D props render too large — scale audit against intended heights** (P2 BUG) Owner, same screenshot: “some 3D objects once imported are too large”, the vent bottom-center his prime suspect. **ROOT CAUSE — the ruler was measuring the wrong thing.** `PROP_HEIGHT` is the browser's own `PROP_H`, and in the browser every prop is a BILLBOARD: camera-facing, so all of its height lands on screen as height, and it has NO DEPTH. `_sync_prop_model` scaled a generated mesh until its AABB was `PROP_HEIGHT` tall, which is a different picture: at the locked 0.72 rad camera a world-vertical edge foreshortens to cos(0.72) = 0.752, while the model's depth — which a card has none of — projects sin(0.72) = 0.659 of itself back on top. Tall thin props barely noticed; squat deep ones ballooned. **FIX:** the ruler now measures what the CAMERA sees. `SkyGearView3D.camera_span(span, yaw)` = `span.y * cos(PITCH) + toward * sin(PITCH)`, where `toward` is the footprint the yaw swings along the view axis; the full three-axis span is measured once per model key in `_claim_prop_model` (`measure_span`, the same union-of-meshes `tools/static_model.gd` writes `model_height` from), so not one generated scene had to be regenerated. One rule, no per-prop magic numbers. **AUDIT — prop / intended (gu) / before / after, as APPARENT screen height at the shipped camera:** vent 52 / 81 (1.56x) / 52 (1.00x) · crate 84 / 124 (1.47x) / 84 · keg 100 / 128 (1.28x) / 100 · brazier 116 / 133 (1.15x) / 116 · crates 148 / 148 (1.00x) / 148 · mast 340 / 339 (1.00x) / 340 · ballista 118 / 118 (1.00x) / 118 · lantern 200 / 182 (0.91x) / 200. And the two rows that are not `PROP_MODEL` but come through the same function: **turret 130 / 269 (2.07x) / 130** — the worst object on the deck, because its -90° yaw swings its 1.894-unit length straight down the view axis · salvage 62 / 97 (1.56x) / 62. **CHECK:** `prop · every wired prop stands the height of the picture it replaced, at the camera that actually looks at it` pins all ten rows within ±10% (every one lands at 1.00x) and cross-checks the measured span against the scene's own `model_height`, so a stale ruler is a fault rather than a silent disagreement; `prop · the ruler is the camera's` pins the rule itself at both ends. **EVIDENCE:** `.shots/sg78-82/props-before.png` against `.shots/sg78-82/props-after.png` — same seed, same pose, same wave. |
| SG-80 | DONE 2026-08-02 (fable/SG-80) | **F4: resize text boxes — width/height, not just position** (P2 FEATURE) **A selected element now has a SIZE as well as a position, and both are edited the same three ways.** The owner's ask verbatim: "With the F4 alignment tool - can we add the ability to reduce or increase the width of text boxes, not just nudge, align them." **THE INTERACTIONS.** A selected element grows brass RESIZE GRIPS on the edges it can actually move — the east grip only for a single-line string, because its height is its point size and that number belongs to `ink.gd`; east, south and the corner for a wrapped block, a widget or a mark. Drag one and it resizes; **Shift during a resize locks it to one dimension**, routed through SG-58's own `DragLock` session rather than a second implementation, so the resize gesture and the move gesture are one thing to learn. The **w×h readout** beside the offset readout is the SG-39/SG-42 typed widget, other pair: click it, type "900, 20", Enter applies, Esc cancels, a malformed pair is REFUSED and the old size kept (one parser, `parse_offset`). **Ctrl+arrows** resize by the SG-39 steps (Shift ×10, Alt ×0.1) — the keyboard half of the grips, built rather than skipped. The selection also draws its FIELD — the box its width defines, which for a short string in a long box is wider than the glyphs and was invisible until now, which is why "this string escapes a box I can see it sitting inside" used to be unanswerable. **THE SCHEMA — the same family, and old files load byte-identically.** A screen entry is now one of two shapes: `[dx, dy]` (SG-42's original) or `{"o": [dx,dy], "s": [dw,dh]}` with either half dropped when it is zero. An offset-only edit still WRITES the bare pair, so a file that predates sizes and a file that never resizes anything are the same file; nothing migrates. Both halves are HOME-RELATIVE — a delta from the size the drawing code chose, so a reflow carries the intent instead of pinning stale pixels. Per-key fallback goes one level finer than before: a malformed size costs the size and leaves the offset beside it standing. Zero deltas are erased. `layout_promote` carries and re-sanitises both shapes and now counts the resized elements in its report. **FLOORS, AND THE VERDICT.** A text box floors at one `SkyGearInk.MIN_PT` glyph — read from the file that owns the number, not copied — and a widget or mark at the 8 px item floor; plates keep 40×28. Deliberately NOT floored at the content width: narrowing a box until its words no longer fit is a legal edit whose consequence the LIVE VERDICT states out loud the same frame, through the audit's own OVERFLOW/OUTSIDE detectors reading the RESIZED bounds. A floor set at the content would have made that verdict unreachable — the detector-silenced-to-pass failure by another route. Ctrl+Z covers a resize; Ctrl+R clears size with position (one entry, one erase). **CHECKS (12 new, 690→702):** `layout · screen size deltas survive a save and a load` · `layout · an entry older than sizes loads unchanged, and still saves as a pair` · `layout · a malformed size entry falls back alone` · `layout · a zero size delta is erased and leaves the offset a bare pair` · `layout · a floor refuses a resize below the ink minimum` · `layout · undo restores the pre-resize size` · `editor · a saved size delta narrows the text box by exactly that much` · `editor · and the selection carries a resize handle on the edge it can move` · `editor · the size delta is measured from the home size — reflow and it follows` · `editor · a resize past the floor is refused, not obeyed` · `editor · and a box narrowed past its own words fires the live verdict` · `editor · the screen is clean again once the delta is erased`. **VERIFY:** harness **705/705 from a clean worktree of HEAD (cd79335) + this diff** (690 baseline + 12 here + 3 for SG-83 below); text audit WINDOWED from that worktree with zero saved deltas — 24 screens × 4 sizes, 8275 strings, CONTAINMENT clean, 0 faint · 0 small; windowed smoke through the real input pipeline (warped cursor + pushed events, no method calls): F4 on the title → two clicks select `skygear` (one east grip, because a single-line string has no editable height) → drag the east grip **−240 px** → Shift-drag locks to width (**+39.75**, the Y travel dropped) → Ctrl+arrows **+2** → the w×h box prefills **1721.8, 83.2** and typed "900, 20" stores **(−1020, 0)** with the height refused and the live verdict firing that same frame → "wide" REFUSED, the delta unchanged → Ctrl+S **with the box still open** saves without typing an "s" → Ctrl+Z restores **(−198.25, 0)** → Ctrl+R clears the screen → F4 off, **zero stderr**. Files: `scripts/{hud,hud_layout,game}.gd`, `tools/layout_promote.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`, `docs/{HUD-LAYOUT,BOARD}.md`, `NEEDS_ALEX.md`. |
| SG-83 | DONE 2026-08-02 (fable/SG-80) | **F4 edits saved with Ctrl+S did not persist — the harness was deleting the player's layout file** (P1 BUG) Owner, 2026-08-02: "Make sure changes made using F4 are being correctly saved, I had made some modifications and hit Ctrl S but it looks like they didnt save." **ROOT CAUSE — the save worked; the HARNESS destroyed it afterwards.** `tests/parity_test.gd` called `DirAccess.remove_absolute(...USER_PATH)` **six times per run** and saved its own fixtures over `user://hud_layout.json` in between. Agents run `SkyGear Tools.bat harness` on this machine many times a day, so any hand-alignment pass the owner saved was wiped minutes later, silently, with the editor having correctly reported it saved. Same family as SG-49 (posed endings writing fake rows into the player's real run log): a tool reaching into `user://` and destroying real player data — and the rule against it was already written a few lines up the same file, for the workshop save ("a harness that depends on a save file is not a harness"). Measured before fixing: a windowed smoke drove F4 → select → nudge → Ctrl+S on a naturally-reached screen AND on a P-posed one, and both wrote the file and reloaded correctly in a fresh process (offset applied to the drawn element) — which is what pointed at the deleter rather than the writer. **FIX.** `SkyGearHudLayout.store` is the one pointer every reader and writer goes through; the harness sets it to `user://hud_layout.harness.json` before its first check and restores it after its last, and the LAST check of every run compares the player's own file byte for byte with what it was before the first check ran. **TWO REAL DEAD KEYS FOUND WHILE THERE, both fixed:** Ctrl+S was swallowed by the screen picker (the list "owned every event" while it was open) and typed into the offset/size readout as the letter "s" (a chord is not a character; Ctrl+S and Ctrl+Z now fall through to the editor). **AND IT SAYS WHAT IT DID:** the bar reads `SAVED ·` plus the real path on disk, or `COULD NOT SAVE to … — nothing was written` in the alarm colour — it printed "layout is clean" on a failed write before, which is silence exactly where an alarm belongs. **CHECKS (3 new, 702→705):** `editor · Ctrl+S writes the file, and a fresh load reads the edit back` (the editor's own key through its own input path, to disk, to a fresh `load_layout`) · `editor · and Ctrl+S is never modal — the typed box and the picker both let it through` · `editor · the harness never touches the player's own saved layout`. VERIFY: harness **705/705 from a clean worktree of HEAD (cd79335) + this diff**. Files: `scripts/{hud,hud_layout,game}.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd`, `docs/{HUD-LAYOUT,BOARD}.md`. Shipped with SG-80. |
| SG-81 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/SG-81) — awaiting the owner's feel-check on the seeded five | **Lab: attach and tune LIGHTS on 3D models — type, color, strength, offset — saved where the renderer reads them** (P2 FEATURE) **THE RENDERER GOT THE READER FIRST, and the lab writes the file it reads.** The owner's ask verbatim: "can we add a way to map lighting effects to 3D models in the lab? (Add customization around types of lighting, color, strength, etc... anything that can be useful here since the models don't have baked lighting)". **THE SCHEMA — `assets/models/lights.json`, keyed by MODEL KEY** (the directory under `assets/models/`), so a row is worn by EVERY live instance of that model: all three braziers, every furnace knight in the wave, the corpse of one still playing `die`. Per light: `type` omni\|spot · `color` (html) · `energy` · `range` and `offset` in GROUND UNITS in the model's own frame · `attenuation` · `angle` + `aim` for a spot only · optional `hz`/`depth`/`shape` throb, where **pulse** is the clean sine the brazier has always had and **flicker** adds an out-of-step second term — a fire guttering, not a lamp humming. A SIDECAR rather than metadata on the .tscn for the reason `weapons.json` is a file: those scenes are GENERATED and a re-ingest eats anything written into them. Rows are normalised on read to carry ONLY the keys their type spends (an omni has no `angle`), the `hud_layout` "zero halves are ERASED" rule. **PER-KEY FALLBACK, and finer:** a model whose entry is not a list of rows loses that model; one bad ROW loses that row and leaves its neighbours lit (`view · a malformed lights entry falls back alone, never the deck` — kept brazier and steam_vent out of a four-key file with two poisoned). **AN ABSENT FILE IS TODAY'S RENDERING** — the flush returns on its first line, no node is made, and both built-in accents stay (`view · an absent lights file makes no light and changes no picture`). **THE BUDGET IS THE LAW HERE AND IT IS ARITHMETIC.** `MODEL_LIGHT_MAX_ENERGY` 2.0 and `MODEL_LIGHT_MAX_RANGE` 460u clamp every row AS IT IS READ, so a hand-typed 40/4000 is read back as 2.00/460 and the lab cannot save a sun; `MODEL_LIGHT_CAP` 8 is the SG-40 nearest-N pattern, second use; `MODEL_LIGHT_ENERGY_BUDGET` 7.5 caps the SUM, because a cap on count alone is one a tuned file walks through. Measured: a 40-row flood lit **3 of 40 for 6.00 energy**; the shipped deck asks 10 lights (10.24 nominal) and spends **7.40 of 7.50 on 7 of them**, against the 7.39 of fixed accent energy the deck ALREADY carried — the table moves that light around, it does not add to it. **FIGURES FIRST, then nearest to the eye**, with a reason rather than a preference: a brazier that loses its light keeps the painted floor pool that has always been most of its read at this camera, and a boarder has nothing to fall back on — without it the deck furniture BEHIND the captain (nearer the eye than anything she is fighting) spent the whole budget on scenery she has her back to. **HONEST ABOUT SG-34:** that guard measures the ENVIRONMENT constants and cannot see this table at all, so it stays green with a full lights file for the wrong reason; the budget is guarded separately by `view · model lights are accents, and the budget says so in numbers`, and the two together cover what one cannot. **A LIT KEY REPLACES THE BUILT-IN, never doubles it:** the brazier/lantern omni and the Boiler's `_boiler_fire` are gated on `model_lit_by_table`, the seeded rows reproduce those lamps exactly (same colour, place, energy, hz and depth), and `_boiler_lamp()` is now ONE expression read by both the built-in lamp and `_model_light_gain`, which divides by `BOILER_LAMP_FULL` to carry the Boiler's health drive onto a table light. The painted floor pool under every flame is drawn either way — it is paint, not budget. **THE LAB: a fourth mode, LIGHTS** (`SkyGear Tools.bat lab`, or `--lights --model brazier`). ADD OMNI / ADD SPOT / COPY / REMOVE and two toggles on the right where the bones live; eleven dial rows (fifteen for a spot) on the SG-39 typed-value widget — click a number and type it, wheel a row, Shift x10 / Alt x0.1; a GIZMO drawing each light's position as a cross, its reach as a ring on the grid (whose squares are 25 ground units) and a spot's aim and cone; DARK ROOM in the LOOK panel kills the lab's own two lamps so what is left is the light you are dialling; Ctrl+Z undoes; REVERT re-reads the file; SAVE writes `assets/models/lights.json` — **not the clipboard**, which is what the FX dials do because their home is a constant. The lab lights the model through `SkyGearView3D.apply_model_light`, the RENDERER's own applier made static, so the lab and the deck are one implementation and not two readings of one schema. **SEEDED FIVE:** brazier, lantern_post, steam_vent (a SPOT, aimed UP out of the grate — the one thing on this deck a cone is right for, and it lights the Boilerwright standing on his own teal ring), boiler, and **armored — the furnace knight, which is the SG-86 candidate and it works**: over the same crop on the same seed and tick, the right-hand knight goes mean luminance **30.4 → 33.4 with hot-orange pixels 10 → 276**, the centre 57.4 → 59.4 and 4504 → 5241; the left-hand one is flat to slightly down (34.3 → 33.4) because a brazier beside him gave up its omni to the budget. Deck-wide the frame is unchanged (mean luminance 41.72 → 41.94 on the same shot), which is the point. **CHECKS (8, harness 719 → 727):** `view · the model lights file round-trips through its own reader` · `view · a malformed lights entry falls back alone, never the deck` · `view · an absent lights file makes no light and changes no picture` · `view · the model-light cap holds under a flood, and no file can save a sun` · `view · every field in the lights schema is read by the renderer` (the twin-guard: eleven fields, each mutated and driven through the whole path — sanitise, request, budget, apply — and a field that moves nothing on a real Light3D fails the build) · `view · model lights are accents, and the budget says so in numbers` · `view · what the lab saves is what the renderer reads back` · `view · a lit model key replaces the built-in flame light, never doubles it`. **EVIDENCE:** `.shots/sg81/` — `deck-before.png`/`deck-after.png` (the same posed deck without and with the file), `knight-before.png`/`knight-after.png` (the SG-86 crop), `lab-brazier.png` (the tool), `lab-armored-darkroom.png` (his chest light alone, gizmo and reach ring drawn). |
| SG-82 | DONE — fable/visual-corrections, 2026-08-02 | **The cape goes OFF by default today — rebuild must re-earn its place** (P1 BUG) Owner, twice now (“looks horrible”, then “atrocious”); the 2026-08-02 screenshot shows a rigid mahogany plank. **SHIPPED:** `HERO_CLOAKS` is empty — the captain's row is commented out with both verdicts and the date, not deleted — and `scripts/cloak.gd` stays in the build and under harness, because SG-63 rebuilds ON it and the simulation half was never what he was looking at. Restoring her cape is uncommenting one line, and that happens when his verdict turns it on. Checks: `cloak · no class wears a cape …` pins the table EMPTY (this is what fails the day somebody uncomments her); `cloak · and off means gone …` keeps the SG-23 fallback, so off means the figure that shipped before capes existed rather than a cape at zero opacity; `cloak · the captain on the deck is bare-backed, through the renderer's own path` stops the table and `_sync_captain` from disagreeing; and the physics checks now drive a rig this test wears one onto by hand. **WHY IT READ AS A PLANK — for whoever rebuilds it.** It is the geometry, not the tuning. The banner is a 5x5 vertex grid (BONES+1 rings by COLS+1 columns) and every ring is bound RIGIDLY to one bone — `weights = [1,0,0,0]`, no blending between rings — so the sheet has exactly four hinge lines and each ring stays a straight rigid bar between them. There is no per-column freedom at all: the whole chain carries eight degrees of freedom (a pitch and a side per bone), so the cape can only swing as one board. A hem cannot ripple, a corner cannot lift, a fold cannot happen. The proportions make it worse: on a 1.76 m figure LENGTH_OF_HEIGHT 0.56 gives a 0.99 m drop against a 0.77 m collar and a 0.99 m hem — as wide as it is long, which is a board's aspect and not a cape's. The only curvature is `wrap_top` 0.055 of height relaxing to `wrap_hem` 0.015: a ~10 cm parabolic bow across a metre of width, invisible at any camera. Every vertex carries the SAME baked normal (0, 0, -1) with no smoothing pass, so the whole sheet shades as ONE FLAT FACET under the deck lamps and never catches the moving highlight that would say cloth. And `_cape_texture()` paints “a vertical weave: every few columns a shade darker, like the planking's boards” in oxblood #5a1f25 — which is the deck-planking generator's own recipe, in red. Flat facet, plus plank stripes, plus board proportions, equals mahogany. **The rebuild needs, in this order:** many more rings and columns with SMOOTH weights spanning two bones; per-column freedom (or a real cloth solver) so the hem moves independently of the collar; recomputed normals so the sheet shades curved; a cut narrow at the collar and flared at the hem; and a texture that is not the planking painter. **EVIDENCE:** `.shots/sg78-82/props-before.png` (the plank itself, at HEAD) against `.shots/sg78-82/props-after.png`, and `.shots/sg78-82/cape-off/idle-close.png` (bare-backed, shoulder camera). |
| SG-63 | DONE — opus/SG-63, 2026-08-02 (cape shipped OFF, see the verdict) | **The 2D reads: impacts/puffs and in-air skill-shape billboards go 3D; cape quality fix-or-kill** (P2 FEATURE) **A — THE PUFFS AND THE BURSTS HAVE BODIES.** Every impact particle in the game was a `QuadMesh` in `BILLBOARD_PARTICLES` mode wearing a painted plate — a flat card swung to face the camera, which is the same disc from every angle and is exactly the tell SG-40 removed from the projectile HEADS. Each behaviour is real geometry now (`PARTICLE_BODY`): **spark** and **shard** are prisms ALIGNED TO THEIR OWN VELOCITY, so a fleck lies along the way it is going and swings as it curves; **steam** is a low-poly sphere and the only LIT thing in the particle layer, tumbling on its own axis so the deck lamps travel across it while it rises. Element identity is untouched — `ELEMENT_FX` motion and the 26/s-vs-8/s light decay are where finding 4 put it and both are still pinned. **AND THE BURST GOT A BODY AT ALL:** `burst` is the death-and-detonation effect (every kill, every powder keg at radius 175, the hulk at 260) and it was the LAST shape with nothing in the air — VFX-PLAN §3/§4 gave arc, cone, line, chain, beam, circle and aoe geometry on 2026-07-31 and this one was skipped, so a thing coming apart drew a painted cartoon star flat on the planking. It is now a hollow shock ring on the deck, two shell rings standing off it (a dome, not a hoop) and a one-shot throw of real oriented debris. **TWO THINGS THE BUILD TAUGHT, both recorded in the source.** (1) A radial fan of RIBBONS does not work and cannot: `_ribbon` sets its width perpendicular to the path AND to the line of sight, so a shard travelling along the view ray has a degenerate cross product and comes out as a fat pale lozenge lying over the fight. Rings are safe from it by construction. (2) `ember_particle.png` is a painted BILLBOARD and about 130 of its lit pixels sit in the cyan-blue hues; wrapped round a prism and minified it made a burst render as a rainbow firework. Mesh bodies wear the generated white dot; the puff keeps `puff_steam` because a soft mask is exactly what eats a low-poly sphere's rim. Checks: `impact · no impact particle is a camera-facing card — every family draws a real body`, `impact · and every body is oriented by its own motion rather than by the camera`, `impact · and the steam puff is lit, so the deck's own lamps model it as a volume`, `impact · the bodies cost no extra emitters and no extra capacity`, `vfx · a burst has a body in the air, not only a mark on the planking`, `vfx · and its shards leave on a spread of angles rather than lying in the deck`, `vfx · a burst throws its debris ONCE however many frames it is drawn for`, `vfx · and the fired ledger is a fixed ring, like every other pool here`. Evidence: `.shots/sg63/burst-before-after.png`, `.shots/sg63/puff-before-after.png`, `.shots/sg63/clips/sg63-fight-{before,after}.gif`. **B — THE SG-78 TRAP WAS UNDER FIVE MORE EFFECTS, and the answer is recorded as a rule.** SG-78 found the aim ring flooding because `_art("ring", …)` prefers `rune_player.png`, and that plate measures ALPHA-255 out to 90% of its radius — a filled disc, not a ring. It fixed that one ring. Measured the rest: the same door was open under the `circle` shape (a Pulse at `radius * 2`), the fire fields, the Colossus turn ring, **the aura edge — which a card WIDENS** — and the Boilerwright's vent stand; plus `burst_impact.png`, opaque at its centre, scaling to 520 ground units on a hulk break. All six draw through `_ring_texture()` now, whose hollowness is guaranteed by construction. `slash_arc.png` was measured too and KEPT: it reads 0 alpha at its centre and peaks mid-radius, so it is genuinely a rim. The `_:` fallback arm also got a wall of air, so a shape added to the sim tomorrow arrives with a body instead of as a plate. Checks: `vfx · no ring or burst decal draws through a plate that measures opaque` (code lines only — the first version counted its own explanatory comments as call sites), `vfx · the two retired plates are opaque at their centres, which is why they are retired` (measured off the PNGs, so the rule cannot be dropped on the grounds that it stopped mattering), `vfx · and the generated ring they were replaced with is hollow at its centre`. The fix is visible in a real fight frame: `.shots/sg63/fight-before-after.png`, middle and right columns — a solid orange plate becomes a hollow ring. **C — THE CAPE: REBUILT, MEASURABLY NO LONGER A PLANK, AND STILL SHIPPED OFF.** Every one of SG-82's four causes is gone. It is a 3x6 LATTICE of eighteen bones (three chains: port, middle, starboard) against one chain of four, so the cloth has 36 degrees of freedom rather than 8 and the columns are separately sprung — a hard turn now lifts one corner of the hem and leaves the other hanging, which the old chain could not do at all. Weights are BLENDED bilinearly across two chains and two rings, so there is no hinge line on the sheet (only the collar corners are still owned outright, on purpose — that is what holds the cape on her). Normals are differenced off the rest surface instead of baked to one flat `(0,0,-1)`, and that surface now has FOLDS in it that open toward the hem, so the sheet shades as several facets and catches a moving highlight. The cut is a garment's: 1.02 m long from a 0.53 m collar to a 0.88 m hem on a 1.76 m figure, against SG-82's 0.99 x 0.99 board. The hem SCALLOPS with the folds and its corners ride up, which matters more than any of the rest at forty pixels — a rectangle reads as a plank however many bones are under it. And the texture is a 2/2 oxblood TWILL with a matching NORMAL MAP, not the deck-planking painter in red. **VERDICT: it wins, and it is still off.** `.shots/sg63/cape-before-after.png` is the four-panel: left column SG-82 (a hard-edged uniformly-flat red signboard), right column the rebuild (a curved, folded, tonally-graded garment with a soft trailing edge). `.shots/sg63/clips/sg63-cape-dash-{before,after}.gif` is the same in motion. It is NOT a second plank — that branch does not apply. **But `HERO_CLOAKS` stays empty and `cloak · no class wears a cape` stays green,** because the owner has vetoed this feature twice by eye and what has been proved here is that the geometry is no longer the reason. Whether he wants a cape at all is a decision only he can make (rule 4), and SG-82's own row says restoring it happens when his verdict turns it on. He stands a head over the captain's 176, which is the point of him: the ledger's "goblins taller than the hero" complaint was about the small three and was fixed PER-KIND precisely so this one could keep its bulk. **SCALE RULE, stated: he is a FIGURE, so he is scaled by HEIGHT, not by `camera_span`.** SG-79's camera-projected ruler is the PROP rule and SG-76's `ruler_key` is its multi-state extension; both live in `_sync_prop_model`. A figure goes through `SkyGearRig3D.setup`, which fits the model's measured Y to a ground-unit height — right here because the number being fitted to is not a billboard's painted height but the simulation's own creature height, and because a figure TURNS: a span-based ruler would resize him every time he changed heading. **CLIP INVENTORY — WIRED (11, and the renderer asks for every one):** idle, **walk**, run, swing..swing5 (the five slashes, through `VARIANTS`), spin, hurt (impact -> flinch) and **die**. **AVAILABLE-UNWIRED (40, aboard under honest names, no reader claimed):** idle_alt/idle3-5, walk2, run2, strafe1-4, turn/turn2/turn180/turn180_2, dash, jump2, attack, jump_attack, slide_attack, kick/kick2, block1-3, hurt2-5, crouch1-6, cast/cast2, taunt, die2, draw/draw2. **THE DEATH, AND WHERE THE SEAM IS.** `enemy.kill()`/`take_damage` set `dead`, run `on_enemy_killed` (scrap, pressure, press-gang, the burst, the sfx) and `queue_free()` on the same line — which is why every death in this game has been a figure ceasing to exist inside its own burst. The renderer-side seam already existed and is exactly where it has to be: `_recycle()` sees a rig nothing claimed this frame, and that is a boarder the simulation has already finished with. It now hands that rig to a CORPSE list instead of freeing it — `want("die", 0, DEATH_WINDOW)`, held **1.60 s** while the clip plays (the same clip-stretched-to-window machinery every swing rides: 2.40 s of death at **1.50x**, 100% shown), then **0.40 s** sinking through the planking with its own shadow fading under it. **Nothing in the simulation moved**: the kill, the payout and the free all happen BEFORE the corpse exists, so no death can hold up a wave, a wave-clear or a payout. Fixed with a MEASURED number, taken off his pack's own root motion before the ingest in-placed it: the walk clip carries 166 ground units in 1.37 s at his drawn 216, which is **99.1 units a second at the 1.76 m reference** (`AUTHORED_WALK_SPEED`). The same measurement puts this pack's run at 236 against the captain's 210 — a twelfth apart — so the run constant stands. `SkyGearRig3D.gait()` picks whichever cycle has to be strained less, crossover sqrt(99*210) = **144**, which leaves the scrapper on his run by six units and changes nothing that shipped. **THE STRIDE LAW RUNS THE OTHER WAY, pinned:** he is TALLER than the reference, so the same ground speed drives his cycle SLOWER — **0.617x against a captain-height copy of him at 0.758x**, same 75 units a second. SG-65 pinned the small case at twice; this is the large case at four fifths. **THE FLINCH** rides the simulation's own `stun_time` (ARC, 0.45 s, and the boarder's state machine returns early through all of it) rather than a hit counter kept behind the renderer's back — a flinch on every tick of damage would freeze a 180-hp wall solid. **THE EMISSIVE ANSWER — the pipeline reads an emission map at last, and it cost one render to get right.** SG-74 declined to list the Boilerwright's because there was no reader; the knight is the case worth writing one for, since `handoff-3d` asked for a furnace-grille chest that emits and nothing else. That session concluded the map was sampling white; this one shows the BASE COLOUR was doing it. Shipped as **black base, map on top, energy as manifest data** (`emission_energy`; his is **6.0**) — his sheet peaks at 49/255, which is 0.03 in linear light and invisible at 1x. **MEASURED, both cameras.** Alone at the game camera the lit area is **5.75% of his silhouette against the painted sprite's 6.48%**, in the same orange (204,142,66 against 207,134,53). The energy is at its knee: 20 gives 5.90% there, and on the deck 6 gives 149 hot pixels, 18 gives 170, 40 gives 165 — the tonemapper is saturated and more buys nothing. **ON THE DECK THE PAINTING STILL WINS ON LIGHT, AND THAT IS REPORTED, NOT HIDDEN** — same tick, same seed, mesh beside billboard (`.shots/clips/knight-vs-sprite-mid.png`, `knight.gif` against `knight-before.gif`): over the same crop the mesh reads **mean luminance 34.1 against 45.4**, peak 161 against 192, and **149 hot-orange pixels against 636**. His furnace and his visor both glow and are legible at fight range — but the painted grille is drawn far larger relative to the figure, and the painting carries a rim light the deck does not give the mesh. **And his hands are EMPTY** where the sprite carries a double-bladed axe. Both filed as **SG-86**, measured, with frames. **THE READ-VERDICT:** he passes this row's own test. At the real camera beside the captain he is unmistakably a WALL — wider, heavier, a head taller — and he TURNS, WALKS and DIES, none of which a card ever did. The two losses above are losses of LIGHT and of PROP, not of mass. **CHECKS (9 new, 710 -> 719):** `figure · the furnace knight builds from the owner's own native rig` · `figure · the knight carries his clip set — a walk for the wall, five cuts, a flinch and a DEATH` (11 of 11, out of 51 aboard) · `figure · and he stands a head over the captain, because the SIM's own row says so` (216 against 176) · `figure · the renderer knows he can die, and knows the scrapper still cannot` · `figure · dying plays the death, and the whole of it fits the window the body gets` (1.50x, 100% of a 2.40 s death in 1.60 s) · `figure · and the body does not start leaving until the death has been played` · `rig · the same ground speed drives the TALLER figure's cycle slower — the stride law, the other way round` · `rig · a wall at seventy-five walks and a rusher at a hundred and fifty still runs` · `figure · his furnace chest emits, and only his furnace chest — the map is the emitter, never a flat colour`. **TIMING:** `anim_timing -- --model armored` took him as a second enemy row and grew two of its own — attack `swing3` at 3.33x ok, locomotion `walk` at 0.62x against a 216-unit stride, death 100% shown; | SG-94 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/SG-94) — the machine is painted and grounded; the "too easy" half is SG-96 and is the owner's to judge | **THE COLOSSUS IS TEXTURED, AND ITS SHADOW IS UNDER IT** (P2 BUG + ASSET). The owner, against a screenshot: *"Collosus is way too easy - he needs to be scary the player. Not sure what's going on with his texture."* Three faults in the OUTSTANDING entry; this row is the first two. **FAULT 1 — NO TEXTURE, AND IT WAS AN ASSET GAP.** The part-segmentation GLB the boss was cut from is a VISUALISATION: 13 geometries, no UVs, zero bytes of image, every part flat-filled with a categorical label colour. `tools/segment_parts.py` threw those colours away for a role palette and `tools/rig_parts.gd` painted each part a flat albedo, so the deck got an untextured machine with the furnace lamp glowing inside it — which is exactly what the screenshot shows, and what "floating dark and gold blobs" describes: the dark-plate parts and the brass parts reading as separate lumps because no continuous surface joined them. **THE MARRIAGE, and WHY THIS DIRECTION.** The owner supplied the TEXTURED TWIN of the same sculpt (`Meshy_AI_Brassbound_Juggernaut_0803021335_texture.glb`, 83.9 MB: one welded surface, 1,318,962 tris, real UVs, four painted maps). Three routes were possible and the choice was made by MEASUREMENT, not preference. *Direct assignment is impossible*: the two exports are not the same topology — 1,318,962 triangles against 1,366,036, and 696,655 vertices against 682,804. *Transferring UVs onto the existing split is possible and wrong*: those parts were decimated to 8,000 triangles by a pass that had never seen a UV, and 5.4% of the textured mesh's vertices (37,446 of 696,655) are SEAM duplicates, so a decimated triangle straddling a seam is not merely possible but likely. *So the parts are re-cut FROM the textured mesh with the segmentation demoted to a label source* — `tools/split_rotors.py`'s trick on the gunner, same direction, and it is now the third argument to `segment_parts.py`. Asserted before anything else, the way split_rotors does: the two exports' axis ratios agree to **0.00002** (`[0.9424, 1.0, 0.644]` both ways), so they are the same object in the same orientation and a flipped re-export cannot silently weld the head to a foot. The label transfer is tight — the median textured triangle sits **0.00074** of the model's longest axis from the segmentation vertex that labels it (under 2.5 mm at the drawn height), p99 0.0033, worst 0.0093 — and proportionate: every one of the thirteen parts comes back with 93.7–98.8% of its segmented twin's triangle count, tracking the two files' overall 96.5%. **THE SEAMS SURVIVE THE DECIMATION** because glTF has already split the mesh at every seam into separate indices that no edge joins, so an edge collapse cannot cross one; and each surviving vertex takes its UV and normal from a vertex it actually DESCENDS FROM, read out of `fast_simplification`'s own collapse history (`replay_simplification` → input→output map), not from a nearest-neighbour guess. Normals ride the same map, so the part keeps the sculpt's shading instead of an 8,000-triangle recompute — and neither `merge_vertices` nor `fix_normals` runs on the painted path, because merging welds exactly the seam duplicates the UVs depend on. **WHAT SURVIVED, MEASURED.** All 13 parts, same names, same `source` geometries, symmetry labels still AGREE with the measurement; **7,996 triangles against the 8,000 budget**; the five beats (`idle`, `walk`, `swing`, `turn`, `die`); and the joints, which moved by at most **9.78 mm on a 3,300 mm figure (0.30%)** — the difference between measuring one joint on two exports of one sculpt. The death still reads: `foot_l` moves **0.001 m** across the whole clip (it never releases — the machine collapses off its own anchor), every other part scatters 0.06–3.23 m, and all thirteen come to rest with their boxes at y ≈ 0.000, nothing hanging and nothing sunk. **THE MAPS, THE HULK WAY.** Shrunk by the project's own law and the project's own function (`tools/meshy.py:shrink_glb`, the 142 MB → 2.3 MB precedent), geometry untouched: **34.66 MB delivered → 0.454 MB, 76× smaller** — base colour 4096→1024 (15.76 → 0.386 MB), normal 4096→512 (16.08 → 0.051), metallic-roughness 2048→256 (2.74 → 0.016), emission 2048→256 (0.08 → 0.001). A 330-unit figure renders at 616 px, over `TEX_FULL_ABOVE_PX`, so it earns the full 1024 albedo. The maps are resized from the DELIVERED pixels before the kit is written, so there is one lossy stage instead of two (leaving it to the shrink alone measured 39.7 dB against a one-pass reference — invisible at 616 px, but free not to spend). Kit 0.79 MB; `boss_parts.scn` 0.22 → 4.6 MB, which puts the boss directory at 5.6 MB, level with `armored` (5.6) and `captain` (5.7). `rig_parts.gd` now leaves the kit's material alone when `parts.json` says `textured` — the flat path stays for a kit with nothing to lose. **THE FURNACE LAMP STAYS, AND AT 1.28 — re-judged, not assumed.** The twin's emission map does not replace it (mean 0.2/255, 0.17% of pixels lit: a few small vents, not a furnace). It is not washing the new paint out: the blown fraction of the frame is 0.043% at energy 0.0 and 0.043% at 2.00, so nothing the lamp does clips. And it saturates about here — measured with the flicker HELD STILL, because a 3.4 Hz flicker 18% deep makes two energies differ by a fifth for no reason but phase (a first sweep read as pure noise until that was fixed): it lifts 2.7% of the frame at 0.60, **4.9% at 1.28**, 5.7% at 2.00. Raising it buys under a point of lit area for 56% more of a shared `MODEL_LIGHT_ENERGY_BUDGET`; lowering it to 0.90 costs a measurable part of the machine's read. **FAULT 2 — THE FLOATING BLOBS.** Every figure on this deck gets ONE contact shadow, sized off its gameplay radius and dropped at its origin. Right for a boarder; wrong for a thirteen-part machine twice over — and the death is where it is worst, because the disassembly throws the parts across two metres of deck while the blob stays at an origin none of them occupies. **ONE RULE FIXES BOTH, and it is the definition of the thing**: a contact shadow belongs to whatever is in CONTACT, so every part darkens the planking under its own measured footprint and that darkening falls off with how far its underside is off the deck (`CONTACT_FADE_M` = 1.0 m, chosen off the model: the legs start 0.11 m up and the torso at 0.94). A machine STANDING then shades under its feet and shins and nothing else — a footprint shadow DERIVED rather than declared — and the same rule, unchanged, grounds each part of the disassembly as it lands. 9 of 13 parts darken the deck standing. `_shadow` gained an opt-in per-axis `depth` (a fallen foot is half again deeper than it is wide); every existing caller omits it and keeps the 0.62 squash it always had. **The global system is deliberately untouched — that ask is SG-95.** **CHECKS (7 new, 797 → 804 rows):** `figure · the Colossus is PAINTED — every part wears the marriage's own maps, not a flat role colour` · `figure · and all thirteen share ONE material, so the kit carries four maps rather than fifty-two` · `figure · and its maps are shrunk to the budget a 616 px figure earns, not shipped at the delivered 4096` · `view · a segmented machine grounds itself part by part, and only the parts actually near the deck` · `view · and a part thrown across the deck TAKES ITS SHADOW WITH IT — the reported floating blob` · `view · and a part lifted clear of the planking stops darkening it` · `view · and every other figure's shadow keeps the squash it always had — the depth is opt-in`. **ALL SIX SUBSTANTIVE ROWS PROVEN TO BITE**, on the two regressions that would actually happen: restore `rig_parts`'s unconditional flat albedo and rebuild, and the harness goes **803/804** reading *"0 of 13 parts textured (flat: leg_l, foot_l, …)"*; make `_part_shadows` return false, and it goes **803/804** reading *"0 of 13 parts darken the planking"*. The shadow rows compare whole SETS of blob centres rather than proximity to the part that moved, because a shin stands directly over its own foot and "is there a shadow near here" cannot tell the two apart. **VERIFY:** harness **804/804 against a 797/797 baseline** — exactly +7 rows and all seven green. Both runs from a PRISTINE EXTRACT rather than from a working tree: `git archive` of HEAD (50a23ed) for the baseline and of this commit's own staged tree for the after, each imported from cold. That is not ceremony — an earlier pass measured 795/797 both sides from a reused worktree, and the two red rows were `railing_segment: no meshes` from a stale import cache for another agent's asset, not from anything real. Extracting the tree is what told the difference. HEAD also MOVED under this work mid-session (020bf8b → 50a23ed, where an earlier pass read 774 → 781), so the whole thing was re-measured on the new base rather than re-stated. stderr unchanged from baseline but for line numbers. Re-filmed: `.shots/clips/boss.gif` (200 frames, 10.0 s — the walk, the slam, the half-health TURN and the disassembly) and `.shots/clips/boss/frame_*.png`. The still the owner asked for is `.shots/clips/boss.png` at the real deck camera with the captain in frame for scale, and `.shots/clips/boss_before_after.png` is HEAD over this diff at the identical staging — the top half is the flat grey-and-olive lumps he photographed. Both shot by `tools/boss_shot.gd` (new), which walks the machine in on its own AI and stops on the simulation's own signal. | SG-90 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/SG-90) — awaiting the owner's feel-check on the death | **The Colossus is a SEGMENTED figure — thirteen rigid parts on hinges, and a death that comes apart** (P2 ASSET) **THE OWNER MADE THE BOSS AND CHOSE ITS APPROACH**, and the approach is the reason this worked: *"I feel like if we just do simple segmented movement it might work well for the boss. It's not exactly a humanoid model, and it doesnt move that much, and its death animation could be the parts just falling apart."* He is right about the material. `tools/rig_local.gd` already argued a rigid bind is "the honest material on a riveted machine" and paid for it with candy-wrapper elbows it did not want — it pays that because the scrapper arrives as ONE surface. **The Colossus arrives already cut**, so there is no seam to stretch and no bind to weight: a part is a node, its joint is that node's origin, a hinge is a rotation, and the death is the articulation running backwards. Numbered SG-90 and not SG-87 because 87, 88 and 89 were already spoken for in `tests/parity_test.gd` (drone, crew, goblin) before this row existed. **THE PART MAP IS MEASURED, NOT TYPED.** The delivered file names its geometries `GLTF`..`GLTF_12`; every name in `assets/models/boss/meshy.json` was derived from that geometry's own bounds — a midline part is a spine part, an outboard chain is an arm, the lowest pair is feet — so a re-export that renumbers them cannot silently swap a leg for an arm. `GLTF_12`→torso (409,886 faces), `GLTF_5`→**back stack** (the brief guessed "shoulders/arms"; it measures 0.025 deep, sits behind the midline and reaches the crown, so it is the dorsal boiler), `GLTF_6`→head, `GLTF_2/9`→shoulders, `GLTF_1/11`→arms, `GLTF/GLTF_10`→fists, `GLTF_4/8`→legs, `GLTF_3/7`→feet. Corroborated by something that is not geometry: Meshy flat-fills each part with a segmentation LABEL colour and both halves of all five pairs share theirs. It cannot ESTABLISH the pairing — thirteen parts are labelled out of a ten-colour palette, so the fists and the feet come back the same yellow — but it agrees with the measurement everywhere. **DECIMATION: 1,366,036 → 7,994 triangles, 0.59% kept, zero credits.** Local, per part, quadric, `tools/segment_parts.py`. Safe for the one reason that keeps SG-13 open for the captain: **there are no skin weights on a rigid part**. Her weights ride her vertices; a part carries nothing but its own surface. The ceiling is not a number this row picked — 8,000 is what the PROMPTED Colossus shipped at, same archetype at the same on-screen height. Split by surface AREA, floored at 220 a part so the head does not become a lump, and **equal across each mirrored pair** so two sides cannot decimate to different counts. Per part: torso 409,886→2,196 · back 187,950→1,366 · shoulders 107,658/104,966→554 · legs 79,278/75,800→504 · feet 84,194/79,880→468 · arms 58,808/56,844→332 · fists 49,950/48,978→248 · head 21,844→220. **Textures: ZERO bytes**, and that is a fact about the delivered file rather than a budget win — the part-segmentation export carries no UVs and no images at all. It is a VISUALISATION: ship it as delivered and the deck gets a harlequin. So each part wears a flat albedo by ROLE out of the project palette, and the furnace grate the prompted Colossus carried in an emission map became a LIGHT (`boss` row in `lights.json`, at the torso's measured front centre, on the knight's flicker) because there are no UVs to map one onto. **THE BEATS**, all five baked as node-transform tracks so `scripts/rig3d.gd` drives a pile of hinged boxes without knowing it is one — the priority list, the one-shot that ends, the crossfade table and the SG-85 death seam all work unchanged, and `skeleton` stays null (the two things that need one already return false). `idle` a slow settle on three periods that never line up (pistons, not lungs) · `walk` · `swing` a two-fisted slam · `turn` · `die`. **The walk's length is DERIVED and it found something:** a 3.30 m figure at 95 units a second asks rig3d for a playback rate of 0.512, which is UNDER the 0.55 floor — so unlike every other figure here the Colossus's rate is not a variable, it is pinned at 0.55 forever, and the cycle had to be authored to be correct at the rate it will always get. 1.915 m of stride per 1.109 s cycle = **95.0 ground units a second against the sim's 95**. This is SG-85's lesson from the other end: the knight was rated against the wrong constant, the Colossus falls off the end of the right one. **`turn` is one new row in rig3d** — the sim has held that beat since long before there was a model for it (`enemy.gd` returns early for all of `state == "turn"` and `take_damage` returns zero through it), and the renderer showed 1.6 seconds of a figure standing still. It sits above the attack and below a death, and is fitted to `SkyGearEnemy.TURN_TIME` — the beat's own length, newly named, because `turn_time` counts DOWN and a shrinking window is a clip that accelerates as it plays. **THE DEATH, which is the centrepiece and the owner's own idea.** Each part gets a moment it lets go, a velocity and a spin, and is integrated under gravity until it hits the planking, bounces once badly and stops; the whole thing is baked, so it ships as a clip like any other and goes through `view3d._recycle`'s existing seam (`want("die")`, corpse, sink) with **no simulation change and no new pool**. What makes it read as a machine failing rather than an explosion: it **breaks at the joints it hinged on** (a part's break point is its own node origin — the elbow it bent at is the elbow it comes off at); it is a **sequence, not a frame** (right shoulder shears first and takes its arm and fist with it, then the head, then the boiler lets go backwards, then a knee buckles and the mass comes down); **the feet stay** — `foot_l` is never released, so the machine collapses OFF its own anchor and leaves it standing, which is the detail that says it was bolted down; and heavy parts get less velocity and less spin than light ones. **Everything is accounted for**: thirteen nodes go in and thirteen come out, moved and never spawned, so the pool is the rig and the cap is one boss. **Two bugs found by measuring rather than by looking**, both invisible to a screenshot: (1) Godot's animation mixer composes a whole Transform3D for any node it touches and a component with no track does NOT fall through to the node's own value — so parts carrying only a rotation track had their position ZEROED and both shoulders collapsed into the middle of the torso, 1.77 m of travel in a clip whose largest authored movement is 22 mm. Every animated part now carries a position track even when it never moves. (2) The death's first integrator rested each part at a fixed height and put the torso 0.65 m and the back stack 0.72 m THROUGH the deck, because a box resting on a corner stands far taller than one resting on a face; it lands on its rotated extent now. **And one found by rendering**, which no headless probe could see: the first palette was two stops too dark and half of that was the metallic — a high-metallic surface on a lamp-lit deck has no environment to reflect and renders as a hole in the planking. Judged from a frame, fixed, re-judged. **EVIDENCE.** 12 checks, harness **771/771** (743 at this session's baseline; the difference is this row's 12 plus a concurrent agent's SG-87/88/89 work in the same tree): `figure · the Colossus builds as a scene of separate parts, not as a skinned rig` · `figure · all thirteen parts are present and named by measurement, not by GLTF index` · `figure · and the whole machine fits the triangle budget the prompted Colossus shipped at` · `figure · it stands at the SIM's own number for the archetype, not the spec's guess` · `figure · it carries the beats the fight actually plays — idle, walk, an attack, the TURN and a death` · `figure · and every beat MOVES the parts — a driven pose differs from the rest pose` · `rig · the Colossus WALKS, and its cycle covers exactly the ground the simulation moves it over` · `rig · the half-health turn is a beat the figure PLAYS, and the whole of it fits the 1.6 s it cannot be burst through` · `figure · DEATH DISASSEMBLES IT — the parts separate and spread across the planking` · `figure · and every part is accounted for — thirteen in, thirteen still on the rig, none leaked` · `figure · and they come to REST on the deck rather than hanging in the air or sinking through it` · `figure · and the painted billboard is still the fallback — delete the model and today's sprite comes back`. On film: `tools/clip.gd -- boss` → `.shots/clips/boss.gif` (10 s — the walk, the slam, the turn, and the machine coming apart). |
| SG-87 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/SG-87) — closes the `gunner_drone` handoff entry, the one that said it would "probably never" be answered | **The GUNNER flies — the owner's Clockwork Sentry Sphere is the propeller drone, not the sentry skill, and its whole animation is arithmetic** (P2 ASSET) **THE NAME WAS AMBIGUOUS AND THE MODEL SETTLED IT.** "Clockwork Sentry Sphere" points at two different things in this game: the **GUNNER**, a hovering propeller drone enemy (91.5 ground units, painted at `assets/art/enemies/drone_front_idle.png`), and the player's **SENTRY** skill, which draws an element-tinted `harpoon_ballista` billboard the prop purge deliberately kept. This row applied the SG-76 hulk rule — *verify by looking, not by reading the file name*, the check that caught two of three owner-stated labels — and it is the GUNNER. **THE EVIDENCE, three independent reads, all agreeing** (`.shots/probe/{front,side,back,deck41}.png`): **(1) Against the drone painting it matches item for item** — riveted brass disc body, ONE round glowing pale-blue lens with a cross-spoke window in the centre of the face, three two-blade propellers in ring mounts (one on top, one to each side), two chains hanging beneath with pointed iron weights, a small pod under the hull. **(2) Against the ballista it shares nothing at all** — the sentry billboard is a ground-mounted crossbow on a timber base with horizontal bow arms and a bolt; this model has no bow, no bolt, no base and no ground contact. **(3) The part-segmentation file corroborates without being looked at**: of its 11 geometries, two pairs of plates 99 units thin in a 14,529-unit model flank the hull and one more caps the mast — four blades and a top rotor — and two 5,688-unit vertical strands hang below the body, which are the chains. Ten minutes, and it is the difference between wiring a drone and wiring a skill. **WHICH OF THE TWO FILES SHIPS: BOTH, each for what only it can do.** The textured export ships as geometry — it is the one with UVs and four maps, and a brass drone with a blue lens IS its texture. The part-segmentation export ships nothing: no UVs, no images, **517,022 triangles** and flat categorical label colours. But it is the only file that knows which triangles are BLADES, and the textured one is a single welded surface — 10,344 triangles, 5,116 welded vertices, and a connected-component pass over it returns **exactly one component**. There is no node to spin. **`tools/split_rotors.py` (new)** transfers the labels across by nearest neighbour and throws the segmentation away: the two files are asserted to be the same object in the same orientation first (axis ratios agree to three decimals, **1.000 / 0.887 / 0.402** — a check a flipped re-export would fail loudly instead of welding blades to chains), the blade groups are found **by measurement rather than by index** (a run of Y-slices too wide to be a mast and too thin to be a ring mount — which is what found the top rotor the segmentation had left inside its own mast assembly), and each rotor's vertices are rewritten relative to its own centroid so the node origin IS the hub. A rotor pivoted at the model origin does not spin, it orbits. **OUT: `Body`, `RotorTop`, `RotorLeft`, `RotorRight`.** **THE MOTION IS A TABLE, NOT A CLIP.** `SkyGearView3D.ROTOR_MOTION` — spin 16 rad/s, bob 9 ground units, beat 2.1 rad/s, lift 22 — read by `_fly`, called at the end of `_sync_rig`. The gunner has no AnimationPlayer and never will (the handoff spec: *"no legs, no spine and no arms to place markers on... deliver it rigged and the rig is wasted"*), so `want("run")` returns early exactly as the harness's clipless-lump fixture has always pinned, and the movement is `rotation.y +=` and `position.y =`. **CAPPED AND POOLED BY CONSTRUCTION**: the rotor children are found ONCE, when the rig is built, and kept on the rig — which is pooled — so a frame with four drones is twelve rotations and four writes and **no tree walk**. The blade angle is `wrapf`-ed, so 150 seconds of spinning is the same arithmetic as the first second (measured: 0.79 rad after 9,000 ticks, not 2,400). The phase is per-drone off the rig's instance id, and the bob and the spin take different multiples of it, so four drones do not breathe in unison. **MEASUREMENTS.** 10,344 triangles, **untouched** — the hulk bargain (SG-76): geometry is left alone and the maps come down. **14.65 MB to 1.29 MB**, four 2048 sheets re-encoded to 1024/512/256/256 by `tools/meshy.py shrink_glb` at the same screen-height law every generated prop went through. Model units 1.898 x 1.684 x 0.763; drawn at **91.5 ground units** = `boarder_height("GUNNER")`, the renderer's own arithmetic — **scaled by HEIGHT, not by `camera_span`**, because it is a FIGURE that turns and SG-79's projected ruler is the PROP rule (`_sync_prop_model`). **10,344 is over the 7,000 `tools/meshy.py` sets as this one asset's override, and deliberately**: that override exists because a propeller blade is a thin flat plate and a decimator welds its front face to its back — it was written the day the pipeline's own drone came back from 3,000 with a hole punched through a blade. This model has three propellers and was never decimated. **IT ALSO BEATS THE INCUMBENT.** A generated gunner has been on the deck since the first boarder batch; side by side (`.shots/probe/front.png` against `.shots/probe/old/front.png`) the owner's is closer to the painting on every count the painting is specific about — two-blade props against four, a cross-spoked blue lens against a plain teal disc, long chains with spear-point weights against short ones, and blackened steel with brass against all-gold. **CHECKS (4):** `figure · the drone arrives in four pieces — a body and three rotors, each pivoted on its own hub` · `figure · its spin is bounded for a whole run and its bob never reaches the planking` · `figure · and only the drone flies — every other boarder walks, and never looks at the table` · `figure · the drone is shrunk the hulk way — a tenth of the delivered file, and every triangle of it still there`. **FILM:** NEW `drone` scenario -> `.shots/clips/drone.gif` (6 s, four drones hovering and shooting over a real fight). |
| SG-88 | DONE 2026-08-02 (opus/SG-88) — closes the `crew` handoff entry; the owner's feel-check is in NEEDS_ALEX | **The CREW are a MESH — the last flat ALLY figures, and the first death an ally has ever played** (P2 ASSET) **THE PACK.** `Male Locomotion Pack (1).zip` (the Little Adventurer through Mixamo's auto-rigger, skin on 41 native `mixamorig_*` bones, grey because the OBJ upload was bare, plus **ten animation-only clips on that rig**) and two loose 28 MB FBXs, `Bayonet Stab.fbx` and `Dying Backwards.fbx`. Maps from `Meshy_AI_Little_Adventurer_0802232831_texture_obj.zip` through `maps_archive`, the captain/Boilerwright/knight precedent: 4096 to 1024, 27 MB of PNG to 1.4 MB. **THE ARCHIVE IS A DIRECTORY, which is new here and needed no code**: the clips arrived in three deliveries, `tools/ingest_model.py`'s `unpack` already returns a directory unchanged, so the three are laid side by side under `.model_originals/crew/` and the manifest points at the folder. A pack that arrives in pieces does not need a re-zip to be ingestible. One manifest entry, one `python tools/ingest_model.py crew`, **verified self-contained AFTER source deletion** per pipeline law. **HEIGHT: 165 ground units, AND IT CAME OUT OF THE SIM.** `handoff-3d` asked for "a touch under the captain's 176 — call it ~1.7 m"; `120 + CREW.radius*3` on the radius `scripts/lanes.gd` already keeps for a crewman is **165**, so nothing had to be invented. It RETIRES a hard-coded **110** the painted crew were drawn at — 110 against a captain of 176 is 62%, a deck of children holding the lanes, and the number was never measured, it was carried across from the browser. `crew_height()` is one function and BOTH paths read it, so the mesh and the sprite it falls back to cannot disagree about how tall a sailor is. The crew share the goblin's 15-unit footprint exactly; the goblin is halved by `FIGURE_SCALE` and the crewman is not, which is the whole difference between something that scuttles and the man holding the line. **CLIP INVENTORY — WIRED (5):** `idle`, **`walk`** (their locomotion, and the one they will nearly always play: the crew push up-deck at 118, under the 144 gait crossover), `run` (the tier above it, so `gait` has both to choose between), **`swing` = Bayonet Stab** (a pike thrust, which is what the painted crewman is holding) and **`die` = Dying Backwards**. **AVAILABLE-UNWIRED (7, aboard under honest names, no reader claimed):** `strafe`, `strafe2`, `strafe3`, `strafe4` (the four strafes), `turn`, `turn2` (the two 90-degree turns) and `jump`. **THE DEATH GOES THROUGH SG-85'S SEAM UNCHANGED** — renderer-side, `_recycle` to corpse list to `want("die", 0, DEATH_WINDOW)` to sink — **and wiring it forced a real bug into the open.** Every other figure the renderer draws is a Node with an instance id; a crewman is a plain `Dictionary` in an `Array` that `_update_crew` calls `remove_at` on, so `"c%d" % i` names a DIFFERENT man the frame after anyone dies. Billboards did not care — the crew are identical by design and a sprite that jumps one lane slot is a sprite. A death cares: `_recycle` would have shelved whichever rig fell off the end of the array, **played the death at the wrong sailor's feet, and handed the dead man's body to a live one**. Fixed with a stable key the renderer stamps onto the crewman the first time it draws him — renderer-owned data living on the simulation's dictionary because that is where it has to live to survive a `remove_at`; the simulation neither sets it nor reads it, and **no sim change**, which is the SG-85 rule. **CHECKS (6):** `figure · your own sailors build from the owner's own native rig` · `figure · and a crewman stands a head UNDER the captain, on the sim's own radius rather than a guess` (165 against 176, from radius 15) · `figure · the crew carry their clip set — an idle, a walk up the deck, a bayonet stab and a DEATH` (5 of 5, out of 12 aboard) · `figure · a crewman's binds resolve against his own skeleton, and the built rig stands its hips at sailor height` (the SG-45 bargain by hand, because CREW is not an `ENEMIES` kind and the self-enumerating boarder loop never reaches it) · `figure · and the renderer knows a crewman can die — the SG-85 seam, on the ally side of the deck` · `figure · the painted crew survive the mesh — the fallback the model-or-billboard fork exists for`. **FILM:** NEW `crew` scenario -> `.shots/clips/crew.gif` (8 s — a muster rung through the sim's own `crew_timer`, six sailors working up three lanes into a rank of scrappers, the bayonet stab, and two of them going down). |

Its albedo is **512, not the 1024 every other figure gets**: 82.5 ground units is 154 pixels at the shipped camera, under `tools/meshy.py`'s `TEX_FULL_ABOVE_PX` of 260 — and six of them share the sheet. **CHECKS (5):** `figure · the goblin builds from the owner's own native rig, where a lump used to stand` · `figure · the goblin carries its clip set — three different attacks, because six of them swinging one animation is one goblin drawn six times` (7 of 7, out of 19 aboard) · `rig · the goblin has no flinch, and a missing clip degrades instead of freezing` · `rig · the same ground speed drives the SHORTER figure's cycle faster — the stride law, at half the captain's height` · `figure · six goblins on the deck are six skeletons and ONE mesh — the figure that arrives in numbers pays for its surface once`. Plus, shared with SG-88: `figure · and the sailor's sheet is twice the goblin's side, because the sailor is twice his height on screen`. **FILM:** NEW `swarm` scenario -> `.shots/clips/swarm.gif` (8 s — six goblins at 230 units a second closing on a mustered crew, three different swings, and two of them dying). **VERIFY (all three rows):** harness **771/771 from a clean worktree of main (6cf58e1, baseline re-proven 755/755) + this diff** — 16 new checks across the three rows. | SG-76 | DONE 2026-08-02 (hulk agent) — the owner's three files are wired, the mapping he stated was WRONG in two places and the models say so themselves, and the sealed state exists in a shipped run for the first time | **The boarding hulk is a mesh, three of them, and the state machine that was supposed to choose between them has been broken since the port.** THE MAPPING, VERIFIED BY LOOKING (the row's first job, because the names are ambiguous and two of the three are swapped): **"Ironbound Gate" (stated OPEN) is SEALED** — chevron plate shut over the door, one thin red seam, and an emission map that is **entirely black**, peak 0.000; **"Emberforge Core" (stated CLOSED) is OPEN** — the iris stands open with the furnace in it and it is the **only one of the three with anything alight in its emission map at all** (peak 0.431 against 0.000 and 0.004); **"Clockwork Gate Fortress" is DESTROYED** as stated — burst iris, torn plates, no fire. Three independent reads agree: the emission maps, the geometry rendered square-on at the game camera against the three paintings (`.shots/models/hulk-raw/*.png`), and the lab (`.shots/hulk-states/lab-*.png`). A furnace either burns or it does not. **MEASURED BEFORE WIRING** — tris / model units (w × h × d) / depth÷width / delivered MB → shipped MB: sealed **2,974** / 1.896 × 1.111 × 1.538 / **0.811** / 46.9 → 0.76 · open **2,950** / 1.897 × 1.034 × 1.105 / **0.582** / 48.6 → 0.82 · destroyed **3,010** / 1.870 × 0.928 × 1.109 / **0.593** / 47.1 → 0.74. **THE DEPTH RATIO IS VIOLATED A FOURTH TIME — and this time it is a NOTE, not a rejection** (SG-21's condition was the owner's own hand, and this is his hand): `handoff-3d/README.md` asked for depth ≤ 0.25 of the width and got 0.58–0.81. What it costs is written below and photographed; what it BUYS is the thing three prompted attempts never had — a hull with real mass that sits behind the bow rail instead of a card pasted over it. **BUDGET — nothing was decimated.** Raw Meshy downloads, but *already remeshed*: 2,950–3,010 triangles apiece, under the 4,000 prop law before anyone touched them, and the 785-px screen budget allows 8,000. 94% of every file was texture (4096 albedo + 4096 normal + 2048 MR + 2048 emissive), so the fix was the pipeline's own lossless-at-this-camera one: `tools/meshy.py shrink_glb` at `tex_budget({screen: 420})` — 1024 / 512 / 256 / 256, the same re-encode every generated prop on this deck went through. **142.6 MB → 2.3 MB, geometry byte-identical.** The SG-74 Boilerwright precedent (pin the delivered number, do not decimate somebody's mesh after the fact) did not even have to be invoked: his triangles were already inside the law. **THE STATE BUG, which is the real find.** `_begin_push` set `hulk.vulnerable = true` on the frame the hulk grappled and **nothing ever set it false** — so `SkyGearLanes.make_hulk`'s own `"vulnerable": false` was dead on the line it was written, SEALED was unreachable in a shipped run, and `boarding_hulk_sealed.png` has been on disk for the whole port without ever being on screen. Now: `make_hulk` arrives sealed, `_update_hulk` counts `HULK_GRAPPLE_TIME` (2.5 s) down and opens the door once — the ONE place that can — and `damage_hulk`'s existing `not vulnerable` guard turns that window into a real beat: the thing is bolting itself to your hull and there is nothing yet to shoot. Breaking it is `destroyed` as before. `SkyGearGame.hulk_state()` is the single answer (the `hulk_bar` pattern, SG-61): the renderer picks its mesh with it, the harness asserts on it, the hulk's own reinforcement trickle waits on it — a shut door sends nobody. **ONE RULER FOR THREE FACES (SG-79's rule, extended rather than bent).** The three states' spans honestly differ, so scaling each by its own would have swung the wall **430 → 529 → 550** ground units of width across one fight — a different object every time the door moved, which is the crate-v1 rejection reason at forty times the size. `_sync_prop_model` grew a `ruler_key`: the hulk's three scenes are all measured by the OPEN state, so **528 / 529 / 521 gu, a 1.4% spread against the 27.7% the per-state ruler would have produced**, and every other prop on the deck is SG-79 untouched. **JUDGED AT THE REAL CAMERA, both poses that killed the prompted attempts** (`tools/prop_shot.gd`, which grew a `hulk` state field and a `painted` flag that forces the billboard through the renderer's own `_no_prop_model` shelf — the before frame is the real fallback path, not a second one only the tool can reach): **mid-deck the mesh wins outright** — the billboard is a camera-facing card 420 units tall at the bow, and from mid-deck it is *invisible* behind the ship's own bow arch (`.shots/hulk-states/open-mid-before.png`), while the mesh shows hull, ramps and a lit iris over the rail (`open-mid-after.png`); **at the bow it is a genuine trade, and it is adopted anyway** — the card is brighter and 672 gu wide against the mesh's 529 (same apparent HEIGHT by the ruler; the painting is a 1.6:1 card and the model is 1.83:1), but the card floats over the bow structure while the mesh stands behind the rail with its ramps landing on the planking and the captain in front of the door (`open-bow-before.png` vs `open-bow-after.png`). **All three states adopted; no state loses.** **TWO NOTES, PHOTOGRAPHED, NOT FIXED** (they are the owner's models): (1) the SEALED state carries a **flat saturated-red slab across its roof** — off-palette, invisible from mid-deck, and at the bow it sits behind the objective plate (`.shots/hulk-states/inside-the-hulk.png`, top centre; plainest at `.shots/models/hulk-raw/boarding_hulk_sealed.png`); (2) the depth above is what lets the captain be **swallowed** when she walks into the hulk's footprint — filed as **SG-84**, with that same frame as its evidence. **CHECKS (5 new, 1 rewritten):** `hulk · sealed until the grapple lands, open while it disgorges, destroyed when it breaks — each state wears its own face` (the sequence, including a sealed hulk shrugging off both `damage_hulk` and `hulk_splash`) · `hulk · no wave, no hulk — the state is empty until one grapples on` · `hulk · and every state the simulation can be in has a face to wear` · `hulk · and the bar reads the same hulk in all three states — full sealed, halved open, gone wrecked` (SG-61 across the states it could not reach) · `hulk · three faces, one wall — one ruler holds the silhouette still while the door changes` (pins both the 1.4% and the 27.7%) · and `lanes · a push wave grapples a boarding hulk to the hull` rewritten from `vulnerable` to `hulk_state() == "sealed"`. The statics bargain — `prop · every wired deck prop scene keeps the statics' bargain` — now covers the three hulk rows unconditionally (**14 scenes**), which is the promise its own comment made ("the moment its scene exists"). **VERIFY:** harness **710/710 from a clean worktree of HEAD (8c45997, baseline re-proven 705/705) + this diff**. **EVIDENCE:** `.shots/hulk-states/{sealed,open,destroyed}-{bow,mid}-{before,after}.png` (twelve frames, same seed, same pose, mesh against billboard), `.shots/hulk-states/inside-the-hulk.png`, `.shots/models/hulk-raw/boarding_hulk_*.png`. Files: `assets/models/boarding_hulk_{sealed,open,destroyed}/` (glb + .import + .tscn + a provenance `meshy.json` that records the owner's file name, the stated state, why the mapping was overruled, and the spec it violates), `scripts/{game,view3d}.gd`, `tools/{static_model,prop_shot}.gd`, `tests/{parity_test,_shot_models}.gd`, `handoff-3d/README.md`, `docs/BOARD.md`, `/NEEDS_ALEX.md`. |
| SG-21 | CLOSED 2026-08-02 — **the owner did it himself**, and the condition this row set is the one that was met | Two prompted attempts were rejected when this row was written; SG-64 spent the owner's one reopened attempt and failed on a new axis (stone masonry, a Gothic arch, and the depth ratio ignored a third time), which made the hand-model verdict FINAL by his own condition. | SG-62 | DONE 2026-08-02 (fable/SG-62) — the fling is dead and the cap is law; the 58% share half is honestly recorded at SG-70, not silently retuned | **THE VECTOR, NAMED: the frame, not the cap.** `velocity += knock_velocity` in `enemy.gd` folded the July-31-capped shove into the WALK velocity — which the move-state lerp keeps ~90% of each frame — so the capped shove was integrated a SECOND time with a ~9× gain. The July-31 check asserted the carry ANALYTICALLY (v²/2a = 386 units) and never measured the enemy's own frame; measured through `_physics_process`, **ONE hit at the 900 cap on a moving boarder carried 1,338 units — mid-deck to the stern wall, straight past the Boiler at 850**. No multiplier stack required; SLEDGE FORCE + Frost builds simply pinned the cap oftener, which is why his build met it constantly. The suspects cleared: vent blast, kegs, sentries, the auto and every skill all funnel through `take_damage` and rode this same escape; KEEL HAULING moves enemies directly but is deliberate player-directed towing, named and left alone. THE FIX — the root, then belt-and-braces POSITIONAL law, because every earlier attempt capped a VELOCITY and was beaten by whatever integrated it: (1) the walk keeps only the walk — the shove is added for the move and taken back off before the lerp can bank it; (2) **`KNOCK_TRAVEL_MAX := 390`** — no shove carries farther from where it began, whatever stack produced it, anchor held across chained hits so a fast-ticking build cannot ratchet it forward; (3) **`KNOCK_STERN_GIVE := 60`** — the stern line: a shove never ends a frame more than 60 units Boiler-ward of its anchor. Stopped, never DELIVERED; sideways and bow-ward stay free, so the rail kill and hitting a boarding party back the way it came are untouched (both July-31 overboard checks green, unmodified). CHECKS: `knockback · the SG-62 fling is dead: one capped hit through the enemy's own frame carries a shove, not the deck` (60 stern-ward against the 60 line, deepest y 420 — the walk to its own lane gun — against 1138 before) · `knockback · no stack of multipliers beats the per-hit cap` (SLEDGE ×1.6 · ×1.4 every source · hits every 0.15 s: shove speed pinned at 900, ground pinned at 390) · `knockback · an enemy is never delivered stern-ward` (a 3-second bow-side barrage moved them 60 and no more) · plus SG-69's `knockback · a knock multiplier is applied once, not squared`. THE ALLY CAP (the row's second half): crew had NO ceiling — a muster is 3 lanes × (2 + Muster Roll) every 14 s, crew never expire, and the no-attrition arithmetic is 700+ by a run's end. **`SkyGearGame.ALLY_CAP := 32`**, counted over live crew + sentries by `allies_alive()`, refused at every spawn door: the muster, the press-gang (the cap test sits AFTER the seeded roll, so the rng stream is byte-identical whether or not the deck is full), and `deploy_sentry` (retires the oldest standing sentry to honour the cast; refuses cooldown-unspent on the one deck with none to retire). THE NUMBER'S REASONING: the lane doctrine's own "a dozen crew" (lanes.gd) + a full double muster arriving (9) + the fattest sentry loadout (8, already bounded by its own retire-oldest law) = 29, margin to 32 — it bites nothing intended and everything runaway. `allies · the alive cap holds under flood` (80 bells asked for 480 crew; 32 allies stand) · `allies · and a sentry never squeezes past the cap — refused full, honoured with room` · `allies · an ordinary muster still arrives whole` (6 from one bell). THE SHARE, measured per the contract (`tools/balance.gd -- 6`, live tempo): allies **25% before → 25% after**, per-seed and verdict detail on SG-70 — the bot cannot see the owner's 58%, so it is filed, not tuned. **JUDGED 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-70), on the repaired rig, per SG-125's "SG-62's fix can finally be judged on a rig that can see it". IT CANNOT SEE IT, AND THE RE-MEASUREMENT SETTLES WHICH HALF OF THIS ROW IS EVIDENCED AND WHICH IS NOT.** THE FLING HALF IS EVIDENCED AND UNAFFECTED: its four `knockback ·` checks assert travel and stern-give in units, through the enemy's own `_physics_process`, with no captain in the loop at all — they are bot-independent and they stand exactly as written. THE SHARE HALF IS NOT JUDGEABLE BY THIS RIG AND NOW DEMONSTRABLY SO: the repaired rig reads **13%** (n=30) where the owner read 58%, and stubbing the bot's movement out moves that by two points, so no before/after taken on a 13% baseline speaks to a 58% one. The contract this row signed — *"measure the 58% share before/after"* — **cannot be discharged by `tools/balance.gd` at all**, which is a stronger and more useful statement than the "25% before → 25% after" originally recorded: that pair was not evidence that the fling fix moved nothing, it was two readings of an instrument that was never pointed at the phenomenon. Detail and the movement isolation are at SG-70; the open question is the owner's, in NEEDS_ALEX §SG-70.
