| SG-29 | DONE 2026-08-02 (fable/SG-29+25) — precise verdict recorded, and the tools now refuse instead of stalling | **Headless capture is impossible in stock Godot 4.7 BY DESIGN, and the old "`get_image()` never returns" diagnosis was half wrong.** Measured with `tools/headless_probe.gd` (kept as scratch, every step printing a marker under an external timeout): under `--headless` there is NO rendering device at all — `RenderingServer.get_rendering_device()` is null, the adapter name is empty; the "vulkan / forward_plus" in the boot line is the requested settings echoed back, not a live driver. No viewport is ever drawn, so `root.get_texture().get_image()` does not hang — it returns null INSTANTLY (`ERROR: Parameter "t" is null`). What actually hangs is **`await RenderingServer.frame_post_draw`**: the headless main loop skips the draw step, the signal never fires, the await never resolves — that is the silent 60 s stall every capture tool hit, since they all await it before reading back. Workarounds tried, all dead ends: `force_draw()` DOES emit frame_pre/post_draw — synchronously, inside the call, so `force_draw()` then `await frame_post_draw` deadlocks by ordering alone, and `call_deferred("force_draw")` + await resolves the await fine — but paints nothing (no device to paint with); `--rendering-driver vulkan` is silently ignored under `--headless`; a SubViewport with UPDATE_ALWAYS reads back null the same; `--headless --write-movie` prints "Movie Maker mode enabled, recording movie in 1600×900" and then produces ZERO frames and a 0-byte .wav. CPU-side textures are unaffected (`texture_2d_create` + `texture_2d_get` round-trips a 4×4 fine) — render targets specifically have no data. Upstream: off-screen rendering is an OPEN proposal, godotengine/godot-proposals#5790; the Movie Maker docs confirm a window must exist. **Windowed stays the only capture path until upstream ships off-screen rendering.** WHAT SHIPPED so nobody loses 20 minutes again: `SkyGearRendererCheck.can_capture()` + `capture_refusal()` (the write-up lives in a doc comment there), wired into `tools/screen_shot.gd` and `tools/text_audit.gd` — both now exit(2) in under a second with "no GPU under --headless…" instead of stalling. Pinned where it matters, IN the headless harness itself: `capture · headless is detected, so a capture tool refuses instead of stalling` and `capture · and the refusal tells you the fix`. |
| SG-25 | DONE 2026-08-02 (fable/SG-29+25) for its three named halves — profile, reserve, D3D12; what remains of the audit is listed at the end, not glossed | **(1) THE BASELINE — the port's first profile ever.** `tools/profile_fight.gd` (new; windowed-only, refuses headless per SG-29): 40 boarders at wave 11, skills casting every 7 frames, bolts every 11, vsync OFF and max_fps 0 so the numbers are cost rather than monitor cadence, 600 frames measured after a 90-frame warmup. Machine context: **RTX 5080, Godot 4.7.1, Forward+/Vulkan, windowed 2560×1440.** Result: **frame p50 4.40 / p95 6.31 / p99 7.96 / worst 8.71 ms** against the 16.7 ms budget — twice the headroom even at the tail of a saturated fight, agreeing with SG-40's bench (p99 5.98 under 51 bolts). Buckets, bracketed by stamp nodes at process_priority ±1000 — NOT `Performance.TIME_PROCESS`, which updates once a second and whose snapshot frames read heavier than the frames around them (first run said script p50 7.33 ms INSIDE a 4.46 ms frame; discarded): script (the sim tick + the view3d mirror, siblings on one callback, not splittable without instrumenting game.gd) **p50 2.25 / p99 4.71**; rd-gpu **p50 1.64 / p99 1.92**; physics **p50 0.19 / p99 1.46**; rd-cpu **p50 0.37 / p99 0.75**. On the deck mid-measure: ~540–595 draws, ~590 k prims, ~700 MB vram, 48–65 live decals, ~35 rigs, 43 boarders / 13 effects / 38 bolts. **The top 3 costs, named and left alone:** 1. the `_process` script span (sim + 3D mirror) at ~half the frame's CPU; 2. the GPU draw at ~1.6 ms (~590 k prims across ~570 calls); 3. the physics tail (p50 0.19 spiking to p99 1.46 — separation under crowding). Recorded, NOT tuned — one profile is a baseline, not a diagnosis. **(2) RESERVED POOL CAPACITY — found already built, verified, and the stale "still open" claim corrected.** Commit 1144d44 gave decals per-class LIVE budgets (`DECAL_BUDGET` 48 telegraph / 24 player / 40 decor, the class derived from the key so a new effect cannot forget to declare itself and quietly spend a telegraph); the free NODE list is shared deliberately and harmlessly — a claim that misses it builds a fresh node, so recycling can never displace anything. The flood guard the audit asked for exists and is green: `budget · and a telegraph still gets drawn on a flooded deck` (200 decorative claims, then a telegraph, telegraph drawn), with `budget · decoration cannot spend past its own allowance` and `budget · a telegraph draws from its own reserve` beside it. DESIGN §13m still said "the budgets in the audit's table are the next step" — stale since 1144d44, corrected this pass with the check strings named. **(3) D3D12 — the first run there has ever been.** Windowed boot: "D3D12 12_0 - Forward+ - Using Device #0: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5080" — boots, renders, plays the same profile scene: frame p50 4.92 / p99 8.06 ms, rd-gpu p50 1.52, vram 796 MB. Verdict: **works; ~10% behind Vulkan at the median and +90 MB vram; no reason to switch, none to fear.** Harness under `--rendering-driver d3d12` (headless): 596/596 — proves the flag breaks nothing, exercises no GPU path (headless has none, see SG-29). **HONESTLY REMAINING from the audit, not this row's claim to have finished:** tonemapping and grading, shader-stutter prevention, and the validation requirements (§13m's last bullet — read, not worked through); and `fixed_fps`/visibility-AABB remain set-from-guidance with only this one baseline behind them — a comparison needs a second measurement. |

| SG-52 | DONE 2026-08-02 (fable/SG-48, found and fixed within minutes, filed per rule 3) | **Two agents, one git index: commit 524f37b briefly DELETED SG-47's five just-committed clip files and reverted its parity/hub/board hunks — the SG-50 shared-file hazard, escalated from files to the index itself.** Mechanics: both agents curate the SHARED index (`git add`, `git apply --cached`, `git update-index`) and `git commit` writes whatever tree the index holds, with no check that HEAD moved. fable/SG-48 staged a filtered tree based on d10f09c; fable/SG-47 committed bb138f8 in the same window; SG-48's commit then landed its stale tree on top, so bb138f8's additions read as deletions. Caught on the commit's own stat line ("delete mode ... clip.gd"), repaired in the next commit by restoring bb138f8's five files + union hub.gd, and the union parity_test.gd/BOARD.md from the working tree (which had always held both agents' edits). Nothing was lost — the deleted content sat safely in bb138f8 and on disk throughout. LESSON for the next concurrent pair: staging your own hunks is NOT enough; between `git diff --cached` verification and `git commit`, re-read `git log -1` and re-diff the index against the CURRENT head, or the commit replays your snapshot over whatever landed meanwhile. |

| SG-47 | DONE 2026-08-02 (fable/SG-47) | **Motion has an evidence path: `tools/clip.gd` poses a scenario, runs real seconds of sim+render, and stitches the frames into an animated file you can watch.** The pose is the screen_poser/vfx_shot idiom — driven sim, `log_runs` off (SG-49), auto-cues suppressed (SG-33) — and BOTH clocks are hand-stepped at 1/60, the sim AND the renderer, so a clip's duration is tick arithmetic rather than wall-clock luck, and a 6-second cutscene is 6 seconds of clip at any refresh rate. The sway stays ALIVE unless a scenario asks for stillness (`still`) — a motion clip of a dead-still ship is evidence about the wrong game. CLI: `-- <scenario> [--seconds N] [--fps N] [--out name]`, `-- list` to name them; BARE invocation is the SMOKE (one short dash clip end to end, stitched file nonzero, frame count asserted against the plan), and the tool sits in the hub as a windowed check like `text`, so `hub -- all` runs the smoke (windowed per SG-29). Scenarios shipped: `fight` (wave 3 mid-swarm off the real spawn queue), `dash` (SG-23's cloak crack through the real key), `projectiles` (SG-40's bolt heads in flight — a mortar's shell, a lance, the deck guns), and ONE PER SHIPPED CUTSCENE, derived from `SkyGearCutscene.list_ids()` so a scene authored tomorrow is a scenario the same day. The headless-testable half is `ClipMath` (tools/clip_math.gd, the LabMath split) + the Python stitcher's refusals, pinned by seven checks: `clip · a plan's frames follow from seconds and fps`, `clip · a degenerate ask still yields one whole frame`, `clip · a fractional ask rounds up to whole frames`, `clip · every named scenario resolves`, `clip · the scenario set covers every shipped cutscene`, `clip · a cutscene clip's default length films the whole scene plus the hand-back`, `clip · the stitcher refuses zero frames` (tools/clip_stitch.py exercised for real against an empty directory, exit 2, no file). Two capture traps met on the way: SG-46's ignored `--resolution` (met here too — every frame came back 2560×1440; the `deck_probe.gd` hunk now forces 1600×900 at the top of `_run`) and GIF per-frame dithered palettes (a 5 s fight was 55 MB; one shared mid-clip palette, dither off, cut it to 15 MB with no motion call lost). Harness 590/590 on HEAD+this (583 + the seven `clip ·` checks). Evidence, all nine produced end to end on this machine: `.shots/clips/fight.gif` (100 frames / 5.00 s), `dash.gif` (40 / 2.00), `projectiles.gif` (60 / 3.00), `run_open.gif` (65 / 3.25), `victory.gif` (123 / 6.15), `defeat.gif` (87 / 4.35), `colossus_arrival.gif` (95 / 4.75), `wave_start_blackout.gif` (42 / 2.10), `wave_start_grapple.gif` (43 / 2.15), plus one frame directory apiece beside each. |

| SG-50 | DONE 2026-08-02 (fable/SG-44, found and fixed in the SG-44 session) | **The "enemies" and "props" groups are tree-wide, and every simulation sweep iterated them raw — two games in one tree reached through each other.** Latent since the harness first built two games at once; made LIVE by SG-44's sandbox: `go_to_title()` on an arriving sandbox would have FREED the live run's boarders and props, `restow_props()` would have swept its deck, and the sandbox's auto-attack would have cut down boarders in a run the player gets back "exactly". Worse, two harness checks were quietly RIDING the bug: the Scald and auto-attack checks picked their victim as the LAST raw-group entry — frequently a leaked earlier game's boarder — and passed only because the attack swept tree-wide too. Orientation is gravity + yaw, position rides the chest joint (`_follow_cloak` per frame), so the idle clip's torso twist cannot turn the cape into a board glued to her spine. **THE SIMULATION** (`scripts/cloak.gd`, per-bone spring chain, damped ~0.75 of critical): velocity in her own frame trails it (measured 1.19 rad cumulative at a full 210 run); the dash CRACKS it — an angular impulse 7→18 rad/s down the chain on the dash's leading edge (measured peak 2.11 rad, harder than the run by construction and by check); idle sway rides the RENDERER'S own sway flag and `_flicker` clock at the ship's periods (0.31/0.73 roll, 0.58 heave), faded out by speed. **CONSTRAINTS:** forward budget totals −0.22 rad so it cannot cross her torso at the 41° camera (blown at −400 u/s it stops at +0.03 rad); back budget 2.0 rad (the crack tops out past horizontal); side ±0.16..0.30/bone; velocity killed at the wall so it never buzzes on the limit. **DETERMINISTIC REST:** sway off + still ⇒ the chain SNAPS to its rest constants at float32 bit-equality (rest stored in the state arrays' own precision — the 64-bit constants differ in the eighth decimal and that is fatal to a bitwise claim) — the framing-check rule honoured. **POOL LAW:** fixed 4 bones, state in packed arrays sized once, `drive()` allocates nothing — pinned across a thousand driven frames. **CLASS RULE:** `view3d.HERO_CLOAKS`, one row per class like HERO_MODELS; captain only (the Boilerwright's leathers read right bare; his opt-in is one row). Row absent ⇒ `wear()` never runs and nothing cape-shaped exists — the fallback pinned. **CHECKS (7):** `cloak · the captain wears a cape: a four-bone chain mounted at her shoulders` (4 on `mixamorig_Spine2`) · `injected velocity swings the chain, and rest brings it back exactly` (1.19 rad, bitwise rest) · `a dash cracks the cape harder than a run trails it` (2.11 vs 1.19) · `the forward swing is clamped so the cape never crosses her torso` (0.03 against −0.22) · `sway on the cape breathes; sway off it is bitwise still — the framing rule` (0.346 rad breath, rest held 60 frames) · `a thousand driven frames grow nothing: fixed bones, pooled state` (3 nodes before/after) · `without the class row nothing cape-shaped exists — the fallback is intact`. **VERIFY:** harness **583/583** (576 base at run time + these 7); `anim_timing` 7/7 BOTH classes, untouched. **EVIDENCE:** `.shots/cloak/{idle,run,dash}.png` (game framing — drape, trail, crack) and `{idle,run,dash}-close.png` (pulled-in detail), by new windowed `tools/cloak_shot.gd` (SG-29 honoured; the run scene holds wave 1 open with one far-future spawn so the draft cannot photobomb it — the SG-36 trap, met first-hand). | SG-50 | DONE 2026-08-02 (fable/SG-45) | **Shared-file hazard, realized: d079c9e committed `tests/parity_test.gd` from the live tree while SG-45's harness block was mid-edit in it**, sweeping an in-progress hunk whose `var lost` collided with the existing `var lost` in `_view()` — so HEAD's harness was a PARSE ERROR (0 checks runnable) from d079c9e until b6eedae renamed the variable and completed the block. | SG-26 | DONE 2026-08-01 (fable/SG-26) | **The last two Articles are built — the sigil side is all nine seals now, and both new ones are vows, not bonuses.** Design source honoured: `docs/META-PROGRESSION-DESIGN.md` §3's table named them and the departures header now records the build. **THE OPENING BID — 2 sigils — "name your weapons from the open matrix — no rerolls, ever."** Every weapon draft becomes the whole 32-cell matrix (8 draftable shapes × 4 elements, `DRAFT_SHAPES` shared with the weighted dealer so the two lists cannot drift), drawn as a grid the draft screen grows when options exceed four — click, or arrows + Enter (`bid_cells` is one static shared by the draw and the hit test, the `hud_plates` rule). The trade is IN THE RUN: `rerolls` forced to 0 at `begin_run` AFTER Deep Pockets' grant, `reroll_draft()` refuses, and SPARE PARTS leaves the catalogue rather than dealing a card worse than a skip. **THE SECOND HAND — 3 sigils — "a fifth hand that fights alone, dealt in place of your first card."** `skill_capacity()` (one reader, no scattered 4s) turns 5; the draft after the hand of four fills deals ONLY the shapes that fight alone (Field/Pulse — no fifth key exists, per the Articles' own keyed rule) IN PLACE of the run's first upgrade cards; telemetry sizes five buckets so the fifth's damage is attributed; the HUD raises a fifth keyless well (`slot4`, layout-checked at six sizes) whose empty state reads "the second hand". They exclude each other — the third exclusion pair. **BALANCE, measured** (`tools/balance.gd` grew a third arg: `-- 6 0 <article>`; 6 seeds, Heat 0): baseline 3/6 held, avg wave 11.3 · **opening_bid 6/6 held, wave 12.0** — the named-kit benefit is real and the tool's bot cannot price the reroll half (it never rerolls; the drawback is executed by the harness instead) · **second_hand 1/6 held, wave 10.8, passives 4.5% vs 3.2%** — the displaced first card measurably bites, the trade is real. **⚠ EVERY NUMBER IN THIS PARAGRAPH IS VOID (SG-125 audit closed 2026-08-03, opus/SG-70), for the same reason as SG-14's: it was measured on the stationary-bot rig SG-118 repaired, and hold-count is the reading that moved most (Heat 0 held 38% → 92% on identical game code).** Both conclusions are specifically at risk and in opposite directions. **"The named-kit benefit is real"** rested on 6/6 against a 3/6 baseline — with the baseline now near 92% both arms saturate against the ceiling and the separation can vanish without the Article changing at all. **"The trade is real"** rested on second_hand's 1/6 under a captain who could not dodge, where losing one early upgrade card is close to fatal; a captain who circle-strafes in the pressure band leans on that card far less, so the measured bite may shrink to nothing. The `passives 4.5% vs 3.2%` figure is a share-of-total and the total moved by roughly 7x when the captain started fighting, so it is not portable at all — and SG-126's own before/after found this rig cannot resolve a passive-share change of a few points between batches of IDENTICAL code. Note also that this row's Heat 0 baseline is **3/6 held** while SG-14's, same tool and same configuration, is **5/6** — n=6 was never enough. **WHAT STILL STANDS, and it is the substance of the row:** all 11 `shop ·` checks are structural — the 32-cell matrix (`tests/parity_test.gd:5443`), the bought reroll still refused (`:5446`), capacity and telemetry both 5 (`:5477`), the fifth shape dealt where the first card would be (`:5492`), the five-card hand (`:5497`), plus the reader guard, the classification guard and the sigil economy. None simulates a fight, so the Articles' MECHANICS are evidenced exactly as written; the row was also already honest that the reroll drawback is priced by a check and not by the bot. **Only the two "is real" balance conclusions are unevidenced.** Re-measure filed as SG-127. **⚠ RE-MEASURED 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-128), Heat 3 (held 75%, NOT pinned against the Heat 0 ceiling), n=120 per arm, pre-registered before any arm ran including the wording of a null. THE TWO CONCLUSIONS COME APART.** Baseline 90/120 = 75.0% held, wave 11.53, taken 219.5. **"THE NAMED-KIT BENEFIT IS REAL" — NOT ESTABLISHED, AND THE ARM IS CONFOUNDED (see SG-130).** opening_bid 104/120 = 86.7% held, **+11.7 points, z=2.30, raw p=0.022**; wave 11.81 (+2.5%, p=0.015); taken 201.1 (-8.4%, p=0.064). All three lean the same way and two clear raw significance — but over the six pre-registered tests (2 arms x 3 statistics) Bonferroni is 0.0083 and **neither survives it**. **The claim AS RECORDED is refuted regardless**: 6/6 against a 3/6 baseline implied a ~50-point benefit, and the measured effect is +11.7 points at best. **And it cannot be attributed to the Article at all**, because the two arms are played by DIFFERENT BOT POLICIES — `balance.gd`'s draft rule prefers a passive when the draft offers <=4 cards and takes cell 0 when it offers more, and the bid opens a 32-cell matrix. Passive share is **5.2% in baseline (114/120 runs) and 0.00% in the bid arm (0/120)**. The bid arm never drafts a passive; the baseline almost always does; passives are separately recorded as barely worth a slot. So a benefit here may be passive-avoidance rather than the vow. Filed as **SG-130**. **"THE TRADE IS REAL" — SURVIVES, BUT IN A DIFFERENT CURRENCY, AND THE RECORDED EVIDENCE IS REFUTED.** second_hand 95/120 = 79.2% held, **+4.2 points, p=0.44 — null**; wave 11.65, p=0.32 — null. The original 1/6-held reading implied a ~33-point hold cost and **there is no hold cost at all**. But **damage taken rises 219.5 -> 264.2, +20.4%, t=4.19, p=0.00003 — the one result of six that survives Bonferroni**, and it clears the 14.4% bar this n can resolve. So the displaced first card DOES bite, measurably and strongly; it bites in punishment absorbed rather than in runs lost, which is not what the row said. The conclusion stands on new evidence; the sentence that carried it does not. **NOTHING WAS TUNED AND NO ARTICLE WAS CHANGED.** The 11 `shop ·` structural checks are untouched and still carry the mechanics. **CHECKS (11 new):** `shop · the bid opens the whole matrix, not a deal` (32 cells) · `and the bid is final — a bought reroll is still refused` · `and the matrix shrinks by the shape you now hold` (28) · `and SPARE PARTS leaves the catalogue rather than dealing dead` · `the second hand raises the capacity and its telemetry together` (5/5) · `the fifth is dealt where the first card would be, and fights alone` · `and the hand is five` · `and without the article that same draft deals cards` (the control) · `two sigils: the bid affordable, the second hand not` · `and the signed bid bars the second hand at any purse` · `and the bar holds the other way round`. The reader guard (`shop · and every article is read by something`) covers both fields by iteration, and the classification guard now ENFORCES articles too (it listed them but only failed on nodes). Sigil economy check green: `16 to own all against 5 obtainable`. **VERIFY:** harness 559/559; text audit windowed (SG-29) CONTAINMENT clean, 0 faint · 0 small, across **23 screens** × 4 widths — two new poses (`draft (the bid)`, `playing (the second hand)`), and the poser now starts every screen from a clean ephemeral workshop so a vow signed for one pose cannot leak into the next. Found and fixed SG-46 in the same session (fourth card invisible), filed on its own row. Add this functionality as the primary purpose, and then the massive screenshot dump is a secondary." **WHAT THE EDITOR DOES NOW.** F4 anywhere: on the gameplay HUD it is the existing plate editor; on every OTHER screen (title, draft, pause, settings, workshop, results, class comparison, controls) it captures the screen's elements AS THE REAL DRAW CODE DRAWS THEM — every string through the `_say`/`_says` funnels, every widget through a new `ui.adjust` hook (offset applied before draw, declaration AND hit-test, so a moved button moves whole), plus an explicit `_element()` route for non-text marks (the draft card's emblem is the shipped exemplar). The screen you are ON is the screen you edit — no screen picker; the game navigates, and the help line says so. **Selection descends:** click a panel (draft cards, sheets via `_open_frame`/`_open_card`; the page itself is panel 0), click again or double-click for the element inside; Esc ascends. A selected element shows its bounds and its NAME — the key it saves under, made by `element_slug` from what it says, DIGITS EXCLUDED so "WAVE 7 / 12" keeps its key at wave 9 (`layout · a readout's number is not part of its key`). **Move it three ways:** drag; arrows (Shift ×10, Alt ×0.1 — the SG-39 steps, spelled out in the header); and the typed-offset box (click the readout, type "dx, dy", Enter applies, Esc cancels, malformed REFUSED — `SkyGearHudLayout.parse_offset`, the SG-39 `_make_value` pattern lifted, not the tools/ file). Ctrl+Z single-level undo (a swap — press again to redo), Ctrl+S save, Ctrl+R resets THIS screen only, F12 photographs the current screen chrome-free into `.shots/screens/` (the single-screen evidence shot; the batch page stays `SkyGear Tools.bat screens`). **THE SCHEMA (version 3, same file family):** `hud_layout.json` grows `"screens": {screen_id: {element_key: [dx, dy]}}` beside `plates`/`slot_items`; user:// wins over shipped, `layout` promote carries and re-sanitises it (`tools/layout_promote.gd`, verified: "SCREEN OFFSETS draft 1 · title 1", four widths hold). An offset is RELATIVE TO THE ELEMENT'S COMPUTED HOME, so a code-side reflow keeps the alignment (`editor · the offset is measured from the home — move the home and it follows`); the plate anchors' invariant holds one level down (`layout · re-anchoring an element leaves it where it was`); malformed entries fall back per-key (`layout · a malformed screen entry falls back alone`). **GUARD RAILS carried over:** snap guides against sibling elements (edges and centres), floor sizes on plates, and the VERDICT LINE is the text audit's OWN detectors attached live every edited frame — OVERFLOW/OUTSIDE from the `_say` funnel, plus `SkyGearHUD.overprints` and `SkyGearUI.collisions`, both EXTRACTED from `tools/text_audit.gd` and now called by the audit and the editor alike, so there is exactly one overlap test in the project (failure mode two closed, not risked). **GUARDS (15 new, 517→532):** the six `layout ·` strings above plus round-trip (`layout · screen offsets survive a save and a load`), typed parsing (`layout · a typed offset takes a pair and refuses everything else`), undo (`layout · undo restores the pre-nudge value`), and the live-draw `editor ·` pass — `the screen you are on is the screen you edit`, `the editor sees the title screen's elements` (14), `a widget's key is its label` (begin_run, settings), `a saved offset moves the element by exactly that much`, `every screen family is reachable and captures its elements` (all 9 families posed and captured), `the draft's cards are panels and the emblem is an element`, `the shipped layout file parses`, and `every key in the shipped file resolves to something drawn` — which went RED on its first run and caught SG-43. **VERIFY:** harness **532/532**; text audit windowed (SG-29) with ZERO saved offsets — CONTAINMENT clean, LEGIBILITY 0 faint · 0 small, 21 screens × 4 widths, 6794 strings (the shipped defaults untouched by the feature — the fast path skips everything when no offsets exist and no editor is open); windowed input-injection smoke — F4 on the title, Enter-descend, arrow nudge, Ctrl+Z restores the pre-nudge value, F12 saves `.shots/screens/edit-title-*.png`, Esc backs out, F4 on the fight HUD, zero script errors on stderr. Instead of these 2D sprites that look like they are cheap?" Every projectile head was `_spark`, a flat billboard of `_spark_texture()` turned to face the camera — the tell that read cheap: a disc that is the same from every angle is a sticker, not a thing in the air. **THE CORE (view3d.gd `_core`/`_make_core`):** one low-poly `SphereMesh` (8×5 segments), scaled in the LOCAL frame into a teardrop STRETCHED down its own velocity (basis columns written directly — `Basis.scaled()` multiplies rows and would land the long axis on the wrong column, the F-03 airstream bug), lit by its own emission (`emission_energy` over the 1.05 glow threshold so bloom catches it, NOT unshaded — unshaded ignores emission). Pooled exactly like the billboards — `_cores` in use / `_free_cores` shelf, hidden-and-reused, trimmed at POOL_SLACK. **PER-ELEMENT, IDENTITY IN THE MOTION not the hue** (`ELEMENT_BOLT`, the audit's finding 4 — the rule ELEMENT_FX/ELEMENT_RIBBON already carry): EMBER a fat throbbing ball (stretch 2.0, pulse 11 Hz) shedding rising flecks; FROST a long narrow shard (stretch 3.4, steady) shedding a tight downward wake; ARC a hard slug flickering violently (pulse 33 Hz); STEAM a soft round billow (stretch 1.4) rising; plus HOSTILE (blunt oxblood inbound, sheds nothing so a lane stays legible — SG-3's danger language) and CANNON (a tight brass slug), the two in-flight ordnance identities kept visibly apart. Low energy 2.1, radius 230u, no shadow, no fog contribution. Ground contact-shadow (`_shadow`) kept under every bolt. Old sprite stays as the art-missing/mesh-disabled FALLBACK via `USE_MESH_CORES` + `_bolt_head`. **BUDGET, MEASURED (`tests/bench.gd -- 90`, windowed, RTX 5080):** 51 live bolts at peak, cores capped at 24, lights capped at nearest 6 — **p99 5.98 ms against the 16.7 ms budget, PASS**, 0 spikes. **GUARDS (7 new, 510→517):** `core · the bolt head is a mesh by default, the painted sprite kept as fallback` · `core · every element's bolt differs on two motion axes, not only its colour` (stretch/pulse/wake each spread ≥3 distinct across the four) · `core · a bolt in flight draws an emissive core, not a spark billboard` (behavioural — fires one and looks) · `core · the core pool stays inside its cap under flood` (24/24 at 200 flooded) · `core · the bolt light pool is smaller than the bolt pool` (6 < 24) · `core · a flood of bolts lights only the nearest few, never all of them` (6 of 40) · `core · and the bolt lights add nothing to the fog and cast no shadow`. | SG-39 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-39) — awaiting owner re-judgement | **The yaw/roll collapse was mechanical and is fixed at the root; typed input is on every numeric row; and the friendliness pass landed — kept OPEN in the ledger for the owner's hands-on verdict, like the crate.** **MEASURED first:** at pitch −90 the stored Euler yaw and roll build the SAME basis to **1.6e-8** (`Basis.from_euler(−90,10,0)` vs `(−90,0,10)`, order YXZ), so the owner's "they do the same thing" is literally one control, not perception — the shipped cutlass fit sits at −96 on that singularity. **PRIORITY 1 — the gimbal collapse.** The mount's live rotation is now a **Basis**, not three Euler fields. Every rotation nudge — the PITCH/YAW/ROLL buttons, the A/D W/S Q/E keys, the wheel over a row, and right-drag — composes a turn about the weapon's OWN live local axis (`LabMath.rotate_local`: `basis.rotated(basis*axis, θ).orthonormalized()`), never an Euler increment, so the three rows are always distinct (measured **0.59** apart at the same pitch that collapsed to 1.6e-8). **File stays Euler** (`rotation:[x,y,z]` degrees, extracted at save via `Basis.get_euler(YXZ)` — the order `Node3D.rotation`/`hold()` applies, pinned by a check); a load that isn't rotated re-saves byte-identical numbers (the euler array is only re-extracted once you actually turn it). **FIT-COMPATIBILITY GUARANTEE:** the shipped fit loads to the same visible pose before and after — the check `lab · the shipped weapons.json fit loads to the same basis after the local-axis refactor` asserts `basis_drift(old, load→extract→reload) < 1e-4` on the real weapons.json rotation (measured **5e-7**). **PRIORITY 2 — typed input everywhere.** ONE reused `LineEdit` (`_make_value`/`_begin_edit`/`_commit_edit`) serves all rows — MOUNT's seven, the four LIGHT rows, the nine FX dials: click the value → type → ENTER applies (clamped to the row's range; rotation sets an absolute Euler component and rebuilds the basis), ESC or click-away cancels, malformed input refused and the old value kept (`LabMath.parse_number` via `is_valid_float`). **PRIORITY 3 — friendliness (all done):** hovering or nudging a rotation row lights the matching coloured local axis ON THE BLADE (weapon axes drawn in `_draw_overlay`, X red/pitch · Y green/yaw · Z blue/roll, active bright) so you SEE which line spins; Shift = step ×10, Alt = ×0.1, both shown in the header via `_step_hint`; wheel over a row nudges it (`_row_scroll`); the dev-note instruction block rewritten to a plain hint per mode; the "renderer has no size for this" fragment reworded to read as information; single-level **Ctrl+Z** undo of the last nudge/typed entry (`_snapshot`/`_undo`). Layout untouched — the owner LIKES the recent changes. **TESTABLE LOGIC pulled OUT of the windowed tool** (SG-29: the lab's readback hangs headless) into `tools/lab_math.gd` (`class_name LabMath`), which the harness loads and asserts directly. **CHECK STRINGS (7 new):** `lab · the fit Euler order is Node3D's own default, so hold() reproduces the basis` (YXZ==2), `lab · the shipped weapons.json fit loads to the same basis after the local-axis refactor` (drift 5e-7), `lab · at pitch -90 the Euler yaw and roll rows collapse to one pose — the reported bug` (1.6e-8), `lab · but a local-axis nudge about pitch, yaw and roll stays distinct at that singularity` (closest pair 0.59), `lab · a local-axis rotation nudge changes the pose`, `lab · the typed-input parser accepts a well-formed number and reads its value`, `lab · and refuses malformed input so the old value is kept`. **VERIFY:** harness **510/510** green (503 baseline + 7); the lab boots WINDOWED clean in MOUNT and FX with zero script errors/warnings (headless screenshot readback skipped per SG-29 — the interaction is described here for hand-verification). | SG-34 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-34) | **MEASURED FIRST, and the premise was refuted — then fixed the fault the measurement actually found.** The board said the Godot deck read dark, cool, vignetted, edges to near-black blue. A PIL probe over the six `.shots/parity/` stitches (HUD-masked deck region, browser LEFT = reference) found the OPPOSITE on every scene: Godot was BRIGHTER (deck luminance ~53 vs the browser's ~44), WARMER (mean R/B ~1.75 vs ~1.49), warmer in the surround, LESS cool (bluish-pixel fraction ~7% vs ~18%) and FLATTER (contrast 0.49 vs 0.66) than the browser — the browser is the darker, cooler, moodier image. The "blue-black crates" were measured warm at the faces (R/B 2.4–2.7) and read cool only against the pool — the sole genuinely-cool object is the `crate_stack` MODEL texture (stayed navy when the cool key was cut 1.45→1.15, so not the lighting — filed SG-41). **FIX in `scripts/view3d.gd` (environment/lights):** deliberate exposure `1.0 → 0.80` (deepen the whole frame to the browser's richer amber); calm the accent pools — boiler-glow omni `0.45+1.25·life → 0.38+0.95·life`, brazier/lantern omnis `1.5/1.0 → 1.2/0.82`, painted floor-pool alpha `0.34/0.24 → 0.26/0.18`, and the primitive-fallback furnace emission `2.6 → 2.0` (the live mesh emission is already 1.0); ambient re-set from cool-purple `#4a4058` to a near-neutral `#494551` (a FAINT cool fill kept for depth, not a strong cast) and fog `#3a2f4a → #3a3340`; the cool moon KEY kept at 1.45 — measured, cutting it only spiked the deck orange (R/B 2.3) and fixed no crate. **Mid-flight the owner retired parity** ("better than the web one ever was") — so the target was reframed as warm/even/legible with the depth a flat Canvas 2D never had, browser as reference not spec. **BEFORE → AFTER → BROWSER (deck-region, HUD-masked; deck / effects representative):** luminance 53→44 (browser 44) · warmth 1.77→1.88 (1.49) · surround-warmth 1.51→1.62 (1.34) · cool% 7→5.6 (17) · contrast 0.50→0.54 (0.66) · p99 163→150 (148) · hi% 0.75→0.35 (0.54). All four measured scenes converge the same way: deepened to browser luminance, hot pool calmed to browser p99, warm and even, a touch warmer/flatter than the browser by intent. Telegraphs/element flashes pop HARDER on the deepened deck (I darkened, not brightened — the task's "brighter deck washes a decal" risk inverted), element light-decay signatures untouched (`impact_at` not edited). **GUARD (1 new check, 502→503):** `view · the deck is lit warm and even, not a hot pool` reads the built `view._environment` + `_moon`/`_lantern` and asserts the shipped constants — Filmic tonemap, exposure in `[0.70,0.92]` (can't return to the washed 1.0), ambient a near-neutral fill (`b−r ≤ 0.05`, no strong cool cast) at energy `[0.45,0.80]`, the cool moon a key not a flood (`1.0 ≤ e ≤ 1.6`, still blue), a warm lantern fill floor present, and the boiler furnace emission `≤ 2.3` (not the old 2.6 sear). Constants not a frame readback (SG-29). **VERIFY:** harness **503/503**; all six stitches regenerated (`python tools/parity.py`), `.shots/parity/{deck,fight,effects,boss,hud,draft}.png`. The five sentences: **Heat 1 RUST** — boarders harden faster (wave-HP scaling 0.06→0.09, wave 12 ×1.66→×1.99); **Heat 2 SHORT FUSE** — and windups 15% quicker (×0.85); **Heat 3 COLD DECK** — and the draft deals two cards not three on one reroll not two (`draft_offers_for`/`start_rerolls_for`, read in `open_draft` and `begin_run`); **Heat 4 BOARDERS ALOFT** — and a hulk grapples on every second wave, 4/6/8/10 not only 4/8 (`pushes_on` via new `game.is_push_wave`, the one question the three push call sites now share); **Heat 5 SKELETON CREW** — and you hold it alone, no crew musters and the deck cannons start at half health (`musters`/`turret_hp_scale_for`, read in `_update_crew` and `begin_run`). **Gating (unchanged rule):** pick any rung up to one above your best clear, clamped on `begin_run`, never before the first victory. Heat 1–4 pay a sigil on first clear; **Heat 5 pays only the record** (§5's rule that the summit is not a power reward). **PART 2 — the ladder (`hud.gd _draw_heat_ladder`).** Five `bare` widgets replace the `ui.choice` row: cleared rungs lit brass, the next reachable rung teal, locked rungs dim with a padlock stating their unlock rule on hover/focus, the selected rung tabbed, and the selected/hot rung's one sentence beneath. STOKED is the ground (clicking the rung you are on steps back to 0); the header always names where you stand. Mouse-first, keyboard works (arrows/Tab/Enter through the same immediate-mode layer), rebind-safe (menu nav is not on the gameplay action map). No rung is `disabled` — so a locked rung still shows its rule on hover — the pick is gated by `is_reachable` instead (the Workshop board's own idiom). **GUARD (6 new checks, harness 481→487):** `heat · the stack is cumulative — no rung drops or softens a lower one` (walks every field of every rung through a `HEAT_HARDER` direction table), `heat · every Heat modifier is read by something` (the talent-guard twin: every HEAT field must appear in a `.get("…"` reader in `workshop.gd`), `heat · Cold Deck deals two cards on one reroll, not three on two` (3 cards/2 rerolls vs 2/1 on a real `begin_run`+`open_draft`), `heat · Boarders Aloft grapples a hulk on the odd pushes too` (`is_push_wave` 4/6/8/10 true at Heat 4, 6/10 false at Heat 0), `heat · Skeleton Crew halves the cannons and stops the muster` (cannon 380 vs 760, `musters(5)` false), `heat · clearing the summit records it but pays no sigil`. **BALANCE EVIDENCE (`tools/balance.gd` now takes a Heat arg, 6 seeds each):** Heat 0 held 5/6, average wave **11.7**, allies 34% of damage; Heat 3 held 0/6, average wave **10.2**; Heat 5 held **0/6, dead on wave 4 every run**, allies collapsed to 4% — a Heat 5 run is clearly, measurably harder than Heat 0, and the ladder is graded rather than a cliff. **⚠ EVERY NUMBER IN THIS PARAGRAPH IS VOID (SG-125 audit closed 2026-08-03, opus/SG-70). It was measured on the stationary-bot rig SG-118 repaired.** Held-count and wave-reached are the two most bot-dependent readings this rig produces — SG-118 measured Heat 0 held **38% → 92%** and damage taken **34.6 → 241** on identical game code — and "allies 34% → 4%" is doubly so, since a captain who closes to the 210-unit band does far more of the damage herself (SG-70 re-measured the Heat 0 ally share at **13%**, not 34%). The specific claim at risk is **"graded rather than a cliff"**: Heat 3's 0/6 was read under a captain who could not dodge, and with Heat 0 now holding ~92% the rungs may well have re-ordered. **A SECOND, INDEPENDENT REASON TO DISBELIEVE IT, and it needed no re-run to find: this row's Heat 0 baseline is 5/6 held and SG-26's Heat 0 baseline on the same rig and the same tool is 3/6.** Same configuration, same seeds, two different answers — n=6 was never enough against a rig whose own header now declares an **8% noise floor**. **WHAT STILL STANDS, and it is most of the row:** the six `heat ·` checks (`tests/parity_test.gd:5252, 5271, 5302, 5319, 5339, 5359`) read data tables and call `begin_run`/`open_draft` directly and never step a captain, so the cumulative stack, every modifier having a reader, the three named rung effects, the summit sigil rule and the ladder UI are all evidenced exactly as written. Monotonicity is guaranteed by the `HEAT_HARDER` direction table rather than by measurement, so the ordering survives too. **Only the difficulty CLAIM is unevidenced.** Re-measure filed as SG-127. **⚠ RE-MEASURED 2026-08-03 (opus/SG-128) ON THE REPAIRED RIG, AND "GRADED RATHER THAN A CLIFF" DOES NOT SURVIVE. THERE IS A CLIFF AND IT IS AT HEAT 5.** Heat 0 n=240: held **86.7%** (Wilson 81.8..90.4), wave 11.77. Heat 3 n=120: held **66.7%** (57.8..74.5), wave 11.34. Heat 4 n=120: held **77.0%** (65.1..85.8), wave 11.59, taken 222. Heat 5 n=120: held **0 of 120 = 0.0%** (0.0..3.1), wave **4.02, sd 0.18**, taken 15. Rungs 0-4 sit inside a twenty-point band; Heat 5 is not further along that curve, it is a fixed stopping point — an sd of 0.18 on wave-reached means the run does not vary. **SG-14's OTHER Heat 5 observation REPLICATES EXACTLY:** "dead on wave 4 every run" is what the repaired rig reports too, so that half of the original paragraph was right for the wrong rig and is right for this one as well. **HEATS 1 AND 2 WERE NOT ATTEMPTED** — machine time went to the SG-127 Article arms instead, and this curve therefore has two rungs missing and must not be read as complete. That does not rescue the claim: whatever Heats 1 and 2 do, they sit between two rungs that are already inside the band, and the cliff at Heat 5 is measured rather than interpolated. **NOT CLAIMED:** Heat 4 reading above Heat 3 would be a re-ordering, but the two Wilson intervals overlap and this sample cannot separate them. **BOT CAVEAT:** she never repairs and never retreats from a wave she is losing, so a human may clear Heat 5 where she cannot; what is established is that Heat 5 is a different KIND of rung, not a further step. **NOTHING TUNED** — whether the wall is intended is a design question for the owner and is in NEEDS_ALEX under §SG-127. **Text audit** windowed (SG-29): CONTAINMENT clean and ZERO COLLIDE at all four widths with the fullest ladder posed (3 cleared, 1 next, 1 locked, longest blurb selected — `tools/screens.gd` `title + heat`), LEGIBILITY `0 faint · 0 small`. **Harness 487/487** (481 at HEAD after opus/SG-15's Colossus-wreck commit landed first, plus my 6 heat checks). (2) **`wave_start_grapple.json`** (wave 4) — 2 keys / **1.40s**, a starboard snap toward the bow where the hulk grapples on, sweeping INTO the pinned gameplay camera, caption "THE PUSH". (3) **`wave_start_blackout.json`** (wave 8) — 2 keys / **1.35s**, the port twin as the dark descends, caption "BLACKOUT". (4) **`victory.json`** — 4 keys / **5.40s**, starts PINNED to the live camera then cranes up and back to eye y 2420 with fov opening 36→46 and the look rising to +1900 — the ONE angle gameplay never shows, where the horizon clears the frame and the cloud sea is visible (STATUS: at 41° the horizon is off the top at every zoom), caption "THE DECK HELD". (5) **`defeat.json`** — 4 keys / **3.60s**, a slow heavy `ease:"in"` push toward the dying Boiler at (0,850), fov tightening 34→29 with the ship listing (roll 1→2.4), ending on a 0.6s HELD pose (duplicate final key) because the results screen follows, caption "THE BOILER IS LOST". **wave_start DECISION — a milestone flourish, NOT every wave:** the payload discriminates by its `wave` field, so `wave_start` fires ONLY on the non-boss event waves 4 (grapple) and 8 (blackout). Rationale: (a) a slow shot seen every wave 60 times is hated — restraint is the design; (b) wave 1 is the establishing `run_open`; (c) **wave 12 is excluded on purpose — a `wave_start` shot there would still be active when the boss spawns and `boss_arrival` would return false, SUPPRESSING the Colossus arrival.** So each flourish is ≤1.5s and sweeps into the pinned gameplay camera. **`run_open` — the one code line outside data:** it has no natural call site because `begin_run` settles into the opening DRAFT, where a cutscene is stopped the instant the game is in a menu (`cutscene_player.advance`). So `begin_run` raises `run_opening` (one line, `game.gd`) and `view3d.gd::_watch_cues` spends it the first frame the deck reaches PLAY (before the `wave_start` branch, cleared so it fires once per run) — `run_open` added to `SkyGearCutscene.CUES` so the existing "every cue is actually called" guard proves the wiring. **SKIP already exists** (`cutscene_player._unhandled_input`: any click or space) so no skip-input row was filed. **Check strings (15 new, harness 463→478):** `cutscene · the run_open/victory/defeat cue carries a playable shot` (×3) + `· and the {id} shot stays inside its duration budget` (×3), `cutscene · wave_start fires on the event waves 4 and 8, never the boss wave or an ordinary one`, `cutscene · and a wave_start flourish is short — under a second and a half`, `cutscene · the {run_open,victory,defeat,wave_start_grapple,wave_start_blackout} shot plays and hands the gameplay camera back exactly` (×5, each 0.00000 gu / lens 36.0868), `cutscene · begin_run flags the run as owing an establishing shot`, `cutscene · and _watch_cues spends it on the first wave, opening the run once`. **Evidence-frame note:** no picture — there is no `tools/cutscene_shot.gd` and headless readback hangs here (SG-29), so per the task the keyframe/duration table above + the play-and-restore checks are the evidence; the missing tool is filed SG-32. **Harness 478/478** green. | SG-16 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-16) — DELETED | **Verdict: DELETE the field, not honour it.** `ELEMENT_FX[*].life` (EMBER 0.70, FROST 0.28, ARC 0.22, STEAM 0.95) was declared per element and NOTHING read it — the emitter's own `lifetime = 1.0` governed every family, so it was per-element timing that never rendered (failure mode one, found by the lab). **Why delete, not honour:** honouring a per-PARTICLE lifetime needs one emitter per element, and rendering-audit finding 3 (DESIGN §13m) is emphatic that emitters are keyed by BEHAVIOUR (spark/shard/steam), colour riding on the particle, because a shared `emit_particle` emitter that is never restarted is the fix to a real bug — per-element emitters would regress that, break the pinned `impact · one emitter per behaviour, not one per element` check, and add a 512-cap node against §13m's still-open pool budgets. It could not even be honoured per FAMILY: EMBER (0.70) and ARC (0.22) share the `spark` emitter with different lifetimes, so the field was self-contradictory as per-element data on a per-behaviour system — proof it was never wireable. **The §13m finding-4 signature survives intact WITHOUT it:** the timing half is the LIGHT decay (26/s FROST·ARC vs 8/s EMBER·STEAM, hardcoded in `impact_at`, tested green — `impact · and their light dies at different rates` frost 26/s ember 8/s), the motion half is rise/spread/speed (tested — `impact · elements differ in motion, not only in colour`, which used to lean on `.life` and now leans on `frost.speed > ember.speed`). Reasoning recorded at the deletion site (the `ELEMENT_FX` const comment). **GUARD (1 new check, the twin of the talent-field/article guards):** `impact · every ELEMENT_FX field is read by the renderer` greps `view3d.gd` for every key on every entry — the table cannot grow another dead field. **Harness 462→463.** Files: `scripts/view3d.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd` (own hunks). Commit: see git log for SG-16. |
| SG-10 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-10) | **The second deckwork verb shipped: HEAVE THE CRATE.** One pass, one verb, complete — sim, discoverability, rules, checks — per the repair pattern. **What it is:** a movable cargo stack (`crates` prop, so it is mirrored into 3D with NO renderer change) the captain heaves across the PORT lane to funnel the boarders walking down it. Held on the same `deckwork` key as repair (`SkyGearKeybinds`), context-sensitive: stand at the crate and the generic HUD prompt reads `HOLD R · HEAVE THE CRATE` with the live binding, a progress bar under it, and `— BOARDERS ARE ON IT —` when contested — all reused from repair, which is the whole point of the table. **Rules as shipped:** (1) COST is TIME and only time — `HEAVE_SECONDS 2.8` out of the fight per heave (heavier than repair's 2.4; a loaded stack is heavy), the sharpest cost the deck has; no new currency, honouring deckwork.gd's doctrine. (2) BALANCE is GEOMETRY — one 150-wide crate against a 380-wide lane band NARROWS a lane and pins the fight to one side, but can never wall it shut for free; the outboard side always stays open (harness-proven). A heave cycles stow→narrow→funnel→stow, so the same key also RETRACTS it. (3) PERSISTENCE: the crate RE-STOWS to its bow home every wave with the rest of the ordnance (the pinned deck re-stow), BY DESIGN — a flank you closed is one you pay seconds to close again next wave, never a permanent free wall, and legible because the crate visibly returns home each wave. The pinned `deck · the deck is re-stowed for the next wave` check stays green (31 standing), NOT silenced. (4) PATHING responds for real: `_funnel_past_crate` ejects a boarder from the crate's footprint to the nearer OPEN side (never toward the stern) inside `correct_enemy_position` — the three lines boarders actually path by — so the funnel is true, not painted (STATUS failure mode one). **ONE source of truth for cargo:** new `game.cargo_rects()` (the 8 fixed walls + the live crate) now feeds `correct_player_position`, the funnel and the 2D debug draw; the crate footprint is derived from the prop the player SEES, so what blocks and what is drawn are one object (STATUS failure mode two). **Coach:** new `shape_lane` hint announces the otherwise-invisible verb while a lane is walking through and the crate sits stowed, carrying the live binding via the same `{key}` substitution as `broken_cannon`. **Check strings (11 new, harness 446→457):** `deck · the deck carries a movable crate to heave`, `deck · the crate joins the one cargo source of truth`, `deck · standing at the crate offers a heave`, `deck · the heave verb has a prompt and a reason it refuses`, `deck · heaving actually moves the crate's footprint`, `deck · a boarder in the port lane is funnelled by the heaved crate`, `deck · and the open side of the funnelled lane still passes`, `deck · and stowing the crate re-opens that column`, `deck · the crate re-stows to its home for the next wave`, `coach · the crate's heave is announced with a lane walking through`, `coach · and the heave line names the bound key, not the {key} token` (asserts the live binding `R`, not the raw `{key}`). **Verify:** harness **457/457** green; text audit windowed CONTAINMENT clean, LEGIBILITY `0 faint · 0 small`. | SG-27 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-27) | **MEASURED, then rescaled — verdict (a), keep the mesh at the browser's height; verdict (b) rejected because the "flat fallback" it names is a myth.** `tools/boiler_measure.gd` (new) poses `main3d.tscn` at the deck framing and measures the ACTUAL rendered Boiler subtree, unprojected through the real camera. **Before:** the generated mesh, scaled to `BOILER_HEIGHT=168` (a taste number, "buys the pipes"), rendered a near-cube — **168 tall × 167 wide × 162 DEEP** ground units, **208.6 px** tall / **290.8 px** wide at 1920×1080 — a solid drum dominating the lower third against the browser's FLAT **150-unit / 183.6 px** block. That is the boarding-hulk failure mode (a mesh as deep as it is wide) and it, not the camera (SG-2), was the "everything looks bigger" residual. **The `boilerH:132` in DESIGN §13c / this board's old title is STALE** — that was the v3/v4 preset; the LIVE build `parity.py` photographs is **storm-dusk-v11, `boilerH:150`**, so 150 is the real target. **Decision:** the primitive fallback that verdict (b) would fall back to is NOT the flat engine block §13c describes — measured it renders 178×**192×192** gu / 222.9 px, a smooth golden ball, CHUNKIER than the mesh (filed SG-30). The mesh's furnace face is authored to +Z, the one direction this locked 41° camera ever sees, so — unlike the hulk's hidden ramp — it reads as the furnace square-on. So: kept the mesh, set `BOILER_HEIGHT 168 → 150` (`scripts/view3d.gd`), pinning the render to the browser's own `boilerH`. **After:** rendered Boiler **150.0 tall × 148.8 × 144.6** gu, **183.6 px** tall = the browser's 183.6 px exactly (ratio **1.000**), width 290.8→259.7 px; the captain (spawns 130u in front, crown 128u) stands clearly clear of it at run start — §13c regression test held, confirmed in `.shots/parity/{deck,fight}.png`. **GUARD (check string):** `boiler · renders at the browser's boilerH, not a taller mesh's own height` (reads the live subtree AABB = 150.0 gu, asserts it and `BOILER_HEIGHT` both equal browser boilerH 150 ±3) — a generated mesh cannot silently regress this again. **Harness 446/446** (baseline 445 + 1). It was the DOWNSCALE.** `ink.gd` chose `MIN_PT=12` to "survive the 0.83 downscale of a 1600 window" and explicitly nothing narrower, but nothing STOPPED the window going narrower — and the HUD is a full-rect Control in a CanvasLayer under `canvas_items`, so the 1920 layout never reflows, it just scales to the window. **Measured before touching anything** (new `tools/legibility_probe.gd`, real 1920 layout scaled per width): at a 1280 window **340 of 627 HUD strings render at 8.0 physical px** — every 12pt label, the exact sub-pixel-stem smudge `MIN_PT` exists to prevent — across the owner's named surfaces: skill/binding labels (`V BLOW`,`F MAIN`,`SPC JET`), lane labels (`PORT`/`CENTRE`/`STARBOARD`), boiler/wave readouts, card titles, draft before/after values, workshop labels. **Contrast was already clean and stays clean** (full text audit, windowed): `0 faint · 0 small`, bare-plate ratios as low as 2.52 on textured brass but the 2px outline lifts effective contrast to 8.8–17 — **no tint changed**. **Fix (minimum intervention, zero layout change):** enforce `get_window().min_size = 1600×900` in `game.gd._ready` (formalises the project's own default windowed size), so the 0.83× downscale MIN_PT was calibrated for is the worst case; single source of truth added to `ink.gd` — `BASE_W=1920`, `MIN_WINDOW_W=1600`, `MIN_PHYS_PX=10.0`, `physical_pt()`. **After:** probe reports **0 of 627 strings under 10px** at the enforced min window (worst is exactly 10.0px); `tools/text_audit.gd` SMALL now means "under `MIN_PHYS_PX` physical px at the min window" (was a dead `pt<MIN_PT` test the funnel could never trip) with a new `PHYS` column, plus `tools/legibility_probe.gd`. **Verify:** harness **445/445** green (2 new ink checks, 0 failures, nothing silenced); | SG-1 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-1) | Check `class · but ground covered against a dashing captain is worse than that` was nondeterministic, not machine-specific: it read `global_position` after `move_and_slide()`, which steps by the engine's real-frame physics delta (`get_physics_process_delta_time()`), not the loop's 0.05 sim tick — so it measured wall-clock jitter (200–570% run to run; 33% and 180% were two draws), compounded by the wave auto-completing and flipping state to DRAFT, which disabled `controls_enabled` mid-measurement. Fix: integrate `\\|velocity\\|* 0.05` (the speed the sim deterministically produces) and force `controls_enabled` on each tick. Now bit-for-bit repeatable — captain 3076, boilerwright 1228 every run — reading **40%** (was flapping). Harness 437/437, three consecutive green runs. Corrected the ledger's 67%/33% mobility gap to 60%/40% (`OUTSTANDING.md`). Fix is this commit (see `git log` for SG-1). |

The port's `reach`/`swing` were never ported — it faked reach with a flat `attack_range + 24..28` fudge and drew a thin streak. **Fixed:** ported `reach`/`swing` into `game_data.gd` (the browser's tuned values), made the sim connect at `reach + target_radius` (`enemy.gd`) so what is DRAWN and what CONNECTS are ONE number (STATUS failure mode two), rebuilt the telegraph in `view3d.gd` as a filled danger wedge (`_fan_texture`) with a growing inner clock, and gave the GUNNER a bright firing band with a counting-down hot core. Evidence: harness `445/445` green; new checks `telegraph · a windup appends a telegraph decal to the deck`, `telegraph · the wedge is drawn at the reach the swing connects at` (drew 92 vs reach 92), `telegraph · every melee windup has a reach and a swing arc to draw`, `telegraph · a ranged shooter is not handed a melee swing`, `telegraph · the windup gives at least the browser's warning time`, `telegraph · the windup pool stays inside its reserve under flood` (48/48); `lanes · a boarder in every lane can reach the Boiler` still green on the new reach. Posed before/after in `.shots/telegraph/{before,after}/telegraphs.png` (`tools/telegraph_shot.gd`). Ranged dashed-aim-line polish filed as SG-28. Commit: see git log for SG-3. |
| SG-2 | CORRECTED 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-2) | **The camera was ported exactly; there is no framing gap. Measured, not asserted** (`tools/cam_measure.gd`): at one output resolution the browser's own `CAM.project()` and Godot's `Camera3D.unproject_position` agree to the pixel on every known length — deck full width 1680 = **3288.1 px in BOTH** (ratio 1.000); at the bow 1137.5 both; one lane 560 = 1096.0 both; vertical bow→stern 1695.7 both; the captain's ground point lands at the identical pixel (960, 705.4). Root cause of the false "zoomed in" finding: the browser's `_f = f * View.unit` (`_render_head.js:53`) scales focal length with resolution exactly as Godot's fixed vertical FOV does, so at any 16:9 aspect the two projections are identical — no `WORLD_SCALE`, deck-rect, distance or FOV gap exists. The original impression was made against a broken browser render (procedural sky fallback, images blocked over `file://`, as `parity.py`'s own comment documents) and/or unmatched resolution. Fresh evidence with the real art in `.shots/parity/{deck,fight,effects,boss}.png`; the one real residual (the Boiler PROP mesh, not the camera) filed as SG-27. Harness 437/437 — camera checks `the lens is the browser's focal length` (36.09°) and `the captain stands where the art was framed for` (0.600) both green, asserting the confirmed-correct invariant. |
